During this festival, it was customary for Paravaiyar to give jewels, clothes, and other necessities to the people who came to the festival, and to serve food and other things to the devotees without any shortage. It was also customary for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to ask the Lord for gold for such expenses. We will see this in the later history of Thirumuthukundram and Thiruppachilachiramam, where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal received gold by asking. Here, the Panguni Uthiram festival is approaching. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asks the Lord for gold to provide for the devotees and pilgrims who come to the festival. Those who do not deeply understand all this speak carelessly, saying that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asked the Lord for gold for Paravaiyar, and the Lord gave it. They speak thus out of ignorance. There are also poets who have sung, "Sundaran will sing for gold," and so on. Therefore, setting aside these words, this world should realize the truth and be saved from offending great people. Charity means giving gold and other things. This is done for everyone who comes to the festival. "Kurai Varuththal" means serving food to devotees, arranging mansions for them to stay, and many other types of hospitality. All these regulations for performing during festivals are great Shiva Punya (meritorious deeds) as stated in the Shivagamas (Saiva scriptures). We have contemplated these in the Puranams of Amarneethi Nayanar and others. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sets out to Thiruppugalur, which is near Thiruvarur, to worship the Lord's feet and ask for gold for Paravai Nachiyar to perform these Shiva Punya. If the question arises as to why Sundaramoorthy Swamigal had to go to Thiruppugalur when he could have asked for gold from his Lord in Thiruvarur itself, the answer is no. What is stated in the Shivagamas? What is stated in the Shiva Puja rules?

It is stated in the Shivagamas that one should not ask for any material desires like gold from one's Atmarttha Nayaka (the Lord worshipped for one's soul's liberation). The Shivagamas prescribe that all puja benefits, karmic benefits, and so on, should be offered to the Lord in His sacred seat. Therefore, Nambiyarurar, who was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's Atmarttha Nayaka, the Lord who resides in the anthill in Thiruvarur, did not ask for gold or other things from Him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred history states that he asked for and received gold only in Thiruppugalur, Thiruppachilachiramam, and Thirumuthukundram, and used it to provide for the devotees during the Thiruvarur festival. We should also remember here that Paravaiyar spent all the items given by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for the devotees of Shiva in Thiruvarur, just as she had previously announced that everyone could take the paddy from Kundaiyur within the boundaries of their respective houses in Thiruvarur. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Thiruppugalur. The 47th verse is as follows: "He went to Thiruppugalur, worshipped at the beautiful temple courtyard of the Lord of gods, circumambulated, prostrated before the Lord, and then, with love born of ancient tradition, he praised Him, stood up, and sang a Thiruppathigam, expressing his intention." This verse is composed. He went to Thiruppugalur, worshipped at the beautiful temple courtyard of the Lord of gods, circumambulated, prostrated before the Lord, and then, with love born of ancient tradition, he praised Him, stood up, and sang a Thiruppathigam, expressing his intention. We have previously contemplated the special features of Thiruppugalur in the Puranams of Muruga Nayanar and Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar. Sekkizhar Peruman sings all these details in this single verse. Sekkizhar Peruman sings the news of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal leaving Thiruvarur, worshipping in Thiruppugalur, and singing a Thiruppathigam, all in a single verse, to show how quickly he did it. Unfortunately, due to our lack of merit, the Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal at this time is not available to us. Now, the 48th verse: "He worshipped for a short while, leaving his mind there, and then came out and did not go to any nearby monastery. The steadfast Vanthondar, with his discerning love, sat on one side of the beautiful courtyard. Was it the grace of the Lord with the deer in his hand, or was it sleep that came to his lotus-like eyes?" This verse is composed. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stood worshipping before the Lord for a short while. Then, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind remained there before the Lord, realizing that he had not received the gold he had intended to ask for. So, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal came out of the temple. He did not go to any nearby monastery; he sat on one side of the temple courtyard. Generally, it was not customary or permissible for great people to stay or sleep in the temple or its courtyard at times other than worship hours, because doing so could lead to physical discomfort and many other improprieties. Therefore, great people would not stay there. So, it is a rule that one should not stay or sleep in the temple courtyard. But here, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is staying in the temple courtyard, contrary to the rule. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is well-versed in worship methods and rules, and he excels in conduct. Even so, due to the Lord's grace, sleep came to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's lotus-like eyes. The 49th verse: "As sleep came, Thambiran Thozhar brought many fired clay planks for the temple work, piled them up, and spread his white silk upper garment over them, and lay down to sleep." This verse is composed. As sleep came to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's eyes, Nambiyarurar, Thambiran Thozhar, brought many fired clay planks, which were used for the temple work, piled them up, arranged them like a pillow, spread his white silk upper garment over them, and lay down to sleep. In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal as "Thambiran Thozhar" (the Lord's friend). This implies that he was entitled to do such an act. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to the bricks, which were kept for temple work, as "Sudumman Palagai" (fired clay planks) in the verse. It is a historical fact that most of the temples in the Chola country in those days were built with bricks. We can still see in old temples that the bricks were very well made, like ingots of pure gold. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to them as "Palagai Sudumman Palagai" (fired clay planks). Just as wooden planks are planed and smoothed, here, the bricks were also fired and were so beautiful and strong. It is a rule of physical comfort that one should rest one's head on a high pillow. Therefore, since Sekkizhar Peruman mentions "Sudumman Palagai Pal" (many fired clay planks), we can assume that he took about two, three, or four bricks and arranged them like a pillow. He then spread his white silk upper garment, which he wore, over the bricks, which were like a pillow. The bricks were hard, so to make them soft enough to rest his head on, he spread his white silk upper garment over them. The upper garment was made of white silk. We have seen that the Lord of Thiruvarur had previously told Nambi that he should always be in wedding attire. Accordingly, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was always in wedding attire, wearing white silk. In addition, he would arrange his beautiful hair into a topknot and adorn it with a garland of flowers where bees would hum. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal lay down to sleep, making the bricks a pillow for his sacred head, which was adorned with such a garland of flowers where bees would hum, and spreading his white silk upper garment over them. Now, the 50th and 51st verses are as follows: "As the surrounding devotees also slept, the husband of Paravaiyar, whose lotus-like eyes were free from sleep, awoke. By the grace of the Lord with the victorious bull, the fired clay bricks became solid gold, shining brightly. He praised the grace of the Lord of Pugazhur, rejoiced, and rose. With his lotus-bud hands folded over his head, he entered the temple, worshipped the Lord with boundless love and overflowing devotion, and sang a sweet Tamil garland of verses, beginning with 'Praising himself,' set to ancient music."

As the surrounding devotees also slept, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the husband of Paravaiyar, whose two sacred eyes were free from sleep, awoke. He rose, worshipping the Lord. At that time, by the grace of Lord Shiva, the fired clay bricks, which were placed as a pillow, became solid gold, shining brightly. Seeing this, he praised the Lord. He glorified the Lord's grace. The bricks that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal had placed as a pillow had turned into solid gold ingots. Among metals, gold is dense and heavy. In those days, it was customary to cast and store gold ingots in the shape of bricks. The bricks had turned into such gold ingots. The devotees who were sleeping with him also awoke. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal rose with joy, folded his two hands like lotus buds over his head, entered the temple, worshipped the Lord with boundless love and overflowing devotion that could not be contained by his intellect, and sang a sweet Tamil garland of verses, beginning with "Praising himself," set to ancient music. The Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 34th Thiruppathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, for Thiruppugalur. It is set to the tune of Kolli. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "O poets, do not sing of the deceitful ones who praise themselves and speak as they please. Sing of my father, the Lord of Pugazhur. In this world, He will give you food and clothing, and your troubles will cease. There is no doubt that you will attain Shiva Loka in the next world." "The Lord of Pugazhur, who resides in the flourishing lotus-filled south, the Lord of Thirunavalur, the son of Sadaiyan, the father of Vanappakai, Vanthondar, who sang these ten verses, those who are skilled in them will enter the supreme world without any doubt." Thiruchitrambalam. This Thiruppathigam was sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to guide the people of this world, praising the Lord for having turned the bricks into gold for him, saying, "O poets, do not sing of the deceitful ones in this world. Sing of my father, the Lord of Pugazhur. In this world, He will give you food and clothing, and your troubles will cease. There is no doubt that you will attain Shiva Loka in the next world." Sekkizhar Peruman composes the 52nd verse: "Having sung the Thiruppathigam, adorned the Thirukkadaikkappu (concluding verse), and praised Him, he came out. Having received the grace of the Lord who gives incomparable joy in this very life, he rose, took the heaps of wealth, and entered Thiruppanaiyur, where the Lord with the moon and the river in his matted hair resides, the Lord with the sacred thread on his chest." This verse is composed. Having sung the Thiruppathigam, adorned the Thirukkadaikkappu, and praised Him, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal came out. Having received the grace of the Lord of Thiruppugalur, who gives incomparable joy in this very life, he rose, took the heaps of gold ingots he had received in Thiruppugalur, and went to Thiruppanaiyur. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 09 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman, by divine grace, continues to sing the Periya Puranam, Part 847, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Thiruppanaiyur and worships. Thiruppanaiyur is a temple located on the way from Thiruppugalur to Thiruvarur. As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal approaches Thiruppanaiyur, just outside the village, at the boundary of Thiruppanaiyur, Lord Shiva grants him a divine vision of His sacred dance. The 53rd verse: "The Lord with the red matted hair granted a vision of His sacred dance at the outskirts of Thiruppanaiyur. With overflowing desire, he prostrated himself there and sang a Thiruppathigam, saying, 'The Lord who is skilled in dancing is indeed beautiful,' for the world to be saved, and then departed with grace." Lord Shiva, who has red matted hair, granted Sundaramoorthy Swamigal a vision of His sacred dance at the boundary of Thiruppanaiyur. At the very spot where the vision was granted, he went forward, prostrated himself on the ground with overflowing desire, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang a Thiruppathigam with the meaning, "The Lord who is skilled in dancing on the stage is indeed beautiful," for the world to attain salvation and live. The Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruppanaiyur is the 87th Thiruppathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the tune of Seegamarappann. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "The Lord who is skilled in dancing is indeed beautiful, in Thiruppanaiyur, where mansions, towers, and mandapams (halls) flourish, and where humming bees sing in the groves, and where a ear-stud and a dangling ear-ring are worn in one ear, and devotees dance with joy." "The Lord who is skilled in dancing is indeed beautiful, the one whose half is the slender-waisted Goddess, the great ascetic, in Thiruppanaiyur, where groves flourish and where women with cotton-soft feet reside. The Lord of Thirunavalur, Vanappakai's father, Vanthondar, whose sweet words please Him, is indeed beautiful." Thiruchitrambalam. Thiruppanaiyur is the 73rd temple on the south bank of the Cauvery in the Chola country. This temple can be reached by going past Thiruvarur Sannanallur and taking a branch road to Panaiyur. The Lord is Soundareswarar, Azhagiyanathar, Thalavaneswarar. The Goddess is Piriruhanayagi, Periyanayagi. The sacred waters are Parachara Theertham, Amirtha Pushkarani, Thirumagal Theertham. The Sanskrit name for this village is Thalavanam, which is called Panaiyur in Tamil. Upon entering the temple, one sees Thunaithirundha Vinayagar (Ganesha who helped). This Ganesha helped King Karikala Chola. Karikalan was born after losing his father. His relatives intended to kill the child and seize the kingdom. Irumbidar Thalaiyar, a Sangam poet and Karikalan's maternal uncle, who tried to protect him, secretly sent the child Karikalan and his mother to this Panaiyur. The queen came to this village with her son, took refuge in this temple, appealed to this Vinayagar, and was protected for eight years with his help. Therefore, this Vinayagar was named Thunaithirundha Vinayagar (Ganesha who helped) because he helped King Karikala Chola. The pond there is named Santhitha Theertham, commemorating the place where the Lord granted Sundaramoorthy Swamigal a vision of His sacred dance at the boundary. It is a small village with many palm trees and sandy soil. It is an ancient temple. There are two palm trees as sacred trees. These trees have been there for a long time. When these mature trees reach their old age, they do not sprout from seeds like banana trees; instead, other palm saplings spontaneously appear and grow at the base of these two palm trees. In inscriptions, the Lord is referred to as Panaiyadiyappan, the Lord of Panangattu. There are seven Shiva Lingams worshipped by the Saptharishis (seven sages). The Shiva Lingam worshipped by Parachara Muni is Thalavaneswarar, the Moolavar (presiding deity), facing west, with a square Aavudaiyar (pedestal). The Somaskandar idol in this temple has a unique posture, holding a fruit in its left hand. These are the details about Thiruppanaiyur. Now, the 54th and 55th verses: "He went to Thiruppanaiyur, which is rich in glory, worshipped by its inhabitants. The servants carried the countless gold ingots to Paravaiyar's mansion in Thiruvarur, which is adorned with jasmine-like smiles. He also worshipped his Lord, who was enshrined in his heart, and then went to Paravaiyar's mansion. The Lord of Munaippadi, the protector, stayed there happily, worshipping the Lord of Thiruvarur first." These two verses are composed. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Thiruppanaiyur, which is rich in glory and worshipped by its inhabitants, and then proceeds to Thiruvarur. At that time, the servants carried all the gold ingots, which were like bricks, to Paravaiyar's mansion. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also worshipped his Lord, who was enshrined in his heart, and then went to Paravaiyar's mansion with joy. During the days he stayed happily with Paravai Pirattiyar, he visited many temples near the beautiful, cool Thiruvarur, worshipped them, and with great joy and desire, he first worshipped Thyagesa Peruman, the Lord of gods, who resides in Thiruvarur, and stayed there. He is the leader of the people of Thirumunaippadi country. Now, the 56th verse: "After staying there for many days, by the grace of the Supreme Lord, he went from there to Thirunannilam, where the Lord, the hero with the great Meru mountain as his bow, resides. He entered the temple, worshipped with joy, and sang a Tamil garland of verses beginning with 'Thanniyal Vemaiyanan,' which is characteristic of that place." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stayed in Thiruvarur for many days with pleasure. During that time, by the grace of the Lord, he went from there to Thirunannilam Perungoyil, where the Lord, the hero who has the great Meru mountain as his bow, resides. He went to the temple there, worshipped with joy, and sang a Tamil garland of verses, the Thiruppathigam, beginning with "Thanniyal Vemaiyanan," which is characteristic of that place. The Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 98th Thiruppathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the tune of Panchamam. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam.

"The Lord, who is cool and hot, begs for alms at every threshold, taking it from women with sweet words. He is the Lord of the cremation ground. The pure Brahmins praise His feet according to tradition. He is the beloved Lord of Thirunannilam Perungoyil." "Those who are skilled in these ten verses sung by Sadaiyan's son, Thiruvaruran, the father of Singi, who loves the Lord of Thirunannilam Perungoyil, built by Ko-Chenganan, who has high white bulls, will enter the supreme world." Thiruchitrambalam. The Thiruppathigam states that Lord Shiva is the beloved Lord of Thirunannilam Perungoyil, built by Ko-Chenganan. This Thirunannilam Perungoyil is the 71st temple on the south bank of the Cauvery. It is one of the Madakkoils (temples built on a raised platform). It is called Madhuvanam. Nannilam is located on the way from Mayiladuthurai to Thiruvarur. The Lord is Madhuvaneeswarar, Pirakasanathar, Devaranyeswarar. The Goddess is Madhuvaneeswari, Devagantharanayagi. The sacred waters are Brahma Theertham, Soola Theertham. Nannilam is the name of the village, and Perungoyil is the name of the temple. This is the place where the Devas, unable to bear the suffering caused by Vrithrasura, transformed into bees and worshipped. Hence, it is called Madhuvanam. As proof of this, a beehive in the form of a lamp flame still exists inside the temple and is worshipped. This is one of the Madakkoils built by Ko-Chenganan Chola Nayanar. This temple has many names like Madhuvanam, Devaranyam, Sundaravanam, Piruhathpuram. Inside the temple, there are also the Mahadevalingam worshipped by Brahma and the Agastheeswarar Lingam worshipped by Agastya. These are the details about this temple. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having worshipped in Thirunannilam, then proceeds to Thiruvezhimizhalai. Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing this history. The 57th verse: "After staying there and singing, the Supreme Lord went to Thiruvezhimizhalai, where the Brahmins, who were exalted by the ancient Vedas, gathered in abundance. The country rejoiced as they laid out a path with a pavadai (carpet), and adorned it with plantain trees, areca nut trees, and beautiful gem-studded toranams (arches)." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes from Thirunannilam to Thiruvezhimizhalai. In Thiruvezhimizhalai, the Brahmins, who were exalted by the ancient Vedic traditions, gathered in abundance. The country rejoiced as they laid out a path with a pavadai, and adorned it with plantain trees, areca nut trees, and beautiful gem-studded toranams, arranged in rows, and welcomed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The 58th verse: "They came and welcomed Nambi, and he too, with a joyful mind, went to Thiruvezhimizhalai, worshipped the Lord, and bowed before the temple, which had risen with abundant light from the ancient sky. He then praised and worshipped the feet of his Lord, who cuts off worldly bonds." This verse is composed. As they came to welcome Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, he entered the temple. Nambi, with a joyful mind, went to Thiruvezhimizhalai, worshipped the Lord, and bowed before the temple, which had risen with abundant light from the ancient sky. After bowing, he praised and worshipped the feet of his Lord, who cuts off worldly bonds.

The 59th and 60th verses are as follows: "He praised the Lord, who is worshipped by Vishnu, who sleeps on the coiled serpent, and by Brahma, who is on the lotus, the Lord who is beyond all, with overflowing joy. He sang a Tamil garland of verses with profound words, saying, 'You have taken the form of a beggar in Vizhi. Please bless me too.' On those days, he received the divine grace from the Lord of Vizhi, who was able to give coins according to the exchange rate. He then worshipped the feet of the Lord of Thiruvanjiyam, who cuts off the bonds of souls and takes them as His own, and sang the flawless Thiruppathigam beginning with 'Poruvanar,' and then went to Arisilkarai Puthur." These verses are composed. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships the Lord of Thiruvezhimizhalai. He praises Lord Shiva, the Lord of gods, who is beyond all, worshipped by Vishnu, who sleeps on the coiled serpent, and by Brahma, who is on the lotus, with overflowing joy. He sings a Tamil garland of verses with profound words, saying, "You have taken the form of a beggar in Vizhi. Please bless me too." He resides there. The Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruvezhimizhalai is the 88th Thiruppathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the tune of Seegamarappann. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "The Lord who grants grace to those who believe, the Brahmin who is the abode of the four Vedas, in Thirumizhalai, where beautiful women adorn themselves, the Lord whom the celestials worship, who resides with the great mountain goddess, you have taken the form of a beggar. Please bless me too." "The Vedic Brahmins, who follow the Vedic law, in Vizhi, where the wide waters flow, you have taken the form of a beggar. Please bless me too. The Lord of Thirunavalur, Vanthondar, who sings sweet Tamil verses, those who are skilled in them will join the Supreme Lord." Thiruchitrambalam. We have contemplated the special features of Thiruvezhimizhalai in the Puranams of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal receives the divine grace from the Lord of Thiruvezhimizhalai, who was able to give coins according to the exchange rate. This refers to the history of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman receiving coins with interest and Thirunavukkarasu Swamigal receiving coins without interest. The Thiruppathigam also states, "You gave coins daily, desiring to hear Tamil with music." Having received the divine grace from the Lord of Thiruvezhimizhalai, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships the feet of the Lord of Thiruvanjiyam, who cuts off the bonds of souls and takes them as His own, and sings the flawless Thiruppathigam beginning with "Poruvanar." The Thiruppathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruvanjiyam is the 76th Thiruppathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the tune of Piyanthai Kantharam. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "The Lord who is like a thread, wearing the sacred thread, with Uma, whose breasts are like tender shoots, does not come to those who are hostile. Our Lord, who is worshipped by the celestials, resides in Thiruvanjiyam. He does not allow karma to afflict His devotees." "In Thiruvanjiyam, where paddy fields shine, the Lord with the lightning-like matted hair, whose feet are worshipped, the Lord of Thirunavalur, who sings sweet Tamil verses, please grant us the joy of singing." Thiruchitrambalam. We have contemplated the temple history of Thiruvanjiyam in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having worshipped in Thiruvanjiyam, then proceeds to Arisilkarai Puthur. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 10 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 848, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrived at Thiruarisilkari Puthur, he went to Thirunaraiyur Siddheechcharam on the way and praised the Lord there, singing a Thirupathigam (hymn). The 61st verse, "Sezhuneer Naraiyur Nilavu Thiruchittheechcharamum Panindhu Ethi Vizhuneermaiyinil Perum Thondar Virippinodum Ethirkolla Mazhuvodu Ilamaan Karathalaththil Udaiyar Thirupputhur Vanangi Thozhu Neermaiyinil Thuthiththu Ethi Thondar Suzh Uraiyum Naal," is followed by the 62nd verse, which states: "Punithanaar Mun Pugazh Thunaiyaarkku Arulum Thiramum Pottraisaitthu Munivar Potra Ezhuntharuli Moori Vellakkangaiyinil Pani Venthingal Ani Sadaiyaar Pathigal Palavum Panindhu Ponthu Iniya Ninaivil Eythinaar Iraivar Thiruvaavaduthuraiyil." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows and praises Thiruchittheechcharam, which is situated in Naraiyur, a place of abundant water. Naraiyur is the name of the town, and Siddheechcharam is the name of the temple. The Lord of Thirunaraiyur Siddheechcharam is Siddhanathar, and the Goddess is Azhagammai. We have already discussed the special features of this holy place in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. This Thirupathigam is the 7th Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirunaraiyur Siddheechcharam. The Thirupathigam is as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Neerum Malarum Nilavum Sadai Mel Oorum Aravum Udaiyaan Idam Am Vaarum Aruvi Mani Pon Kozhiththu Seram Naraiyur Siddheechcharame Poraar Puramai Punithan Amarum Seeraar Naraiyur Siddhi Imai Or Ulaga Eythuvare." Thiruchitrambalam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after bowing and worshipping Thirunaraiyur Siddheechcharam, as stated in Sivagnana Siddhiyar that it is rare to attain the Saiva faith with a humble disposition, proceeds to Thirupputhur, where Lord Shiva, holding a battle-axe and a young deer in His hand, is worshipped. The devotees, with great eagerness, come forward to receive him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after worshipping Thiruarisilkari Puthur, sings a Thirupathigam in praise of the Lord, who, in the past, bestowed gold coins upon Pugazhthunai Nayanar at this very place. We will learn about Pugazhthunai Nayanar's Puranam in a later section of Periya Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, when he visited this place, included the nature of the Lord who bestowed grace there in his Thirupathigam. This Thirupathigam is the 9th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Indalam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Malaikku Magal Anja Madakariyai Uriththeer Eriththeer Varumuppurangal Silaikkum Kolai Sevuganthu Etroliyeer Silpalikku Ilgal Thorum Selavoliyeer Kalai Kombum Kari Maruppum Idari Kalavam Mayil Peeliym Kaar Agilum Alaikkum Punal Ser Arisil Thenkarai Azhagaar Thirupputhur Azhaga Neere. Kaarur Mazhai Peythu Pozhi Aruvik Kalaiyoda Agil Undittu Iru Karaiyum Porur Punal Ser Arisil Thenkarai Pozhilaar Thirupputhur Punithar Thammai Aarooran Arum Thamizh Ainthinodu Ainthu Azhagaal Uraippaargalum Ketpavargalum Seerur Tharu Devar Ganangalodum Inangi Sivalogam Eythuvare." Thiruchitrambalam. In the 6th verse of this Thirupathigam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praises the grace of the Lord who bestowed gold coins upon Pugazhthunai Nayanar. That verse is as follows: "Agathadimai Seyyum Anthana Than Arisil Punal Kondu Vanthu Aattugindraan Miga Thalarvu Eythi Kudaththaiyum Num Mudimel Vizhththittu Nadunguthalum Vaguththu Avanukku Nitthal Padiyum Varum Endru Oru Kaasinai." We have already discussed the Sthala Puranam (history of the temple) of Thiruarisilkari Puthur in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after bowing and worshipping Arisilkari Puthur, received the Lord's permission and proceeded to visit various holy places such as Thirunageswaram, Thiruvaidaimarudur, Thenkurangaduthurai, and finally arrived at Thiruvaavaduthurai. The 63rd verse states: "Vilangum Thiruvaavaduthuraiyil Meyaar Koyil Pudai Valam Kondu Ulam Kondu Urugum Anbinudan Ulpukku Irainghi Ethuvar Valam Kol Pathigam Maraiyavan Endru Eduththu Valavan Sengannan Thalam Kol Pirappum Sirappiththu Thamizh Solmalai Saaththinaar." Thiruvaavaduthurai is a glorious holy place that causes the world to shine. This glory is attributed to Thirumooladevar Nayanar, who revealed the meaning of Sivagama (Saiva scriptures) in Tamil as Thirumandhiram for the benefit of cows, and also to many other special features of Thiruvaavaduthurai. In such a glorious Thiruvaavaduthurai, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with his heart captivated and melting with love, enters the temple, bows, and praises the Lord. At that time, he offers a garland of Tamil words, a rich Thirupathigam beginning with "Maraiyavan." In this Thirupathigam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal describes the story of Kochchengan Chozha Nayanar, who was born as a king in the same place where he had previously served as a spider. Sekkizhar Peruman, in this verse, mentions the beginning of this Thirupathigam as "Maraiyavan Endru Eduththu Sirappiththu Saaththinaar" (began with "Maraiyavan" and praised it). After this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal will visit Thiruvaavaduthurai again and sing many more Thirupathigams. Those are parts of later stories. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman mentions that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang the Thirupathigam beginning with "Maraiyavan" at this time. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruvaavaduthurai is the 66th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Thakkasi. Only 5 verses of this Thirupathigam are available to us. The first two verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Maraiyavan Oru Maani Vanthu Adaiya Vaaramay Avan Aaruyir Niruththa Karaikkol Veludai Kaalanai Kaalaal Kadandha Kaaranam Kandu Kandu Adiyen Iraivan Emperumaan Endru Eppothum Ethi Ethi Nindru Anjali Seythu Un Araikkol Sevadiyarku Anbodum Adaindhen Aavaduthurai Aathi Emmane. Therundavaiyidai Noolkondu Silanthi Siththirappanthar Sikkana Iyathra Surunda Senchadaiyaayathu Thannai Chozhan Aakkiya Thodarchi Kandu Adiyen Purandu Vizhnthu Nin Pon Malar Paatham Potri Potri Endru Anbodum Pulambi Arundu En Mel Vinaikku Anji Vanthu Adaindhen Aavaduthurai Aathi Emmane." Thiruchitrambalam. "O Lord of Thiruvaavaduthurai, having seen many of Your acts of grace with my own eyes, I have come to You, fearing my strong karma. Please protect me." This is the gist of this Thirupathigam. We have already discussed the special features of Thiruvaavaduthurai in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Now, the 64th verse states: "Saaththi Angu Vaigu Naal Thayangum Anbarudan Kooda Perthum Irainghi Arul Petru Pennor Paagi Thenkarai Mel Thigazhum Pathigal Pala Paninthu Adainthaar Munaippadi Thalaivar." After offering the garland of Tamil words and staying there for some days, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, accompanied by the shining devotees, again bowed, received the Lord's grace, and proceeded to visit many holy places on the south bank of the Cauvery, where the Lord, with Uma as one half of His body, is pleased to reside. From Thiruvaavaduthurai, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visits various holy places on the south bank of the Cauvery, such as Thirukozhambam, Thiruchathanur, Thenkurangaduthurai, and also the holy places on the south bank of the tributary rivers that branch off from the Cauvery. He then proceeds to Thiruvaidaimarudur to worship the Lord, who is known as Mahalingam and Idai Marudhu Eesar. The verse states: "Mannu Maruthin Amarnthavarai Vanangi Madhurachchon Malargal Panni Punainthu Paninthu Ethi Paravi Ponthu Thondarudan Annathiyil Irunthu Agazhvaar Aranaar Thirunagechcharaththai Munni Pukku Valam Kondu Muthalvar Thiruththaal Vananginaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows and praises Idai Marudhu Eesar, Mahalingam Peruman, the Lord who resides in the stable Marutha tree, and offers a garland of sweet Tamil words. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruvaidaimarudur is the 60th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Thakkasi. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Kazhuthai Kungumam Thaan Sumanthu Eyththaar Kaippar Paazh Puga Matrathu Pola Pazhuthu Naan Uzhandru Ulthadumaari Padusuzhiththalai Pattanan Enthaay Azhuthu Nee Irunthu En Seythi Manane Angana Arane Enamaattaa Iluthaaiyenukku Or Oyvagai Arulaay Idai Maruthu Urai Enthai Pirane. Araikkum Santhanaththodu Agil Undi Aivanam Sumanthu Aarnthu Iru Paalum Iraikkum Kaveri Thenkarai Thanmel Idai Maruthu Urai Enthai Piranaai Uraikkum Ooran Ozhiththigal Maalai Ullaththaal Ugandhu Eththa Vallaargal Naraippu Mooppodu Nadalaiyum Indri Naathan Sevadi Nannuvar Thaame." Thiruchitrambalam. "O Lord, my Father, who resides in Idai Marudhu, please grant me a way to escape, for I am a fool." This is the crown jewel of the Thirupathigam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after bowing and worshipping Thiruvaidaimarudur, proceeds to Thirunageswaram, enters the temple, circumambulates it, and bows at the feet of the Lord. The 66th verse states: "Perugum Pathigam Pirai Ani Vaazh Nuthalaal Paadi Peyarnthu Nirai Thiruvin Maliyum Sivapuraththu Devar Perumaan Kazhal Vanangi Urugum Sinthaiyudan Ponthai Umaiyor Paagar Thaam Magizhndhu Maruvum Pathigal Pira Paninthu Kalaiyanallur Marungu Adainthaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after bowing and worshipping Thirunageswaram, sings a Thirupathigam beginning with "Pirai Ani Vaazh Nuthalaal." The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirunageswaram is the 99th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Panchamam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Pirai Ani Vaazh Nuthalaal Umaiyaal Aval Pezhganikka Nirai Ani Nenju Anunga Neelamaal Vidam Undathu Enna Kurai Ani Kullai Mullai Azhaiththu Kulir Maathavi Mel Sirai Ani Vandugal Ser Thirunageschcharaththane. Kongu Anai Vandu Arathra Kuyilum Mayilum Payilum Thenkamizh Poombazhil Suzh Thirunageschcharaththanaai Vangam Mali Kadal Suzh Vayal Naaval Ooran Sonna Pangam Il Paadal Vallaar Avardham Vinai Pattarume." Thiruchitrambalam. "O Lord of Thirunageswaram, who swallowed the poison, I inquire about Your acts of grace, such as when Uma, with a crescent moon on her forehead, was frightened." This Thirupathigam praises the Lord by asking about His acts of grace. Thus, after singing the Thirupathigam and receiving the Lord's permission, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Thiruchivapuram, a holy place filled with complete Shiva wealth, bows at the feet of Lord Shiva, the chief of the Devas, and with a melting heart, proceeds further to visit many holy places where the Lord, with Uma as one half of His body, is pleased to reside. He then arrives at Thirukalaiyanallur. The 67th verse states: "Semmai Maraiyur Thirukalaiyanallur Iraivar Sevadi Keezh Pommai Vanakkam Pera Irainghi Munbu Paravi Thozhuthu Ezhuvaar Kommai Maruvu Kurumbai Mulai Umaiyaal Enum Thirupathigam Meymai Pura Palavum Miga Sirappiththu Isaiyin Vilambinaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, bowing at the feet of the Lord of Thirukalaiyanallur, where righteous Brahmins reside, offers a complete obeisance with his three instruments (mind, speech, and body), praises the Lord, and then sings a Thirupathigam beginning with "Kurumbai Mulai Malarkuzhali." In this Thirupathigam, he beautifully describes many true Puranic stories with musical melody. In this Thirupathigam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal includes the stories mentioned in the Maha Puranas, which are called "Meymai Puranam" (true Puranas) by Sekkizhar Peruman. Therefore, all the stories mentioned in the Maha Puranas are true, and they convey a unique truth. Sekkizhar Peruman shows us that they are "Meymai Puranam," meaning they are about the true Lord, Sat. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirukalaiyanallur is the 16th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Thakkaragam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Kurumbai Malarkuzhali Konda Thavam Kandu Kurippinodum Sendru Aval Than Gunaththinai Nangu Arinthu Virumbum Varam Koduththu Avalai Vettu Aruli Seytha Vinnvar Kon Kan Meviya Oor Vinavil Arumbu Aruge Surumbu Aruva Arupatham Panpaada Ani Mayilgal Nadamaadum Ani Pozhil Suzh Ayalin Karumbu Aruge Karunguvalai Kanvalarum Kazhanik Kamalangal Muga Malarum Kalaiyanallur Kaane. Thanpulun Mathiyum Thaangiya Senchadaiyan Thaamaraiyon Thalai Kalanaa Kaamara Munpaadi Un Bali Kondu Uzhal Paraman Uraiyum Oor Niraineer Ozhugu Punal Arisilin Then Kalaiyanallur Athanaai Nanbudaiya Nan Sadaiyan Isaignani Siruvan Naavalarkon Aarooran Naavin Nayanthu Uraisai Panpayilum Paththum Ivai Paththi Seythu Paadavallavargal Allalodu Paavam Ilargal Thaame." Thiruchitrambalam. Thirukalaiyanallur is the 68th holy place on the south bank of the Cauvery. It is currently known as Sakkottai and is located near Kumbakonam. The word "Kalaiyam" refers to a pot, and "Kumbam" refers to a pot that contained living beings. This place was where living beings took refuge during the great flood (Jalapralayam) at the time of the deluge. Hence, it is called Kalaiyanallur. It is a holy place situated on the south bank of the Arisil River, which has abundant water. The Lord is Amirthakala Natha, Amirthakalasa Natha, Amirthakaleswarar, and the Goddess is Amirthavalli. The Theertham (holy water) is Amirthatheertham. People refer to this temple as Kottaichivan Koyil. The Dakshinamurthy idol here is a new idol with a wonderful structure. This idol has a Rudraksha mala in its upper right hand, fire in its upper left hand, Chinmudra in its right hand, and a book in its left hand. The hair is like a solar halo. It is adorned with a boar, and Brahma and Vishnu are seen bowing on either side. The Ardhanari (half-male, half-female) and Thapasviyammai (ascetic mother) forms here are also special. These are the special features of Thirukalaiyanallur. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after bowing and worshipping Thirukalaiyanallur, proceeds to Thirukudamookku, also known as Kumbakonam. We will continue our discussion. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 11 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 849, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. From Thirukalaiyanallur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Thirukudamookku. The 68th verse states: "Angu Nindru Thirukudamookku Adainthu Paninthu Paadi Poy Mangai Paagar Valanjoliyai Maruvi Perugum Anbu Uruga Thangu Kaathaludan Vanangi Thamizhaal Parasi Arasinukku Thingal Mudiyaarum Adiyaliiththa Thirunallurai Sendru Adainthaar." From Thirukalaiyanallur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrives at Thirukudamookku, bows, and sings a Thirupathigam. He then proceeds to Thiruvalanjoli, the abode of the Lord who has Uma as one half of His body. With overflowing love, his heart melting, and abundant devotion, he bows and praises the Lord with a Tamil Thirupathigam. Afterwards, he proceeds to Thirunallur, the holy place where the Lord bestowed His feet upon Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. The Thirupathigams sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirukudamookku (Kumbakonam), Thiruvalanjoli, and Thirunallur are not available to us due to our lack of merit. The 69th and 70th verses continue as follows: "Nallur Iraivar Kazhal Potri Navindru Naduvar Nambar Pathiyellaam Irainghi Ethi Ponthu Isaiyaal Paravum Thammudaiya Aninthavar Tham Sottruththuraiyin Marungu Eythi Allur Kandar Koyilin Ulladainthu Valam Kondu Adipanivaar Azhalneer Ozhugi Anaiya Enum Ansol Pathigam Eduththaruli Kazhal Neediya Anbinil Potrum Kaathal Kura Paraviya Pin Sezhuneermaiyinil Arul Petru Ponthu Paravaiyaark Kezhvar Muzhuneeru Anivaar Amarntha Pathi Palavum Paninthu Munnuvaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praises the feet of the Lord of Thirunallur, and then proceeds to visit and praise many holy places of the Lord in between. From Thirunallur, he departs and visits various holy places in between, such as Thiruppalaithurai and Thiruchakkarappalli, and then arrives at Thiruchottruthurai. He enters the temple of Lord Shiva, who has poison in His throat, circumambulates it, and bows at His feet. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal then praises the Lord of Thiruchottruthurai and sings a beautiful Thirupathigam beginning with "Azhalneer Ozhugiyanaiya." The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruchottruthurai is the 94th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Kausikam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Azhalneer Ozhugiyanaiya Sadaiyum Uzhaieer Uriyum Udaiyaan Idam Am Kalaineer Muththum Kanaga Kuvaiyum Suzhalneer Ponni Chottruththurai Thaaru Neer Chottruththuraiyul Mutraa Mathiser Muthalvan Paathaththu Attraar Adiyaar Adinaayooran Solthaan Ivai Kattraar Thunbu Ilare." Thiruchitrambalam. After singing this Thirupathigam and bowing, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds further, visiting many holy places where the Lord is pleased to reside. He visits holy places such as Thandankurai and Thiruvethikudi. The 71st and 72nd verses are as follows: "Devar Perumaan Kandiyur Paninthu Thiruvaiaaru Athanaai Mevivanangi Poonthuruththi Vimalar Paatham Thozhuthu Irainghi Sevil Varuvaar Thiru Aalampozhilil Serndhu Thaazhnthu Iravu Paavusayanaththu Amarntharuli Palli Kolla Kanavin Kan Mazhapaadiyinil Varuvatharkku Ninaikka Maranthaayo Endru Kuzhagu Aagiya Tham Kolam Ethirkaatti Arulak Kuriththu Unarnthu Nizhalaar Solaikkarai Ponni Vada Paal Eri Nedumaadum Azhagaar Veethi Mazhapaadi Adainthaar Nambi Aaroorar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after singing and worshipping at Thiruchottruthurai, departs from there and visits holy places such as Thandankurai and Thiruvethikudi. He then desires to visit more holy places where the Lord resides. He bows and worships Thirukkandiyur, the abode of Lord Shiva, the God of gods, and then proceeds to Thiruvaiyaru, where he worships Aiyarrappar. Afterwards, he bows and worships the feet of the Lord of Thiruppoonthuruththi and then arrives at Thiruvalampozhil. He bows to the Lord there and spends the night sleeping there. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this as "Paavusayanam." "Paavuthal" means to lie down on the ground, spreading the entire body. In medicine, it is considered unnatural to sleep with limbs bent. While he was sleeping, the Lord appeared in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's dream and asked, "Have you forgotten to come to Thirumazhapadi?" and showed him His beautiful, youthful form. The phrase "Have you forgotten to come to Thirumazhapadi?" would be sufficient here, but Sekkizhar Peruman states that Lord Shiva, in the dream, reminded Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Have you forgotten even to think of coming to Thirumazhapadi?" This is because Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was always in a state of Shiva Yoga, never forgetting the Lord's feet. He was not one who forgot the Lord. Forgetting is the absence of remembrance. Therefore, it would not be appropriate to say "forgotten." Here, for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the thought of coming to Thirumazhapadi is a special remembrance. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman states that Lord Shiva, in the dream, reminded Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Have you forgotten even to think of coming to Thirumazhapadi?" He showed him His beautiful, unique, and joyful form. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praised this form as "Ponnaar Meniyane" in the Thirupathigam he sang for Thirumazhapadi. Thus, after the Lord showed him His form, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal woke up, understood the meaning, and proceeded to Thirumazhapadi, a holy place with beautiful streets and tall mansions, situated on the north bank of the Cauvery, which has shady groves along its banks. Nambi Aaroorar Peruman arrives there. The 73rd and 74th verses are as follows: "Adainthu Thirukopuram Irainghi Anbar Suzhudan Pugunthu Panam Kol Aravum Aninthaar Mun Paninthu Vizhnthu Param Karunai Gunam Kol Arulin Thiram Potri Konda Pulagaththudan Urugi Punarntha Isaiyaal Thirupathigam Ponnaar Meniyane Endru Eduththu Anne Unnai Allaai Yaar Yaarai Ninaikkene Ena Ethi Thanneer Illaa Pathigamalai Saaththi Thozhuthu Purambu Adainthu Mannum Pathiyil Sila Naatkal Vaigi Thondarudan Magizhndhu Ponni Karaiyin Irumarangum Paninthu Melpaal Pothuvaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having arrived at Thirumazhapadi, approaches the temple, bows at the Gopuram (temple tower), and with devotees surrounding him, enters the temple. He bows down before the Lord, who is adorned with a serpent with a hood, and praises His boundless compassion and grace. With his body covered in goosebumps and his heart melting, he sings a musical Thirupathigam beginning with "Ponnaar Meniyane" and ending with "Anne Unnai Allaai Ini Yaarai Ninaikkene," offering a unique garland of hymns. "Anne Unnai Allaai Ini Yaarai Ninaikkene" is the crown jewel and the essence of the Thirupathigam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang this Thirupathigam with this crown jewel, "Whom else can I think of but You?" in response to the Lord's question, "Have you forgotten to think of coming?" We can see the unique quality of this Thirupathigam in the fact that people still eagerly recite it today. We also see that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praised and expressed his deep devotion in this Thirupathigam. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirumazhapadi is the 24th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Nattaragam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Ponnaar Meniyane Puli Tholai Araikkasaithu Minnaar Senchadai Mel Milir Kondrai Aninthavane Manne Maamaniye Mazhapadiyul Maanikkame Anne Unnai Allaai Ini Yaarai Ninaikkene. Eraar Muppuramum Eriya Silai Thottavanai Vaaraar Kongaiyudan Mazhapadiyul Meyavanai Seeraar Naavalarkon Aarooran Uraiththa Thamizh Paaroor Eththavallaar Paralokaththu Iruppaare." Thiruchitrambalam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang the Thirupathigam and stayed at Thirumazhapadi for some days, rejoicing with the devotees. After that, he proceeded westward, visiting the holy places on both banks of the Cauvery. Until now, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal had traveled along the south bank of the Cauvery. It is noteworthy that after being reminded by the Lord, he visited both banks of the Cauvery. The holy places visited by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal on the north bank of the Cauvery include Thiruanbil, Aalathurai, Thiru Thavathurai, Vadakarai, Thirumaanthurai, etc. The holy places on the south bank include Thirukaanur, Thiruniyamam, Thiruvengur, etc. However, the Thirupathigams sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for these holy places are not available to us due to our lack of merit. Now, the 75th, 76th, and 77th verses are as follows: "Seyya Sadaiyaar Thiruvaanaikkaavil Adainthu Thiruththondar Eytha Munvanthu Ethirkolla Irainghi Koyil Ulpugunthu Aiyar Kamalachchevadi Keezh Aarvam Peruga Vizhnthu Ezunthu Meyyum Mugizhkka Kan Pozhi Neervellam Parappa Vimmavaar Maraigal Ena Malarntha Senchol Thamizh Pathigam Niraiyum Kaathaludan Eduththu Nilavum Anbardhamai Nokki Iraiyum Panivaar Emmaiyum Aaludaiyaar Endru Ethuvar Uraiyur Chozhan Maniyaaram Saaththum Thiramathai Unarnthu Aruli Valavar Perumaan Maniyaaram Saaththikkondu Varum Ponni Kilarum Thira Neer Muzhuthalum Vazhuvi Poga Ketham Ura Alavil Thirumanjanakkudaththu Athu Pukku Aatta Aninthu Aruli Thalarum Avanukku Arul Purintha Saattrinaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrives at Thiruvanaikka, the temple of the Lord with red matted hair. The devotees come forward to receive him. He bows, enters the temple, and falls at the lotus feet of the Lord, rising again with overflowing devotion. His body is covered in goosebumps, and tears of joy flow from his eyes as he is overcome with emotion. He then sings a Tamil Thirupathigam of eloquent words, beginning with "Maraigal Aayina Naangum" (the four Vedas), with abundant love, and looking at the devotees, he praises the Lord, saying, "He is the Lord who owns even us." "Maraigal Aayina Naangum" is the beginning of the Thiruvanaikka Thirupathigam. "Emmaiyum Aaludaiyaar" is the crown jewel of the Thirupathigam. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 75th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Kantharam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Maraigal Aayina Naangum Matru Ulla Porulgalum Ellaa Thuraiyum Thoththira Thiraththu Iraiyum Thonmaiyum Nanmaiyum Aaya Araiyum Poombunal Aanaikkaavudai Aathiyai Naalum Iraivan Endru Adiservaargal Emmaiyum Aaludaiyare. Aazhiyaarkku Arul Aanaikkaavudai Aathi Pon Adiyin Nizhale Saranaaga Nindru Arul Kura Ninainthu Vaazhavalla Vanthondan Van Thamizh Maalai Vallaar Poy Ezhu Maa Pirappum Attru Emmaiyum Aaludaiyare." Thiruchitrambalam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings the Thirupathigam and also describes the miraculous story that occurred at Thiruvanaikka. He praises the grace of the Lord who accepted the pearl necklace of the Chola king of Uraiyur and includes it in his Thirupathigam. A long time ago, a Chola king, while wearing a pearl necklace, bathed in the Cauvery River. When he immersed himself in the Cauvery's turbulent waters, the necklace slipped and fell into the water. The king was saddened. When the necklace slipped, the king remembered the Lord of Thiruvanaikka and prayed, "O Lord, please accept this necklace." As he prayed, the Lord caused the necklace to enter the holy water pot. When the holy water was being offered to the Lord of Thiruvanaikka, the necklace fell on His body through the water, and He adorned it. This is the Sthala Varalaru (history of the temple). Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praises this in his Thirupathigam. The verse in which Sundaramoorthy Swamigal describes this story is the 7th verse in the Thirupathigam we just saw: "Thaaram Aagiya Ponni Than Thurai Aadi Veluththu Neeril Nindru Adipotri Ninmala Kol Ena Aange Aaram Konda Em Aanaikkaa Udai Aathiyai Naalum Eeram Ullavar Naalum Emmaiyum Aaludaiyare." Thus, after singing the Thirupathigam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stayed at Thiruvanaikka for some days. We will continue our discussion. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 12 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 850, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stays at Thiruvanaikka for some days and worships the Lord. The 78th verse states: "Saatri Angu Thangu Naal Thayangum Pavala Thirumeni Neetrar Koyil Emmarungum Sendru Thaazhnthu Nirai Viruppinaal Potri Angu Nindrum Poy Poruvil Anbar Maruviya Thondu Aatrum Perumai Thiruppaachilaachiramam Sendru Adainthaar." As mentioned earlier, while staying at Thiruvanaikka, after offering garlands of Tamil words, he visits and worships the temples of Lord Shiva, who has sacred ash on His coral-like body, in the vicinity. These include Thirumookkeechcharam in Thiruchirappalli Uraiyur and Thirukarkudi. He bows there and praises the Lord with abundant devotion. Then, he proceeds further and arrives at Thiruppaachilaachiramam. In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Thiruppaachilaachiramam as "Poruvil Anbar Maruviya Thondu Aatrum Perumai Thiruppaachilaachiramam" (Thiruppaachilaachiramam, the glorious place where incomparable devotees perform service). The "incomparable devotees" refers to the story of Kolli Mazhavan and the service of devotees like him, which has been prominent in this holy place for generations. We have seen in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, from verse 2209 to 2218, that when Thirugnanasambandha Peruman visited this holy place, Thiruppaachilaachiramam, he performed a miracle by curing Kolli Mazhavan's daughter of the Muyalagan disease. Such service rendered by Kolli Mazhavan is glorified in this holy place. Moreover, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is now going to sing a Thirupathigam here. This is also a glorious place. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings this Thirupathigam as if appealing to the devotees. Therefore, this holy place is also glorious for having devotees who stood there and inspired him to sing the Thirupathigam. Hence, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it in that manner. The 79th, 80th, and 81st verses are as follows: "Sendru Thirukopuram Irainghi Devar Malintha Thirunthu Mani Mundril Valam Kondu Ul Adainthu Muthalvar Munnam Vizhnthu Irainghi Nandru Perugum Porul Kaathal Nayappu Peruga Naathar Ethirnindru Paravi Ninaintha Porul Arulaathu Ozhiya Nernindru Anbu Neengaa Achchamudan Aduththa Thiruththozhamai Paniyaal Pon Peraatha Thiruvullum Puzhunga Azhungi Puramboru Paal Munbu Nindru Thiruththondar Mugappe Muraippaadu Udaiyaarpola Enbu Karainthu Piranaar Matru Illaiyo Enna Eduththukindraar Nitthamum Neengaa Nilaimaiyin Neengi Nilaththidai Pulam Kezhum Pirappaal Uytha Kaaranaththai Unarnthu Nonthu Adimai Orumaiyaam Ezhumaiyum Unarththi Eththanai Arulaathu Ozhiyinum Piranaar Ivaralaathu Illaiyo Enbaar Vaiththanan Thanakke Thalaiyum En Naavum Ena Vazhuththinaar Vazhi Thondar." These three verses are consecutive. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Thiruppaachilaachiramam, bows at the Gopuram, and circumambulates the holy courtyard. This courtyard is "Devar Malintha Thirunthu Mani Mundril" (a beautiful, jeweled courtyard where Devas gather). It is a courtyard where Devas gather, waiting for the Lord's command to enter the temple and worship Him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal circumambulates such a courtyard, enters, and bows down before the Lord. There, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays to the Lord for gold. He prays for "Nandru Perugum Porul" (wealth that brings good). Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays for wealth to be used for Maheswara Puja (worship of Shiva) and for the Lord's festivals. Therefore, it is wealth that brings good. He stands before the Lord with great desire and worships Him. However, the Lord does not bestow the desired wealth. Since the Lord does not bestow the desired gold, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal feels a burning sorrow in his heart, even though his love remains unwavering, due to the friendship and service he has with the Lord. When love is present, fear and love do not coexist. However, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's love remains unwavering here, and the fear he experiences is the fear that arises before the love of a mother. Thus, with unwavering love and fear, because the Lord did not bestow the gold he desired, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, standing before the Lord, turns away from Him and sings a Thirupathigam as if appealing to the devotees standing in the sanctum, asking, "Is there no other Lord?" Sekkizhar Peruman composes the 89th verse: "Nitthamum Neengaa Nilaimaiyin Neengi Nilaththidai Pulam Kezhum Pirappaal Uytha Kaaranaththai Unarnthu." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who was serving in Thirukkailayam, never separated from the Lord. He laments, realizing the reason for his birth in this world, having left Thirukkailayam. We have discussed this reason in detail in the beginning of Periya Puranam, in Thirumalai Sirappu and Thaduththaatkondu Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who is eternal, even if separated from his body, never separated from the Lord in his thoughts. When he left Thirukkailayam, he asked for the boon, "O Lord, please stop me and take me into Your grace when I am deluded by human illusion." Having received that boon, he came to this world. Lord Shiva commanded him to be born in this world, saying, "Be born in the southern land." This was for two main reasons: for the southern land to prosper due to great penance, and for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to compose the Thiruththondaththogai (a collection of hymns about devotees). To achieve this, the Lord made him fall in love with women. Therefore, for the southern land to prosper due to great penance, and for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to compose the Thiruththondaththogai, these two are the main reasons for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's incarnation. His falling in love with women in the Nandavanam (garden) of Thirukkailayam was a secondary reason that led to the main reason. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal realizes this reason here and laments. This is because he needs gold for his pilgrimages and the service of devotees in the southern land, and also for his two marriages. One marriage, with Paravai Nachiyar, has already taken place. Now, he needs to marry Sangili Nachiyar. To accomplish all these, he needs essential wealth. Even though he is asking the Lord for it now, the Lord is not bestowing it. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal realizes that the purpose of his incarnation in this world must be fulfilled and laments. Sekkizhar Peruman states, "Adimai Orumaiyaam Ezhumaiyum Unarththi" (revealing the oneness of his servitude for seven births). Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's oneness with Lord Shiva, without any duality in his devotion, is such a oneness. With such a feeling, he sings a Thirupathigam, appealing to the Lord as if speaking to the devotees. We can see that the oneness of servitude for seven births is mentioned in every verse of this Thirupathigam. Sekkizhar Peruman composes this verse in a way that exemplifies the idea that "my servitude to Your feet is like a comparison," which is found in the first verse of this Thirupathigam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings the Thirupathigam with the sentiment, "Even if he does not bestow it, is there no other Lord?" and praises, "I have given my head and tongue to Him alone." This Thirupathigam is composed in such a way that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asks, "He did not give me the gold I asked for. If he is not there, is there no one else to bestow grace upon me? Is there no other Lord besides him?" The Lord is everywhere. Therefore, even if He does not bestow grace here, it means that I will receive it in other places where the Lord manifests. The Lord is the only leader who bestows grace and takes us into His fold. I have accepted Him alone as my Lord. He is my support, my refuge. There is no one else but Him. Therefore, I must ask Him alone. I must ask Him alone. This is the inner meaning. This is the characteristic of chaste women. This is also religious chastity. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruppaachilaachiramam is the 14th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Thakkaragam. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Vaiththanan Thanakke Thalaiyum En Naavum Nenjamum Vanjam Ondru Indri Uythanan Thanakke Thiruvadikku Adimai Uraiththa Kaal Uvamane Okkum Paiththa Paambu Aarththu Or Kovanaththodu Paachilaachiramaththu Em Paramar Pitthare Otthu Or Nachchilar Aagil Ivaralaathu Illaiyo Piranaar. Esina Alla Igazhnthana Alla Emperumaan Endru Eppothum Paayina Pugazhaan Paachilaachiramaththu Adigalai Adithozha Pal Naal Vaayinaal Kuri Manaththinaal Ninaivaan Vala Vayal Naaval Aarooran Pesina Pechchai Poruththilar Aagil Ivaralaathu Illaiyo Piranaar." Thiruchitrambalam. This Thirupathigam has 12 verses. Sekkizhar Peruman describes what the Lord did after this Thirupathigam was sung in the next verse. The 82nd verse states: "Ivvagai Paravi Thirukkadaikkaappum Esina Endru Isaippa Meyvagai Virumbu Tham Perumaanaar Vizhunithi Kuvai Aliththu Arula Maivalar Kandar Karunaiye Paravi Vanangi Appathiyidai Vaigi Evvagai Marungum Iraivar Tham Pathigal Irainghi Angu Irunthanar Sila Naal." Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang the Thirupathigam and also included "Esina Alla" (not abusive) in the Thirukkadaikkaappu (concluding verse). He sang up to 11 verses with the sentiment "Is there no other Lord besides him?" and in the 12th verse, the Thirukkadaikkaappu, he sang the opposite, "Esina Alla Igazhnthana Alla" (not abusive, not contemptuous). The Lord, who desires true love, the Lord of Thiruppaachilaachiramam, bestows a great heap of wealth, gold coins. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praises and bows to the great compassion of the Lord, who has a dark throat. What kind of compassion is this? It is the compassion that, when he worshipped the Lord earlier, He did not bestow grace, but when he sang the Thirupathigam, He bestowed grace. It is the compassion that commanded him to sing the Thirupathigam and rejoiced in hearing it. We can recall the past stories of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, such as "Archana Paatte Aagum" (worship is song) and "Pitthan Endre Paaduvaai" (sing as a madman). Therefore, the Lord, who is compassionate enough to listen to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's Thirupathigams, bestowed grace. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal remembers that great compassion, praises it, bows, and stays at that holy place for some days. At that time, he visits and praises many holy places of the Lord on both banks of the Cauvery, in the vicinity of Thiruppaachilaachiramam. We will continue our discussion. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 13 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 851, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. The 83rd verse states: "Appathi Neengi Arulaal Pogi Aavin Anju Aaduvaar Needum Eppeyar Pathiyum Irumarungu Irainghi Iraivar Paingeliyai Eythi Paippani Anivaar Kopuram Irainghi Paangamar Pudai Valam Kondu Thuppu Urul Veniyar Kazhal Thozhuvaar Thondrum Kangaalarai Kandaar." From Thiruppaachilaachiramam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having received the Lord's permission, proceeds to visit all the holy places on both banks of the Cauvery where the Lord, who bathes in the five products of the cow (Panchagavya), resides. These are believed to be holy places such as Thiruchchenthurai, Thiruvalanthurai, Thiruththuraiyur, Thirupparaiththurai, etc. However, the Thirupathigams sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for these holy places are not available. Thus, after visiting all these holy places, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrives at Thiruppaingeeli, a holy place where the Lord resides. He bows at the temple Gopuram of Lord Shiva at Thiruppaingeeli, circumambulates it, and bows at the feet of the Lord, who has coral-like matted hair. At that time, the Lord appears before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in the form of a Kangaalar (a mendicant carrying a skull). The 84th verse states: "Kandavar Kangal Kaathal Neer Vellam Pozhithara Kaikkkuvitthu Irainghi Vandaraik Kuzhalaar Manam Kavar Balikku Varum Thiruvadivu Kandavargal Kondathu Or Mayalaal Vinavu Kootru Aaga Kulavu Sol Kaarulaaviya Endru Andar Naayagarai Paravi Aaraniya Vidangar Am Arum Thamizh Punainthaar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, seeing the Lord in the form of a Kangaalar, with tears of joy flowing from his eyes due to love, folds his hands and bows. He then composes a beautiful Tamil Thirupathigam, beginning with "Kaarulaaviya," praising the Lord of the Devas, with the crown jewel "Aaraniya Vidangar," as if speaking the words of women with hair adorned with bees, who, captivated by the Lord's form as He comes for alms, are enchanted by Him. The wives of the Rishis of Tharukavanam, seeing the Lord in this form, were captivated and surrendered their hearts to Him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal composes this Thirupathigam as if these women, captivated by His form, are speaking words of deep love to the Lord. "Aaraniyam" means "coming from the forest," and "Vidangar" means "one whose form is not carved," meaning a naturally formed divine image, not created by anyone. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal composed this Thirupathigam as if women in love with the Lord are singing it. The Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 36th Thirupathigam in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Kolli. The first and last verses of this Thirupathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Kaarulaaviya Nanjai Undu Irul Kandar Venthalai Odu Kondu Oorellaam Thirinthu Ensai Veer Bali Or Idaththile Kollum Neer Paarellaam Pani Ummaiye Paravi Paniyum Paingeeliyir Aaramavathu Naagamo Sollum Aaraniya Vidangare. Annam Ser Vayal Suzh Paingeeliyil Aaraniya Vidangarai Minnum Nonnidai Mangai Maar Palar Vendik Kaathal Mozhintha Sol Mannu Thol Pugazh Naaval Ooran Vanthondan Vaaymozhi Paadal Paththu Unni Innisai Paaduvaar Umaikkezhvan Sevadi Servare." Thiruchitrambalam. The 85th verse states: "Paravi Appathiga Thirukkadaikkaappu Saaththi Mun Paninthu Arul Petru Karavil Anbargal Tham Kootamum Thozhuthu Kalanthu Inithu Irunthu Ponthu Aruli Viraviya Ingoymalai Muthalaaga Vimalar Tham Pathi Pala Vanangi Kuravalar Solai Ani Thiruppaandik Kodumudi Adainthanar Kongil." After singing the Thirupathigam as mentioned above and offering the Thirukkadaikkaappu, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows before the Lord, receives His grace, bows to the company of sincere devotees, joins them, and stays there happily. Then, he proceeds further, visiting many holy places of the Lord, such as Thiru Ingoymalai. However, the Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiru Ingoymalai is not available to us due to our lack of merit. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal thus visits all the ancient holy places along the banks of the Cauvery, after Thiruppaingeeli, and arrives at Thiruppaandik Kodumudi in Kongu Nadu. He arrives at Thiruppaandik Kodumudi in Kongu Nadu. The 86th and 87th verses are as follows: "Konginil Ponni Thenkarai Karaiyur Kodi Kodumudi Koyil Mun Kurugi Sanga Venkuzhaiyaar Uzha Valam Seythu Saarnthu Adi Anbinil Thaazhvu Ezuthu Pongiya Vetkai Perugida Thozhuthu Punithar Ponmeniyai Nokki Ingu Ivar Thammai Marakka Onnaathu Endru Ezuntha Meykkurippinil Eduppa Annelaar Adigal Marakkinum Naamam Anjezuththu Ariya Eppozhuthum Enniya Naave Insuvai Peruga Idaiyaraathu Iyambum Endru Ithanaai Thinnaiya Unarvil Kolbavar Matra Pattrilen Ena Sezhunthamizhaal Nanniya Anbinil Pinniyur Navindraar Namachivaaya Thirupathigam." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrives at the Karaiyur Paandik Kodumudi temple, situated on the south bank of the Cauvery in Kongu Nadu. He circumambulates the temple of the Lord, who wears a conch earring, and bows at His feet with love. With overflowing desire, he bows and looks at the Lord's golden body. A true realization arises in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's heart that he can never forget this Lord. Until now, Nambigal (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) had traveled in Chola Nadu, visiting holy places on both the north and south banks of the Cauvery. Here, after worshipping Thiru Ingoymalai, which is the 63rd holy place among the holy places on the north bank of the Cauvery, he leaves Chola Nadu and travels to Kongu Nadu. This holy place is referred to as Paandik Kodumudi in the Thirupathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. One of the Sthala Varalarus (histories of the temple) states that this place got its name because a Pandyan king was cured of his skin disease here. Kodumudi is the name of the Lord. It is one of the great peaks of Meru, according to history. The Lord resides here, having made it His abode. Therefore, the Lord is known as Kodumudinathar. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, captivated by the splendor of the Lord's form here, is deeply immersed and realizes in his heart that he can never forget Him. Even if he forgets the Lord's feet, his tongue, which always remembers the five-lettered name (Panchaksharam), will continuously utter it with sweet relish. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal then sings the Namachivaya Thirupathigam, beginning with "Matra Patru Enakku Indri" (without any other attachment), with rich Tamil, bound by love. The tongue should continuously utter the five-lettered name until it becomes calloused. Remembrance is an act of the mind. The tongue, continuously stimulated by the mind, should become accustomed to uttering it on its own. When the mind, which is the internal instrument, and the tongue, which is the external instrument stimulated by it, become one and accustomed, the action of the mind becomes the action of the tongue. Sekkizhar Peruman states that he sang this Thirupathigam with sweet relish, in accordance with the saying that the function of the tongue is to utter the Namachivaya mantra, to utter the holy name. The Namachivaya Thirupathigam is the name of this Thirupathigam. The Thirupathigam beginning with "Matra Patru Enakku Indri" is called Namachivaya Thirupathigam, just as Kolara Thirupathigam and Thiruneetru Thirupathigam are named. The inner realization that arose in his heart, "I can never forget Him," and the thought that was ingrained in that feeling, become the essence of the Thirupathigam. Sekkizhar Peruman highlights the unique feature of this Thirupathigam, which is about the five-lettered mantra and also about this holy place. Sekkizhar Peruman further describes the greatness of this Thirupathigam in the next verse. The 88th verse states: "Ulagaiyellaam Uyya Uruthiyaam Pathigam Uraiththu Meyvunarnvu Ara Orumai Nilaviya Sinthaiyudan Thiruvarulaal Neenguvaar Paangunar Pathigal Palavum Mun Paninthu Paramartha Thaazh Potri Ponthu Than Mani Malar Padappai Kulavumi Kongil Kaanjivaay Perur Kuruginaar Murugum Aatharaavaal." In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman states that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang the Namachivaya Thirupathigam, which is a firm assurance for the salvation of the entire world. He also states that he praised the Lord's grace with a mind filled with true knowledge and unwavering oneness. Then, with overflowing love, he proceeds to visit many holy places, praising the Lord, and arrives at Thirupperur, situated on the banks of the Kanchi River in the flourishing West Kongu Nadu, which has beautiful gardens of cool flowers. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this region as "Mee Kongu." Kongu Nadu is divided into three main regions: East Kongu (Keezhkongu), West Kongu (Melkongu), and North Kongu. "Mee Kongu" refers to West Kongu Nadu. Thirupperur, currently known as Perur, is located there. It is very close to Coimbatore. The Kanchi River flows there. It is called Kanchi River until it reaches Perur, originating from the Vanni Moolam in the Silver Mountain range, about 25 Naligai (a measure of distance) west of Perur. From Perur, until it merges with the Cauvery River, it is known as Noyyal. The Noyyal River is one of the tributaries of the Cauvery. It is always flowing with water and never dries up. It has the unique feature that the bones of the deceased, when immersed at the confluence of the Noyyal River and the Cauvery, turn into stones over time, which can still be seen today. Although it is a small river, it has the unique feature of having 32 dams that bring prosperity to West Kongu. Its water, the water of the Kanchi River, also known as the Noyyal River, is still known to cure many diseases. All its special features have been extensively praised by Kachiyappa Munivar Peruman in the Perur Puranam. Perur is the name of the town. It was a very large city in ancient times, as evidenced by historical records and old inscriptions. This holy place, Perur, is also known by many other names such as Melai Chidambaram, Aadhipuri, Piravaa Neri, etc. This holy place is praised by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in the Oorthogai Thirupathigam as "Perur Pattip Perumaan Piravaa Neriyane." Sekkizhar Peruman explains in the next verse that the Lord showed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal the dance of Thillai Ambalavanar (Lord Shiva of Chidambaram) here. The internal evidence from Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's words, "We received Perur Peruman in Thillai Chithambalam," confirms this. Therefore, this holy place is indeed Thillai Ambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman, mentioning this holy place, begins with the first word given by the Lord, "Ulagelaam." Therefore, this also confirms that it is Thillai Ambalam. We will continue our discussion of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's visit to Perur. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 14 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 852, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrives at the sacred place of Thirupperur. Sekkizhar Peruman continues to describe the darshan (vision) of Thirupperur.

The 89th verse: "Having reached that sacred place, he entered the temple of the Lord who ruled before, and surrounded by true devotees, he circumambulated it. As he stood before the Lord, the eternal One, He clearly revealed the extended form of His dance in the Thillai assembly hall. With hands clasped, and eyes overflowing with tears of joy, he beheld it."

Having reached Thirupperur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal entered the temple of the Lord who had previously ruled over him. Surrounded by true devotees, he circumambulated the temple and went before the Lord. At that moment, the Lord directly revealed to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal the extended form of His dance, as He performs it in the Thillai Ambalam (Chidambaram). He clearly showed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal the vision of the Thillai dance right there. Our Lord had previously shown the vision of Thiruppugazhi at Thiruvizhimizhalai to Pillaiyar (Thirugnana Sambandar), and the vision of Thiruchenkattankudi at Thirumarugal to Pillaiyar. Similarly, here at the sacred site of Thirupperur, the Lord clearly revealed the vision of the Thillai dance. This illustrates the principle that when one worships at a sacred place, one should behold and worship the Lord's divine form, including His sacred emblems, so that they are deeply imprinted in one's mind. It is the nature of the soul to see what the Lord reveals.

After this experience, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, emphasizing the vision he saw at Thirupperur over the one at Thillai, states that he "received the Lord of Perur in the Chithrambalam of Puliyur." As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal witnessed this dance, he was overcome with joy, and tears flowed like a torrent.

The 90th verse: "Upon seeing it, he bowed and fell, then immediately rose. Who can measure and describe the consequence of that state which befell the Shiva-like noble one, due to boundless love and the melting of bones?"

As mentioned before, immediately upon seeing the dance vision revealed by the Lord, he bowed, fell to the ground, and then rose. The boundless love, which knew no limits, caused even his bones to melt. With the supreme bliss, unknowable by the five senses, pervading him and filling him with true awareness, Sekkizhar Peruman states that it is impossible for anyone to comprehend or describe the profound joy that resulted from this experience for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the noble devotee, after he directly attained his Lord who performs the great dance of bliss. Each sense organ perceives its own specific objects. However, the bliss of Shiva is a supreme bliss that is beyond the reach of the five senses and the pleasures derived from them; it is a bliss that cannot be known by the five senses.

We can compare this verse with the verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman, "Let all five great senses become eyes," which he wrote after Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's darshan at Thillai.

Now, the 91st and 92nd verses are as follows: "The beloved one, having attained such a state of grace, rejoiced in the song that remained in the flood of joy, praising Him. He stayed in that prosperous town. 'If I have received the grace to worship the dancing foot (Sevadigal) that was lifted in the golden assembly, what more can I achieve by going elsewhere?' With this thought, he prepared to depart. Then, leaving that place, he traveled, crossing mountains and rivers, and many other lands. He worshipped many sacred places of the Supreme Being and praised Him with powerful Tamil verses. He worshipped Venjama Koodal and then, traveling a long distance, he arrived at Thirukkarkudi Malai in the south."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having had such a darshan at Thirupperur, became a beloved devotee filled with abundant grace. He joyfully sang praises, remaining in the flood of bliss, and stayed in that prosperous town. With the thought, "What more can I achieve by going elsewhere, if I have received the grace to worship the dancing foot of the Lord of Koothan (dancer), lifted in the beautiful golden Ambalam (hall)?" he prepared to depart from there. With this thought, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sang a Thiruppathigam (a set of devotional verses) and, having received the Lord's permission, set out. To be able to worship the Lord's dance is Shiva-Anandam (the bliss of Shiva). To be able to worship the Lord's dance is the most excellent means to attain liberation. Therefore, there is nothing to be gained by going elsewhere. He composed a Thiruppathigam expressing this sentiment, but unfortunately, due to our lack of merit, that Thiruppathigam is not available to us.

Thirupperur is one of the Thevara Vaippu Thalangal (temples mentioned in Thevaram but not sung in detail). Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has praised it in his "Oorthogai" (a collection of verses about various towns) as "Perur Uraivai Patti Peruman Pirava Neriyane" (Lord of Patti, residing in Perur, the path to freedom from rebirth). In Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's Thillai Thiruppathigam, the special grace shown by the Lord of Thirupperur, who revealed His dance to Nambigal (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) in the Thillai Ambalam, is praised. The Lord's name is Thiru Aanpatti Udaiyar. This name is also praised in Thirunavukkarasar Swamigal's Kaappu Thiruththadagam (a protective hymn). This place is near Coimbatore and is known by various names such as Adhipuri and Pirava Neri. The Kanchi Nadi (river), Brahma Theertham (sacred pond), and others are excellent sacred waters. The Kanchi Nadi is praised in Thevaram as "Anikanchi" (beautiful Kanchi). It is said that bones immersed in it turn into stone, a phenomenon still observable. The Brahma Theertham is known to cure all kinds of diseases and bring health, a quality still observable today. Naradar, Kalavar, Viswamithrar, Pattimuni, Gomuni, Kamadhenu, Indra, and others worshipped here and received blessings. Kings such as Musukundan, Karikal Chozhan, Kujathvajan, Kulasekharan, and Thriloka Chozhan have also worshipped here, as mentioned in the Sthala Puranam (local legendary history). The Sthala Puranam composed by Kaviraaksha Kachiappa Munivar is particularly renowned. The Kanakasabai (golden hall) built by Madurai Azhagathri Nayakkar is renowned for its excellent sculptural artistry.

Vidwan Kandasamy Mudaliar, the father of Sivakavimani Desikanar C.K. Subramania Mudaliar, the commentator of Periya Puranam, had great devotion to this place. He performed many renovation works and also composed several literary works such as "Thirupperur Killai Viduthuthu," "Perur Mummani Kovai," "Perur Pachai Nayaki Ammaiyar Oosal," "Maragathavalliyamman Malai," "Pottrikazhivennpa," and "Pachai Nayaki Ammaiyar Aasiriya Virutham." In addition, many other books are dedicated to this sacred place, including "Perur Patti Puranam" (also known as "Pirava Neri Puranam"), "Pachai Nayaki Ammai Pillai Tamil," "Arasampalavirutham," "Thirupperur Sigaiyaruthaan Vannam," "Kannadi Viduthuthu," "Vilakku Viduthuthu," "Maragatha Manjari," "Pattiswarar Kovai," "Pachai Nayaki Amman Panthadi," "Thirupperur Patti Vinayagar Ananda Kazhippu," "Marudhachala Pathi Thai Magal Easal," and "Uyir Varkkamalai," along with individual poems. All these highlight the special glories of Thirupperur.

Having worshipped at Thirupperur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after receiving the Lord's permission, crossed difficult mountain ranges, forests, and overflowing rivers, and worshipped many of the Lord's sacred places.
