Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, thinking, "Is all this for me? For this humble one?" and contemplating his immeasurable greatness and his own immeasurable smallness, his heart melted. He then began to sing the Thiru Pathigam, starting with "Muthuvaay Oori," praising the Lord. He sings about the Lord's coming with aching sacred feet, his coming in the harsh midday, his going to every house, and his offering food with kind words, saying, "You are very hungry." With the astonishment of a mind filled with the pride of realizing that the Lord had done this for his devotee, tears flowed like a waterfall, and his entire body was covered with goosebumps from the fresh water. He sang the Thiru Pathigam, praising and worshipping Lord Shiva, whose crown is adorned with honey-laden Konrai flowers.

The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 41st Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, dedicated to Thirukachoor Aalakkoyil. It is set to the Kollikkavvanam Pann (melody). The first, second, sixth, ninth, and tenth verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Muthuvaay oori kathara muthukaattu eri kondu aadal muyalvaane
Mathuvaar konrai puthuvi soodum malaiyaan magal tham manavaazhaa
Kathuvaay thalaiyil palini kolla kandaal adiyaar kavalare
Athuve aamaaru ithuvo kachoor Aalakkoyil Ammaane."

"Kachchu er aravu ondru araiyil asaiththu kazhalum silambum kalikka palikku endru uchcham podhaa oor oor thiriya Kandal adiyaar orukaare Ichchai ariyom engal perumaan ezhel pirappum ena aazhvaay Achchamillaa kachoor vadapaal Aalakkoyil Ammaane."

"Piravaay iravaay pennaay moovaay petram eri peysuzhthal Thuravaay maravaay sudakaadu endrum idamaka kondu nadamaadi Oruvaay thalaiyil pali nee kolla kandal adiyaar orukaare Arave ozhiyaay kachoor vadapaal Aalakkoyil Ammaane."

"Kaathal seythu kazhiththu pithatrithu kadimaamaliyitru unai eeththi Aathal seyyum adiyaarirkku aiyam kolvathu azhagithu Othakkanden unnai maraven umaiyaal kanava ena aazhvaay Aatharkazhani pazhana kachoor Aalakkoyil Ammaane."

"Annam mannum vayal suuzh kachoor Aalakkoyil Ammaanai Unna munnum manaththu aarura Aaruran per mudivaiththa Mannu pulavan vayal navaloorkon senchol navan vanthondan Pannu thamizh nool maalai vallaar avar en thalaimel payilvaare."
Thiruchitrambalam. Sivashiva.

Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings. The sacred place of Thirukachoor Aalakkoyil is located near Chennai, a short distance from Singaperumal Kovil on the road to Sriperumbudur, in Thyagarajapuram Thirukachoor. There, both Kachoor Aalakkoyil and the hill-base temple of Marundeeswarar Kovil are situated. The Lord of Kachoor Aalakkoyil is named Virunditta Eeswarar, Virunditta Varadar, Kachapeswarar. The Goddess is Anjanakshi Ammai. The Sthala Viruksham (sacred tree) is the Banyan tree. The Theertham (sacred water body) is Kurma Theertham. The hill-base temple is Marundeeswarar Kovil. The Lord is Marundeeswarar. The Goddess is Andhaka Nivaranai Ambal, Irul Neekki Thayar. This hill is named Marundhu Malai (Medicine Hill), Oushadhagiri. The Ganesha is Thalamoola Vinayagar. We saw that the hill where Marundeeswarar Kovil is located is Marundhu Malai. The Marundeeswarar Kovil is surrounded by natural beauty and a peaceful environment. Inside this temple, among the sculptures on the small mandapam (hall) pillars, there is a delightful sculpture of the Lord looking at Sundarar with food. The Moolavar (presiding deity) is a tall idol, and the Gomukham (outlet for sacred water) is turned. Opposite Brahma, who is a Koshta Murthy (niche deity), Chandeswarar is seen as Chathurmukha Chanda (four-faced Chanda). Near the flagstaff, there is a small pit of earth, which is called Marundhu (medicine). During the Masi festival, on the ninth day, the festival of the Lord begging for alms and offering food to Sundarar is celebrated. There is also a small temple for Iranditta Eeswarar. The Chithirai Perunthiruvizha (grand festival) is celebrated. The Thyagesar in Kachoor Aalakkoyil is named Amudha Thyagesar.

During the churning of the milk ocean, when Mount Mandara began to sink due to its weight, Vishnu took the form of a tortoise (Kachapa) and supported Mount Mandara from below to prevent it from sinking into the ocean. The Sthala Varalaru (temple history) states that Vishnu, in the form of a tortoise, came to this place and worshipped Lord Shiva to gain the power to bear the weight of the mountain. Since Mahavishnu worshipped Lord Shiva in the form of a tortoise, this sacred place was named Kachoor Thirukachoor. It is also called Adhi Kachapesam. The Kurma Theertham here is very special. Indra suffered from a disease due to a curse. The Ashwinidevas, the physicians of the heavens, could not cure his disease despite much treatment. Therefore, the Devas came to Earth to search for medicinal herbs to cure the disease. They searched in many places but could not find the medicine. They consulted Narada, who told them that if they worshipped Lord Shiva and Ambal, who reside on the Medicine Hill, they would receive medicine by their grace. Accordingly, the Ashwinidevas came here and worshipped the Lord, and Lord Shiva showed them the place where the medicine was. The Devas took two herbs, Bala and Athibala, and cured Indra's disease. Since the Lord gave medicine to cure the disease, he is called Marundeeswarar. Since Ambal spread light to carry the medicine, she was named Irul Neekki Ambal (Goddess who removes darkness). Appar Swamigal has also sung a Thiru Pathigam for this place, the 70th Thiru Pathigam of the 6th Thirumurai. Therefore, we saw that there are two Shiva temples in this place: one is Kachoor Aalakkoyil, and the second is Marundeeswarar Kovil at the foot of the hill. It is customary to first visit Kachoor Aalakkoyil and then Marundeeswarar Kovil. This is the 26th of the Padal Petra Sthalams (temples praised in hymns) in Thondai Nadu. This is referred to as "Nannilaththu Perungoyil Vai" and "Nigal Maadakkoyil" and "Por Pachoor Aalakkoyil." These are the historical details about this place. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 27 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 865, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Verse 183:

"Vandhiththu Iraivar arulaal poy mangai paagar magizhndha idam Munthi thondar ethirkolla pukku mukkan perumaanai Sindhiththida vandhu arul sai kazhil panindhu senchol thodai punainthe Anthichchekkar perugu oliyaar amarum kaanchi marungu anainthaar."

Having worshipped at Thirukachoor Aalakkoyil and obtained divine permission from the Lord, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal departs from there and visits other sacred places where the Lord, who shares his body with Uma, resides. Thus, he proceeds to Thirukanchi, also known as Thirukanchipuram. On his way from Thirukachoor to Thirukanchipuram, he visits sacred places along the banks of the Palar and Seyar rivers, such as Thirumukkoodal at the confluence, Thiruvillam, Thirumakaral, Thirukkuranganilmuttam, and others. He then reaches Thirukanchipuram. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows at the feet of the three-eyed Lord, who grants grace immediately upon thought, and sings Thiru Pathigams (hymns) as garlands of beautiful words. He then arrives near Thirukanchipuram, where Thiru Ekambavar, whose body shines with the abundant light of the evening sky, resides with pleasure.

Verses 184 and 185:

"Andru vennai nallooril ariyum ayanum thodarvariya Venri mazha velvidai uyarthaar veda muthalavaraay Vandhu nindru sabai mun vazhakku uraiththu nere Thodarndhu aatkondavar thaam indru engku eytha pettrom endru Eyil suuzh kaanchi nagar vaazhvaar Malku magizhchi migapperuga marugumani thoranam naatti Algu theepam nirai kudangal akilin thoopam kodiyeduththu Selva manaigal alankariththu petri aadal muzhavu athira Palgu thondarudan koodi pathiyin puram poy ethirkondar."

When the news reaches Thirukanchipuram that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has arrived near the city, the devotees of that city are filled with immense joy. They speak among themselves with happiness, saying that they have been greatly blessed by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's arrival. How do they speak? They say that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was taken as his own by the Lord. The Lord, who was difficult for Vishnu and Brahma to find, appeared as the leader of the Brahmins in Thiruvannainallur, argued his case before the assembly, and directly took Sundaramoorthy Swamigal as his own, based on a previous connection. The residents of Kanchipuram rejoice, saying that Nambigal, who was thus taken as his own by the Lord, has today come to Kanchipuram, and they have received a great blessing. This verse describes the inner joy of the residents of Kanchipuram. The next verse describes the actions they take due to that joy. As their joy overflows, they erect beautiful archways in the streets of Kanchipuram, light abundant lamps, place full pots, burn incense, raise flags, decorate their wealthy homes, and with the sound of many drums for dancing, they gather with many devotees and go outside Kanchipuram to receive Nambigal.

Verse 186:

"Aanda nambi ethirkonda adiyaarai vananga ethirvanangi Neenda mathil gopuram kadandhu nirai maalikai veethiyil poondhu Poonda kaathal vaazhththinudan punai mangala thooriyam olippa Eendu thondar perugu thiru ekambaram senru eythinaar."

Nambigal, who was taken as his own by the Lord, bowed to the devotees who came to receive him, and they in turn bowed to him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal tried to bow to the devotees, but before he could, the devotees bowed, making his bow a return bow. Thus, having returned the bow, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal crossed the long wall and gopuram (temple tower) of Kanchipuram and proceeded along the street filled with mansions. With great love and blessings, and the sound of auspicious musical instruments, he reached the sacred Ekambaram temple, where many devotees had gathered. Thiru Ekambaram is the temple where Lord Ekambar resides. It is called Thiru Ekambam. "Aambaram" means mango tree, and "Ekam" means unique or one. It is called Ekambaram. This name came about because of the sacred mango tree under which the Goddess performed penance inside this temple. "Ekambam" refers to the temple where the Lord resides.

Verse 187:

"Aazhi nedumaal ayan muthalaam amarar nerungku gopuram mun Poothiyura manmisai meni porundha vanangi pugundhu aruli Suuzhu mani maalikai palavum thozhuthu vanangi valam kondu Vaazhi mani pon koyilin ul vanukka van thondar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal approaches the temple. Before the gopuram, where Vishnu with his discus and Brahma, along with other celestials, gather, he prostrates himself on the ground, his body touching the dust, and enters. He worships and circumambulates the many beautiful mansions surrounding the temple. Then, Van Thondar, who is a close devotee, enters the golden temple of Thiru Ekambar, which is a glorious jewel.

Verse 188:

"Kaigal kooppi mun anaiyaar kambaiyaaru perugivara Aiyarthamakku miga anji aara thazhuvikkondirundha Maiyulaavum karum nedum kan malaiyaal endrum vazhipadum Poojaiyya kamalachchevadi keezh thirundhu kaathaludan veezhnthaar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with his hands clasped above his head, proceeds to the presence of the Lord. He goes to the Lord's presence. The Goddess, with dark, long, kohl-lined eyes, who, seeing the Kamba river overflowing, was greatly afraid for the Lord's sacred body and embraced him fully, and who always worships, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal falls with great love at the sacred lotus-like feet, which are worshipped by her. When the Kamba river overflowed, the Goddess feared that the Lord's sacred body would be damaged by the flood. She embraced the Lord, who is such a great Lingam. The Goddess in Kanchipuram always performs Shiva Puja. As Lord Shiva had blessed, "Your worship never ends with us," the Goddess performs eternal worship. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal bows before the Lord's feet, which are worshipped by the Goddess.

Verses 189 and 190:

"Vizhundhu pottri paravasamaay vimmi ezhundhu mey anbaal Vaazhndhu sinthai udan paadimaaraa viruppil puram poondhu Suuzhndha thondarudan maruvum naalil thollai kachchinagar Thazhndha sadaiyaar aalayangal palavum saarndhu vananguvaar Seeraar kaanchi mannum thiru kaamakkottam senru irainji Neeraar sadaiyaar amarndharulum needu thirumeetrali mevi Aara anbil panindhu eththum alavil onchudaraam Aaraar perumai thiruppathikam paadi magizhndhu paravinaar."

As mentioned before, he prostrates himself on the ground, praises, becomes ecstatic, sobs, rises, and with true love and a fulfilled mind, he sings. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, when he fell at the sacred feet, thinking that they were the feet always worshipped by the Goddess, sobbed with ecstasy, contemplating that divine grace. After that, he rose and sang. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal had no desire to leave the temple. With that unwavering desire and undiminished love, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes out of the temple with the devotees and stays in Kanchipuram. While staying there, he visits and worships many ancient Shiva temples in Kanchipuram with the devotees. We know that there are countless Shiva temples in Kanchi. He goes to the glorious Thirukamakottam, which is established in Kanchi, and worships there.

Kachi Thirukamakottam is the temple where Goddess Kamakshi resides. This is the only Goddess temple for all the temples within the boundaries of Kanchipuram. Since the Lord resides there as the goal of the Goddess's penance, it is also a temple of the Lord, as worshipped by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships at Kachi Thirukamakottam and then reaches Kachi Thirumettali. Kachi Thirumettali is located to the west of Thiru Ekambam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings Thiru Pathigams (hymns) beginning with "Thiru Ekambathin Merku Thisaiyil Irukkinrar" (He is to the west of Thiru Ekambam). This Thiru Pathigam itself is a brilliant, imperishable flame of knowledge. This is its implication. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 21st Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, dedicated to Thirukachi Mettali. It is set to the Nattaragam Pann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Nondhaa onchudare nunaiya ninainthirundhen Vanthaay poy ariyaay maname pugundhu nindra Sinthaay enthai piraan thirumeetroli uraiyum enthaay Unnai allal ini eththa maattene."

"Paaroor pallavan oor mathil kaanchimaa nagarvaay Seeroorum puravil thirumeetroli sivanai Aaruran adiyaan adiththondan Aaruran sonna Seeroor paadalvallaar sivalogam servaare."
Thiruchitrambalam.

"Nondhaa onchudare, enthaay, ere, aiyaa, malaiye, unnai allal ini eththa maatten. Unaiye ninainthirundhen. Ninaithalum ivvoonudalam pugunthaay, poy ariyaay. Ninaiyaathu irundhaalum veraa vandhu en ullam pugavalla meypporul. Adiyaarkkuth thondu pattu ini piravaamai kettu ozhindhen. Nun adaindhen. Ettraalen kurai en idarai thurandhozhindhen. Nee maalaayina theerththarul seyyum meypporul. Ninnai ninaithalume nin ninaiya paniththaay. Unnai allal udalil uyir ullalavum naavadanaal veru uraiyen."

Thus, this Thiru Pathigam has the greatness of guiding the people of the world with divine teachings, weighing the nature of remembering the Lord, His grace, and its benefits. Because of this special quality, Sekkizhar Peruman praises this Thiru Pathigam as "Paaraar Perumai Thiru Pathigam" (the great Thiru Pathigam praised by the world). We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 28 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 866, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Having worshipped at Kachi Mettali, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Onakaandhan Thali and worships the Lord. Verse 191:

"Onakaandhan thali mevum oruvar thammai urimaiyudan pezhuni amaindha Thozhamaiyaal perugum adimai thirampesi kaanamodu pon vendi neyyum paalum kalaivilangum yaanpathikam eduththu etti ennil nithi pettru inithu irundhaar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal praises Lord Shiva, who resides in the sacred temple of Thiru Onakaandhan Thali, with a sense of intimate friendship that he willingly embraced. We saw in the Puranam of Thaduththaatkonda that the Lord himself blessed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, saying, "We have given ourselves to you as a friend." Thus, even though he speaks of the growing devotion of being a servant to the Lord, his Thiru Pathigams (hymns) are structured with a sense of friendship. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings a beautiful Thiru Pathigam, beginning with "Neyyum Paalum" (ghee and milk), which reveals the nature of the arts, desiring gold. The Lord then bestows countless treasures upon him. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 5th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, dedicated to Thiru Onakaandhan Thali. It is set to the Indhalapann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Neyyum paalum thayirum kondu nitthal poosanai seyyaluttraar Kaiyil ondrum kaanam illai azhiladi thozhuthu uyin allal Aivar kondu ingku aatta aadi aazhkuzhippattu azhundhuvenukku Uyyumaaru ondru aruliccheyyar Onakaandhan thali ulire."

"Ovvanam mel eruthu ondru erum Onakaandhan thali vulaartham Aavanam seythu aalum kondu arai thukilodu pattu veekki Kovanam merkonnda vedam kovaiyaaka Aaruran sonna Paavana thamizh patthum vallaarkku paraiyum thaam seytha paavam thaane."
Thiruchitrambalam.

Thiru Onakaandhan Thali is the third Padal Petra Sthalam (temple praised in hymns) in Thondai Nadu. It got its name because the Asuras Onan and Kandan worshipped the Lord here. There are two separate shrines with separate circumambulatory paths within the same temple. The Lord is Oneswarar and Kantheeswarar. The Goddess is Kamakshi Ammai. In this temple, the deity worshipped by Salandharan is in a separate temple to the south. It is believed in the temple history that after Nambigal sang this Thiru Pathigam here, gold fell from the mango tree in front of the shrine.

Now, verses 192 and 193:

"Angan amarvaar anekathangavathaththai eythi ul anaindhu Sengkan vidayaarthamai panindhu then neyppurindhu endru eduththa thamizh Thangku idamaam ena paadi thaazhndhu piravum thaanangal Pongku kaathaludan pottri purindha pathiyil porundhu naal Paadal isaiyum paniyinaal paavai thazhuva kuzhai kampar Aadal maruvum sevadiyaarai nanninaaru pol ul niraindhu Thidamaam karuthil thiruppathikam paadi kaathal sirandhu irundhaar Needha muthoor puraththu iraivar nilavum pathigal thozha viruppaal Maada nerungku vanpaarththan panankaattooril vandhu adainthaar."

From Thiru Onakaandhan Thali, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Anekathangavatham and worships. He enters the temple in Thirukachi Anekathangavatham, worships the Lord, and sings the Thiru Pathigam beginning with "Then Neyppurindhu" (honey and ghee). In it, he sings the Thiru Pathigam, implying that this is the dwelling place of the Lord, and worships the Lord. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thirukachi Anekathangavatham is the 10th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the Indhalapann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Senjadai emperumaanathu idam aingkanai akkone eriththu eriyaadi kulavaanathu idam kuraiyaa maraiyaa maanai idaththu or kaiyan idam matham maarupada pozhiyum malai pol aanai oriththa piranaathu idam kalikachchi anekathangavathame."

"Veedu pera pala oozhigal nindru ninaikkum idam vinai theerum idam Peedu pera periyaaridangkondu mevinar thangalai kaakkum idam Paadum idaththu adiyaan pugazh oorun uraiththa immaalaigal patthum vallaar Koodum idam sivalogan idam kalik kachchi anekathangavathame."
Thiruchitrambalam.

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having been blessed with the service of singing, resides in Kanchipuram, where the Lord Ekambathar, whom the Goddess embraced, stands softened. He worships the dancing sacred feet and stays there with devotion. Then, with a desire to worship all the sacred places where the Lord resides in that ancient city of Kachi, he goes and worships them. We know that there are countless Shiva temples in Kanchi, such as Kanchi Thanam, Iravai Thanam, and others. Having worshipped all these, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with continuous remembrance, reaches Thiruvanpaarthan Panangaattoor.

Verses 194 and 195:

"Selvamalku thiruppanankaattooril semponchelunchudarai Allal arukkum arumarundhai vanangi anbu pozhi kannneer Malku nindru vidaiyinmel varuvaarai enum vandhamizh pathikam Nalla isaiyudan paadi poondhu purambu nannuvaar Mannu thirumaal per anaindhu vanangi paravi thiruvallam Thannul eythi irainji poy saarum melpaal sadaikkattrai Pinnan mudiyar idam palavum peni vanangi perunthondar Senni mukil thoy thadankuvattu thirukkaalaththimalai sernthaar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships the golden, bright flame, who is like a rare medicine that removes the sorrows of birth, residing in the wealthy Thiruppanangaattoor. He worships with tears of love flowing, and sings the rich Tamil Thiru Pathigam, beginning with "Vidaiyinmel varuvaanai" (He who comes on the bull), with good melody. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal then proceeds from there. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Thiruvanpaarthan Panangaattoor is the 86th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the Seekamarappann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Vidaiyin mel varuvaanai vedaththin porulaanai Adaiyil anbu udaiyaanai yaavarkkum arivonna Madaiyil vaazhaigal paayum vanpaarththan panankaattoor Gangai thariththaanai saaraathaar saarbu enna."

"Paaroorum panankaattoor pavalaththin padiyaanai Seeroorum thiruvaar sivan per sennaiyil vaiththa Aaruran adiththondan adiyan sol adinaay sol Ooruran urai seyvaar uyarvaanaththu uyarvaare."
Thiruchitrambalam.

Thiruvanpaarthan Panangaattoor, also known as Thiruppanangaadu, is the ninth Padal Petra Sthalam (temple praised in hymns) in Thondai Nadu. It got this name because it is next to the village of Vanpakkam and the palm tree is its Sthala Viruksham (sacred tree). It is a place where two great Rishis, Agastya and Pulastya, worshipped. There are two shrines for them. The Lord worshipped by Agastya is Thazhpuriswarar, Panangaattunathar. The Goddess is Amirthavalli. To its northeast, the Lord worshipped by Pulastya is Kirupanatheswarar. The Goddess is Kirupanayaki. The tree is the palm tree. The Theertham (sacred water body) is Jattagangai Theertham. From Thiru Ekambam in Thirukachi, one can reach this place by going a short distance south to Aiyan Kulam, then turning west on the road to Arcot. One can reach it by crossing the Palar and Vegavathi rivers. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having sung the Thiru Pathigam, reaches Thirumalperu. There, he worships the Lord and sings a Thiru Pathigam.

From there, he obtains divine permission from the Lord and reaches Thiruvallam, where he worships. Then, he lovingly worships many places where the Lord resides to the west. These are Thirukkarapuram and other places mentioned here. Although Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visited Thirumalperu, Thiruvallam, and Thirukkarapuram and sang Thiru Pathigams, due to our lack of merit, those Thiru Pathigams are not available to us. Thus, having gone west from Thiruvallam and worshipped many sacred places, he then travels a long distance and goes northeast directly to Thirukalathi Malai. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman and Thirunavukkarasu Swamigal visited Thirukkarikkara and then Thirukalathi Malai. That path is different. The path Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is now taking to Thirukalathi Malai is different. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the great devotee, reached Thirukalathi Malai, which has large peaks touching the clouds. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 29 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 867, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Thirukalathi Malai. Verse 196:

"Thadukkalakaa perungkaathal thalai nindru arulum kannappar Idukkan kalaindhu aatkondarulum iraivar magizhndha kaalaththi Adukkal sera anaindhu panindhu arulaal eri anbaarum Aduppaththirumun senru eythi malaimel marundhai vananginaar."

The love of Thirukannappadevanayanar, that great love that cannot be restrained, was paramount. Kannappadevanayanar stood supreme in that unrestrainable great love. His love was like a great flood breaking a dam, completely immersed in the Lord, where the body, inner and outer senses, worldly attachments, and everything else became powerless and disappeared. Such was the love of Thirukannappadevanayanar. What kind of love was that Kannappadevanayanar's love? It was the distress he felt when he saw blood flowing from the Lord's eyes, the wound. It was the intense desire to remove that distress, that trouble, that had come upon the Lord. The place where Lord Shiva, to whom Kannappadevanayanar showed such love, resides is Thirukalathi Malai. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes and reaches that sacred hill, prostrates himself on the ground, obtains divine grace, and with love overflowing like a river, he goes to the presence of the Lord and worships the Lord, who is like medicine on the mountain.

Verses 197 and 198:

"Vanangi ulla kazhikoora magizhndhu pottri madhura isai Anangu chendaadu enum pathikam paadi anbaal kannappar Manam kol malarchchevadi panindhu vaazhndhu poondhu mannum pathi Inangu thondarudan kezhumi inbuttri irukkum annaalil Vadamaathiraththu paruppathamum thirukkedara malaiyum muthal idama Aranaartham uvandha ellam ingkeyirundhu irainji nadamaadiya Sevadiyaarai nanninaaru pol ul niraindhu thidamaam karuthil thiruppathikam paadi kaathal sirandhu irundhaar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships Lord Kalathi, and with his heart overflowing with joy, he praises and sings the Thiru Pathigam, beginning with "Anangu Chendaadu" (the dancing doll), with sweet music. With love, he bows at the lotus-like sacred feet of Kannappar, which are cherished in his heart, and lives a blessed life. He then comes out and stays for some time in that sacred place with the accompanying devotees, enjoying himself. During that time, he worships all the sacred places where Lord Shiva resides, such as the Sri Parvatham (Sri Sailam) in the northern region and Thirukedara Malai (Kedarnath), as if he were seeing and worshipping the Lord's dancing sacred feet directly from Thirukalathi. With unwavering resolve, he sings Thiru Pathigams for all these places and remains filled with great love. Earlier, we saw that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman also worshipped Thirukannappadevanayanar and rejoiced in a similar manner. Similarly, here, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also worships Thirukannappadevanayanar and, remembering the great life he received thereby, praises the Lord and sings the Thiru Pathigam beginning with "Chendaadu." That Thiru Pathigam is dedicated to Thirukalathi Thirumalai. It is the 26th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the Nattaragam Pann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Chendaadum sivane en chezhunjudare Vandhaarum kuzhalaal umai paagam magizhndhavanai Kandaar kaathalikkum gananaathan em kaalaththiyaay Anda unai allal arindhu eththa maattene."

"Kaaroorum pozhil suuzh gananaathan em kaalaththiyul Aaraa innamuthai ani naval Aaruran sonna Seeroor senthamizhkall seppuvaar vinai aayina poy Peraa vinnulagam peruvaar pizhaippu ondru ilare."
Thiruchitrambalam.

Thus, with love, he bows at the lotus-like sacred feet of Thirukannappadevar, which are imbued with sacredness, and attains a blessed life. He then comes out of the temple and stays for some time in Thirukalathi Thirupathi with the devotees, filled with love. While there, he worships all the sacred places where Lord Shiva resides, such as Sri Sailam (Sri Parvatham) in the northern direction, from Thirukalathi itself. As if seeing and worshipping the Lord's dancing sacred feet directly, he sings Thiru Pathigams for all these places with that resolve, while staying in Thirukalathi. Among the Thiru Pathigams sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, Thiru Pathigams for Sri Sailam (Sri Parvatham) and Thirukedaram (Kedarnath) are available. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for Sri Sailam is the 79th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai. It is set to the Nattappadai Pann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Maanum maraiyinamum mayilnamum kalandhu iyangum Thaame miga meyndhu thadanjunai neerkalai parugi Poomaa maram urinji pozhilvoodu senru pukku Theemaam pozhil neezhal seeparpathamalaiye."

"Nallaar avar palaar vaazhththaru vayal navalvooran Sellalvoora ariya sivan seeparpathamalaiyai Allal avaiththeera chonna thamizh maalaigal vallaar Ollai sela uyar vaanakam aanndu angku iruppaare."
Thiruchitrambalam.

Sri Sailam, a sacred place, is rich in natural resources and is difficult for humans to access. It is a place where Shiva resides, known as Sri Parvatham. In this Thiru Pathigam, the beauty of Sri Sailam is praised by describing the activities of deer, peacocks, elephants, parrots, and other animals that live there. This Thiru Pathigam by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal implies that for most of the year, Sri Sailam is a habitat for these animals, without human presence. Now, the Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal from Thirukalathi Malai, worshipping Thirukedaram, is the 78th Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, set to the Natapadai Pann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Vaazhvaavathu maayam ithu mannaavathu thinnam Paazh povathu piravik kadal pasi noy seytha parithaan Thaazhaathu aram seymin thadankannaan malaronum Keezh mel ura nindran thirukkedaram enire."

"Naavinmisai araiyanodu thamizh gnana sambandhan Yaavar sivan adiyaarkalukku adiyaan adiththondan Devan thirukkedaraththai oorun urai seytha Paavin thamizh vallaar paralokaththu iruppaare."
Thiruchitrambalam.

This Thiru Pathigam is an instruction by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal on the truth of impermanence. This worldly life is an illusion; it appears to exist but is destined to perish one day. Even within this short life, there are many sorrows. Therefore, before death comes, go to the Lord's Thirukedaram or speak of Thirukedaram. Although it is very difficult to go and worship directly at Thirukedaram, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, out of compassion for the living beings of the world, guides them by saying, "Praise with your mouth." Thus, this Thiru Pathigam is structured. Therefore, these two Thiru Pathigams for Sri Sailam and Thirukedaram were sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal while he was in Thirukalathi. Similarly, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visited and sang for many other northern sacred places, such as Thiru Indraneela Parpatham, Thiru Anekathangavatham, and Thirukailayam (Thiru Nodiththanmalai). However, due to our lack of merit, those Thiru Pathigams are not available to us. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having resided in Thirukalathi Malai, then obtains divine permission from the Lord and departs from there. Verse 199:

"Angku sila naal vaippin arulaal poondhu poruvidaiyaar Thangum idangal ena palavum saarndhu thaazhndhu thamizhpaadi Pongu punarikkaraimarungu puviyul sivalogam pola Thingal mudiyathu amarndha thiru ottriyurai senru adainthaar."

After staying for some days in Thirukalathi, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal obtains divine permission from the Lord and proceeds to other sacred places where Lord Shiva resides. He worships them and sings Tamil Thiru Pathigams. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal departs from Thirukalathi after obtaining divine permission from the Lord. The Lord in Thirukailayam had blessed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who was Alalasundarar, saying, "Go to the southern land." Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal incarnates to bless the southern direction, which has performed great penance. There, in the sacred garden of Thirukailayam, Alalasundarar, who was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, was there. The two female companions of Uma Devi, Kamalini and Anindithai, were picking flowers in the garden. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind was drawn to these two women due to divine grace. Therefore, by the Lord's command, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal incarnates in the southern direction. The female companions of the Goddess also incarnate accordingly. Among them, Kamalini incarnates as Paravai Nachiyar in Thiruvarur and grows up. We have contemplated the marriage of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Paravai Nachiyar and their history in the Puranam of Thaduththaatkonda. Now, Anindithai Ammaiyar, another female companion of the Goddess, incarnates as Sangili Nachiyar in Gnayiru, near Thiruvottriyur, and grows up. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having married one of the Goddess's female companions, now proceeds towards the other, due to divine grace. Divine grace within Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prompts him, and he embarks on a pilgrimage from Thirukalathi Malai towards Thiruvottriyur. Therefore, departing from Thirukalathi, he visits Thirukkarikkara, Thirukallil Punniyakotteeswarar, and other sacred places, singing Tamil Thiru Pathigams. The Thiru Pathigams sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for these places are not available to us due to our lack of merit. Thus, having visited these sacred places, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal reaches Thiruvottriyur, a sacred place by the seashore, which is like Sivalokam (the abode of Shiva) on this earth. Just as the Lord resides in Thiruvarur in the form of a anthill, here too, the Lord resides in Thiruvottriyur in the form of a anthill. The Lord Thyagesa in Thiruvottriyur is very special, embodying the greatness mentioned in the Thevaram (hymns). Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman praises it as "Puviyul Sivalogam" (Sivalokam on earth). Now, the events related to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's story, who came here from Thirukailayam, will unfold before the Lord in Thiruvottriyur temple, after the parts related to Kamalini's appearance at the entrance of Thiruvarur temple. Therefore, the historical note that the Lord resides here with pleasure, just as he resided in Thirukailayam, is also found here. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Thiruvottriyur. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 30 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 868, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeds to Thiruvottriyur. Verses 200 and 201:

"Annal thodarndhu aavanam kaatti aannda nambi ezhuntharula Ennil perumai aadhipuri iraivar adiyaar ethirkolvaar Vannaveethi vaayil thorum vaazhai kamugu thoranangal Sunna nirai ponkudam thoopa theepam eduththu thozha ezhungaal Varamangala nalliyam muzhanga vaasamaalai ani arangil Pura mangaiyargal nadamaada pozhiyum vella poomaari aramangaiyarum amarargalum veesa anbarudan pugundhaar Piraman thalaiyil paliyugandha piranaar viru."

The devotees of the Lord in Thiruvottriyur, known as Adhipuri, hear the news that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the great devotee whom the Lord himself followed, showing the document and taking him as his own, is arriving at Thiruvottriyur. Upon hearing the name Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, what comes to the minds of the devotees is that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the one whom Lord Shiva himself came and took as his own, showing the document and continuously following him with the palm leaf. The devotees of the Lord in Thiruvottriyur, which is Adhipuri of immeasurable greatness, go to receive Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. In every beautiful street and at every entrance, they erect banana trees, areca nut trees, and archways. They carry auspicious pots filled with gold, incense, and lamps, and go forth to worship. Auspicious musical instruments play. In the dancing halls of that city, dancing girls perform. The celestials shower a flood of flowers from the sky. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the great devotee beloved by Lord Shiva, who accepts alms in Brahma's skull, enters the city of Thiruvottriyur with his accompanying devotees.

Verse 202:

"Ottriyurin umaiyodum kooda nindraar uyar thavaththin Pattrumikka thiruththondar parandha kadal pol vandhu eendi Sutram anaindhu thuthiseyya thozhuthu thambiraan anbar Kottramazhaverudaiyavar tham koyil vaayil eythinaar."

Lord Shiva, who stands united with Uma in Thiruvottriyur, is worshipped by the devotees who are deeply devoted to his great penance. These devotees gather like a vast ocean, surrounding and praising him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the beloved devotee of the Lord, bows to the assembly of devotees and reaches the entrance of the temple of the Lord, who has a victorious bull.

Verse 203:

"Vaanai alakkum gopuraththai magizhndhu panindhu pugundhu valar Koonal ilam venpirai chchadaiyaar koyil valam kondu ethir kurugi Oonum uyirum karaindhu uruga uchchi kuviththa kaiyinudan Aana kaathaludan veezhnthaar aaraa anbin aarurar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal joyfully bows down to the towering gopuram (temple tower) that seems to measure the sky, and enters. He circumambulates the temple of the Lord, whose matted hair is adorned with a crescent moon, and approaches the Lord's presence. With his flesh and soul melting, and his hands clasped above his head, Aarurar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), with overflowing love, prostrates himself on the ground.

Verse 204:

"Ettu variyil ottri nagar neengal ennum ezhuththariyum Naattamalarum thirunuthalaar narum ponkamala chchevadiyil Kootum unarvu kondu ezhundhu kothil amudha isai kooda Paattum paadipparavi enum pathikam eduththu paadinaar."

The Lord of Thiruvottriyur is renowned as the Lord who knows writing, "Ottriyur nagar neengal" (you are not from Ottriyur). This name came to the Lord based on an ancient historical event associated with this place. This was the place where the Lord performed that divine and miraculous event. We have previously contemplated this in detail in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu. It is a divine history connected with the temple's history. Sekkizhar Peruman also describes that divine and miraculous event here, singing about the Lord of Thiruvottriyur as "Ottriyur nagar neengal ennum ezhuththariyum naattamalarum thirunuthalaar" (the Lord with the sacred forehead that blooms with knowledge, who knows the writing "you are not from Ottriyur"). Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with a sense of union at the sacred feet of such a Lord of Thiruvottriyur, rises and sings the Thiru Pathigam, beginning with "Paattum Paadipparavi" (singing and praising), with flawless, nectar-like music. The Thiru Pathigam sung by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is the 91st Thiru Pathigam of the 7th Thirumurai, dedicated to Thiruvottriyur. It is set to the Kurinjipann (melody). The first and last verses of this Thiru Pathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"Paattum paadipparavi thirivaar eettum vinaigal theerppaar koyil Kaattum kalamum thimilum karaikke Ottum thiraivaay Ottriyurai."

"Ottriyurum aravum pizhaiyum pattri ooram pavazhachchadaiyaan Ottriyur mel oorun uraiththa kattru paada kazhiyum vinaiye."
Thiruchitrambalam.

The Lord of Thiruvottriyur removes sins. "Paadi paravi thiruvaar eettum vinaigal theerppaar" (He removes the sins accumulated by those who sing and praise). "Thannai munnam ninaikka thannaiye tharuvaar" (He gives Himself to those who think of Him first). We see Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's earlier historical note in this Thiru Pathigam. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sings the Thiru Pathigam. Verse 205:

"Paadi arivu paravasamaam parivu pattra purampoondhu Needu virippil perungkaathal niraindha anbar palavottra Thedum ayanum thirumaalum aritharkku ariya thiruppaadham Koodum kaalangalil anaindhu paravi kumbittu inithu irundhaar."

Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having sung the Thiru Pathigam, with his intellect becoming ecstatic, with that great love that caused his intellect to lose control and become absorbed in divine bliss, with that love overflowing, comes out of the temple. Many devotees, filled with great love and devotion, praise him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal approaches and worships the sacred feet of the Lord, who is difficult for Brahma and Vishnu to find, and happily resides there. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visits the temple at all times. According to the rules prescribed in the Agamas (scriptural texts) for worship times, he participates in all the daily worship services, worships the Lord of Thiruvottriyur, sings praises, and happily resides in the sacred place of Thiruvottriyur. While he is thus residing, Sekkizhar Peruman is now about to sing the sacred history of Sangili Nachiyar. For this, Sekkizhar Peruman now sets the stage. Sangili Nachiyar, with that sacred name, is Anindithai Ammaiyar, one of the female companions of Uma Devi in Thirukailaya Malai, who incarnated on Earth to marry Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. We saw in the Puranam of Thaduththaatkonda that in the sacred garden of Thirukailayam, two female companions of Uma Devi, Kamalini and Anindithai, were picking flowers. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with the sacred name Alalasundarar, came to that garden. Divine grace prompted him, and his mind was drawn to those two women. Therefore, by the Lord's command, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal incarnates in the southern direction. The female companions of the Goddess also incarnate accordingly. Among them, Kamalini incarnated as Paravai Nachiyar in Thiruvarur and grew up. We have seen the marriage of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Paravai Nachiyar and their history. Now, Anindithai Ammaiyar, another female companion of the Goddess, incarnates as Sangili Nachiyar in Gnayiru, near Thiruvottriyur, and grows up. Now, the event of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal meeting Sangili Nachiyar in Thiruvottriyur and the subsequent historical events will be sung by Sekkizhar Peruman. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 31 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 869, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. The sacred history of Sangili Ammaiyar's marriage with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in Thiruvottiyur. Sekkizhar Peruman begins to sing this sacred history. This is the 206th verse. This verse is in Sekkizhar Peruman's own words. Sekkizhar Peruman states, "We shall now narrate how this person, who was in this state, left Mount Kailash for him by grace, and how Aninditaiyar came and was born on this earth, grew up, and then joined the shoulders of Vanthondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) adorned with fragrant garlands."

Sekkizhar Peruman states that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has arrived at Thiruvottiyur and is residing there. Even before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal came to Thiruvottiyur, a long time ago, our Goddess, who was known by the sacred name Aninditai Ammaiyar in Thirukkailayam (Mount Kailash), came to the southern direction by the divine command of the Lord, in order to marry Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. She has now incarnated on this earthly plane, grown up, and is about to marry Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Sekkizhar Peruman begins to narrate this history for everyone to know. Up until now, our minds have traveled with the events of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred life.

At this moment, Sekkizhar Peruman prepares us to move from one history to another by indicating that the sacred history of Sangiliyar is now beginning. Accordingly, he sets the stage for this verse as his own statement. The Goddess, who was known by the sacred name Aninditaiyar in Thirukkailayam, is Aninditai, meaning one who is without blemish. By the divine command of the Lord, she separated from Thirukkailayam and incarnated on this earthly plane, which is the land of karma, and is growing up. The 207th verse states: "She, Aninditaiyar of old, by the grace of the Lord with the dark throat, incarnated in the fourth lineage, which is the lineage of the Vellalars, who are known for their abundant goodness, and who live like the sun."

Near Thiruvottiyur, there is a place called Gnayiru. This place is located north-west of Minjur, on the way from Chennai to Thiruvottiyur, after crossing the Kusasthalai River. This is the glorious place where Sangili Nachiyar incarnated and grew up. There is a very ancient Shiva temple here, which was renovated during the Chola period. The Lord here is Bhootheeswarar (also known as Pushparatheeswarar), and the Goddess is Swarnambikai. Between the temples of the Lord and the Goddess, there is a shrine for Karparaneeswarar. There are also three other shrines for Hiranyeswarar, Kapaleeswarar, and Jambukeswarar. The local legend states that a Chola king, who was on a pilgrimage to Kasi and came this way, tried to pluck a beautiful lotus flower that had bloomed alone in the middle of a pond, and his eyes became blind. When he prayed that he would renovate this temple upon his return from the pilgrimage, he regained half of his eyesight. When he returned, the Lord appeared with a great light like the sun on that lotus. After he built the sanctum sanctorum at that spot and renovated the temple, he regained his full eyesight. Therefore, this place was named Gnayiru (Sun).

The oral tradition states that this place was named Gnayiru because the Lord appeared with a great light like the sun on the lotus in the pond. Aninditaiyar, one of the two female companions (Thozhiyar Pengal) of Uma Ammaiyar in Thirukkailayam, came to this world by the divine command of Shiva for the sake of Nambi Arorar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal). She incarnated as the daughter of Gnayiru Kizhar in this place called Gnayiru and is growing up. Sangili Nachiyar, after growing up here, went to Thiruvottiyur, stayed in a maiden's chamber (Kannimadam), performed floral services (Pushpa Thiruppani), and married Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. We will see this history in great detail in the Puranam. The house where Sangili Nachiyar incarnated and grew up is known as Gnayiru Kizhar Manai (Gnayiru Kizhar's house). It is located northeast of the Hiranyeswarar temple with appropriate identifying marks. It is known that the descendants of that lineage lived in that house until the time of Murugesa Mudaliar, and then recently sold it to a Muslim and moved to Chennai.

Sivakavimani Desikanar has written a note stating that this sacred house should be reclaimed and protected as a sacred monastery (Thirumadam) for Shaivites to worship, as this is a great duty and a supreme sacred act for the Shaivite people. He asks if the Shaivite world will do this. In Thiruvarur, Paravai Nachiyar's sacred house is a separate temple. In Thiruppoonduruthi, there is a sacred monastery built by Thirunavukkarasu Perumanar himself. In Thirunallur, there is a sacred monastery built by Amarnithi Nayanar, where he attained blessings. In Sirkazhi, there is the sacred house where Thirugnanasambandha Peruman incarnated. All these are priceless treasures for the Shaivite community. Similarly, this sacred mansion where our Sangili Nachiyar incarnated should also shine in the same way. It should be protected as a sacred monastery where Shaivites worship. Protecting and worshipping these is a great duty of Shaivites. Sivakavimani Desikanar also prays that divine grace should facilitate this. In such a place called Gnayiru, the fourth lineage, the Vellalar lineage, is classified in ancient Tamil texts. The classified lineage is the Vellalar lineage.

Among the many people, from the Brahmins to the Vellalars, who lived as good citizens with excellent conduct in such a lineage, in the fourth lineage, the Vellalar lineage, in that place called Gnayiru, there lived a great person named Gnayiru Kizhavar, who excelled in the characteristics of the Vellalars. Kizhavar is a special traditional name for Vellalars. It is also pronounced as Kizhar. Kizhamai means right or ownership. Those who have ownership are called Vellalars, meaning owners of the land. Therefore, it is customary to call them Kizhavar or Kizhar. This is their traditional name. Since he was a prominent and important person in the sacred place of Thirugnayiru, he was called Gnayiru Kizhar after the name of the village. We see names like Sengundrur Kizhar, Kovur Kizhar, and Kundaiyur Kizhar. Similarly, in this village of Gnayiru, he was prominent and well-known to everyone, so he was called Gnayiru Kizhar.

His sacred daughter who incarnated was Sangili Nachiyar. She was formerly known as Aninditaiyar in Thirukkailayam. Now, she incarnates here as Sangili Nachiyar, the sacred daughter of Gnayiru Kizhar. The three continuous verses, 208, 209, and 210, are as follows: "She who came with unforgetting love for the lotus feet of the daughter of the mountain (Uma), and whose consciousness was elevated, was Sangiliyar, who knew herself. She played with other young girls with wavy, spear-like eyes, and passed through all the stages of childhood. Not only did she excel in the conduct of her noble lineage, but she also possessed divine qualities that astonished everyone on earth. As her beautiful breasts grew, causing her waist to ache, her father said to his wife: 'Our daughter's form and qualities are not merely those of ordinary mortals, but are of a higher, divine nature. There is nothing comparable to her. The time for her marriage is approaching.' His wife, who was like a creeper of chastity, replied, 'I accept what you say.'"

The daughter of the mountain king (Uma Ammaiyar) is one whose love for the lotus-like feet is unforgettable, and who is foremost in that discipline. Aninditai, in her previous state, served Uma Ammaiyar in Thirukkailayam. With that consciousness, the Goddess incarnates in this world. She incarnates with the sacred name Sangili Nachiyar. Such an incarnation with prior consciousness occurs only by the grace of Shiva. We will see this in the sacred Puranam of Kochchengat Chola Nayanar. Sangili Nachiyar, in her previous state as Aninditai Ammaiyar, incarnates here with an inner knowledge of her true nature, which was not revealed to her by anyone else. The parents named the incarnated Aninditaiyar as Sangiliyar. She grows up, playing games appropriate for her age with groups of young girls, and passes through all the stages of life. Sangili Nachiyar not only excels in the conduct of her lineage, which is appropriate for her incarnation, but also possesses divine qualities. Sangili Ammaiyar grows up in a way that astonishes everyone in the world.

Growing up with divine qualities means that while living in this world, one does not engage in worldly affairs, but rather cultivates actions and words related to the Lord. As she grows up in this manner, Sangili Ammaiyar reaches the age of marriage. Her parents are astonished by Sangili Ammaiyar's divine qualities. Sangili Ammaiyar's father tells Sangili Ammaiyar's mother, "Our daughter's form and qualities are not only those of ordinary people, but she is also endowed with superior divine qualities. It is time for her marriage. Therefore, our daughter is superior to the people of this world, so we must find a suitable groom and arrange her marriage."

Sangili Ammaiyar's mother, understanding this, says, "Arrange her marriage in a suitable place, befitting our family's welfare and Sangili Ammaiyar's qualities and good character." Sangili Ammaiyar's mother continues to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 32 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 870, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Sangili Ammaiyar's father and mother were discussing finding a suitable groom for Sangili Ammaiyar, and Sangili Ammaiyar overheard them. However, her father and mother were unaware that Sangili Ammaiyar was listening to their conversation. Sangili Nachiyar, who happened to be nearby, overheard her mother and father talking. The 211th verse: "Sangiliyar, who heard her father and mother speaking, said, 'This proposal is not for me. My life belongs to one who is fully blessed by our Lord, Ishan.' Fearing what might happen if her parents went against this principle, her consciousness became confused, and she fainted, falling to the ground." Sangili Ammaiyar, who heard her mother and father discussing her marriage, told them, "This talk of marriage is not suitable for me. My life belongs entirely to one who is fully blessed by the Lord." Fearing that her parents might do something contrary to this principle, Sangili Ammaiyar's consciousness became confused, and she fainted, falling to the ground. The passage from Thiruvachagam, "We are your devotees, who have you as our Lord. We will serve your devotees. We will be companions to them. They will be our husbands. Our breasts will not embrace those who are not your lovers," comes to our mind, doesn't it?

Now, the 212th verse: "Her father and mother, who were standing nearby, panicked, lifted her with tenderness, and with anguished hearts, asked, 'What has happened to her?' They supported her, sprinkled cool, fragrant rosewater, and stroked her, saying, 'What has come upon you, O fair-browed one with a bow-like forehead?'" As Sangili Ammaiyar fainted, her father and mother, who were nearby, panicked and tenderly lifted her. With anguished and confused hearts, they held Sangili Ammaiyar, asking, "What has happened to her?" Her father and mother had spoken as mentioned earlier, and Sangiliyar had heard them standing nearby. Sangiliyar, upon hearing it, had spoken thus and fainted. Her mother and father had spoken without realizing that Sangili Ammaiyar was standing nearby. Upon hearing their words, Sangili Ammaiyar replied, and before her parents could recover from their sudden astonishment, Sangili Ammaiyar fell to the ground and fainted. Immediately, her mother and father panicked, tenderly lifted her, and held her in their arms, asking, "What has happened to her?" We see this sequence of events. They sprinkled cool rosewater on Sangili Ammaiyar's body and stroked her, and her fainting and exhaustion subsided. Sangili Ammaiyar's parents experienced various emotions such as panic, tenderness, and anguish. When a beloved person suddenly shows signs of distress, those who love them experience events such as panic, tenderness, and anguish.

Panic is a trembling of the entire body due to fear. Tenderness is compassion. Anguish is mental suffering. Having experienced all these emotions, the parents supported Sangili Ammaiyar, sprinkled water on her, and relieved her fainting. They then asked, "What has happened to you here?" When her parents asked, Sangili Ammaiyar expressed her thoughts. The 213th verse: "When her parents asked, she spoke without hesitation: 'What you have said today about me is not suitable for my nature. I belong to one who has been blessed by the Lord with the victorious bull. From now on, I will go to Thiruvottriyur and live by the grace of Shiva.'" When her parents, who had given birth to her, asked as mentioned earlier, Sangili Nachiyar spoke her mind without hiding it. Here, since she had to answer her mother and father about her beloved, Sangili Ammaiyar, leaving aside the natural shyness inherent in women, spoke to her parents about her beloved. Sangili Ammaiyar, with the shyness inherent in womanhood, would not normally speak much about her beloved. But here, Sangili Ammaiyar, setting aside her shyness a little, is about to speak about her beloved. This is because a calamity is about to occur that could change the very essence and purpose of her life. When such a calamity is imminent, the truth must be revealed. If it is not revealed, her purpose will be ruined. Fearing this, and also because those who are asking are her own mother and father, Sangili Nachiyar speaks her mind without hesitation. What she says is that marrying her to anyone else is not suitable for her nature. This verse does not say "marry," but "what you have said," meaning, "if you do as you have said, it will not be suitable for my nature," says Sangili Nachiyar.

Due to the natural shyness of a woman, she should not even speak the word "marriage" to another man. Based on the power of chastity, Sangili Ammaiyar says that doing as they said is not suitable for her nature. Therefore, she says, "I will not belong to anyone else as you have said. I am one who belongs to a person who has received the grace of the Lord, who is the embodiment of the Lord's grace. My rightful Lord has already been granted by the grace of Lord Shiva. Until that marriage takes place, I will now go to Thiruvottriyur and live by the grace of the Lord, and by the grace of the Lord, I will obtain the one who is rightfully mine," says Sangili Nachiyar to her parents. The 214th verse: "Hearing those words, her father and mother were filled with weariness, fear, and wonder. They kept her words hidden from others. A man, ignorant of the nature of one equal to her in their noble lineage, desired her and sent some people to propose marriage. They went and spoke. The parents of the maiden, hearing this, were bewildered." Hearing Sangili Ammaiyar's words, her father and mother were filled with weariness, fear, and wonder. Sangili Ammaiyar's mother and father kept all these words that Sangili Ammaiyar had spoken to her parents hidden from everyone else. They kept it hidden from the outside world without telling them that this was Sangili Nachiyar's nature. Because Sangili Ammaiyar said that marrying her to anyone else, as her mother and father had decided, was not suitable for her nature, and because she spoke of severing worldly ties, her parents felt weary. Because she said, "I belong only to one who has received the grace of the Lord," her father and mother, not knowing that truth, feared that all their previous discussions would become an offense against Shiva. And because Sangili Ammaiyar said, "From now on, I will go to Thiruvottriyur and live by Shiva's grace," her parents were also filled with wonder upon hearing it. So, weariness, fear, and wonder came upon them all at once. However, even if they explained these matters to the world, they would not understand. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar's parents kept it hidden from the world. Nevertheless, it was very difficult for them to separate from their daughter and leave her in Thiruvottriyur as Ammaiyar wished. They did not agree to it. In this situation, a man, unaware of these qualities and truths, desired Sangili Ammaiyar and sent some people to propose marriage. They came to Sangili Ammaiyar's father, Gnayiru Kizhar, and asked for Sangili Ammaiyar for him. Sangili Ammaiyar had not told anyone about her decision, so her decision was unknown to anyone. Therefore, even if we assume that a man, unaware of all this, sent his relatives to propose marriage for Ammaiyar, the news that Sangili Ammaiyar, as she grew up, was not only excellent in ordinary worldly qualities but also possessed abundant extraordinary divine qualities, was known to everyone. Therefore, even after hearing that, this man should not have sent a marriage proposal to Ammaiyar's parents. If a man sends such a proposal even after knowing Ammaiyar's divine qualities, it means he does not know Ammaiyar's nature, nor does he know his own nature. Therefore, he sends his relatives for marriage negotiations. They come to Gnayiru Kizhar and convey the message. The 215th verse: "Her father, hearing that, unable to reveal her true nature, sent them away with words that would cause no harm. Before they could reach their destination, the one who had sent them died, as if he had committed a sin there. Those who had gone with him also died upon their return. The parents of the maiden, hearing this, were bewildered." The relatives sent by that man to propose marriage arrive. But Sangili Ammaiyar's father, without openly telling the visitors all the events and circumstances that had occurred so far, spoke some comforting words to them, so that they would not be saddened and would not face any difficulties. He told them that they were not in a position to agree to this marriage. Thus, Sangili Ammaiyar's father sent them away. This is a worldly custom. It is a good practice in worldly life to conceal the truth to some extent and indirectly refuse, so that no harm comes to anyone. The visitors might have been relatives. Therefore, there was a possibility of quarrels and suffering among relatives. Therefore, their intention was to avoid harm and bring about good. "If it brings about blameless good, even falsehood is truth," says Thirukkural. Therefore, concealing the truth to some extent and avoiding the harms that would come from telling the truth as it is, is a good practice in this world. At that time, Adigal Nayanar said, "He will not help here now." Those were words he spoke out of love for the Lord and love for his Guru. Similarly, in worldly life, in the path of good living, this practice is very applicable in terms of social harmony. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar's father sent them away with words that would not offend them. Before those who had come could return to their place, the one who had sent them to propose marriage died. And upon their return, those who had gone with him also died. Hearing this, the parents of the maiden were bewildered. Sangiliyar possessed divine chastity. Anyone who tried to harm her would face the death penalty. Here, the one who sent people to propose marriage to Sangili Ammaiyar dies. Therefore, that event takes place as if he died as a result of his evil deed. Even if poison is consumed unknowingly, it will kill the one who consumes it. Therefore, even if he did not intend to harm her, that action resulted in harm to Ammaiyar. Similarly, those who went to propose marriage and returned also went to the abode of Yama. Agreeing to his command and going and speaking were also offenses. Here, because two great offenses occurred, an offense against Shiva and an offense against chastity, those souls were punished. Hearing all this, Sangili Ammaiyar's parents were bewildered. This happened as if the Lord had set a rule for the people of this world to know that no one who wishes to live well would speak inappropriate words about Sangili Ammaiyar. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar's parents came forward to fulfill the action that Sangili Ammaiyar had spoken of, the action of going to Thiruvottriyur and seeking the grace of the Lord. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 33 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 871, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. The 216th verse: "As if by a divine decree, that no one who wishes to live would speak inappropriate words about the maiden Sangiliyar, this event occurred. Her parents, with sorrowful hearts and fear, explained Sangiliyar's divine nature to the elders of their village as it was, and agreed to her wish." This verse states that as if by a divine decree, that no one who wishes to live in this world would speak inappropriate words about the maiden Sangiliyar, this event occurred. Sangili Ammaiyar's parents explained Sangiliyar's divine nature to the elders in their village as it truly was. Those parents, with very sorrowful hearts and fear, agreed to Sangiliyar's wish. They agreed to Sangili Ammaiyar's desired action, which she had spoken of, to perform Shiva tapas (penance) in Thiruvottriyur. The 217th verse: "This maiden's actions are divine, and those who know it fear to speak otherwise. She knows no words other than the glory of the Lord she worships. These are her qualities. Therefore, we shall take her to Thiruvottriyur, to the Lord with the serpent-crested matted hair, as she has desired." Sangili Ammaiyar is divine, a goddess among women. Those who know this would fear to speak any other words about Sangili Ammaiyar. Her parents clearly understood that Sangili Ammaiyar knew no words other than the glory of the Lord she worshipped. Sangili Ammaiyar's action was an action dedicated to Lord Shiva, and her parents and relatives clearly understood this. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar's parents decided that they should take Sangili Ammaiyar to the Lord in Thiruvottriyur, the Lord who has a hooded serpent on his matted hair. That was the best decision. Verses 218 and 219: "Her father and relatives, addressing Sangiliyar of sweet speech, said, 'Go to Thiruvottriyur, where the southern waters meet, and by the grace of the Lord with the eye on his forehead, reside in a Kanni Madam (maiden's hermitage) in that city surrounded by beautiful ponds, and perform penance.' Her father and relatives, knowing no other course of action by the will of the Lord with the crescent moon on his head, decided to do as the maiden Sangiliyar had said. With abundant wealth, they took her to Thiruvottriyur, to the Lord with the bow that destroys external forces." Her parents, relatives, and others gathered and spoke to Sangiliyar, who had sweet speech. "Ammaiyar, you should go to the city of Thiruvottriyur, where Lord Shiva resides, and worship the Lord there by His grace. You should reside there as you wish. We will arrange a Kanni Madam and provide facilities for you to perform penance," they said. Sekkizhar Peruman structures these lines in this verse as if her parents and others are speaking: "Go to Thiruvottriyur, and by the grace of the Lord with the eye on his forehead, reside in a Kanni Madam in that city surrounded by beautiful ponds, and perform penance." Selkathi means the ultimate goal to be attained, which is Mukti (liberation). Mukti, or liberation, is the ultimate and complete state that souls can attain. Sekkizhar Peruman has also used the word "gathi" (state) here. This also refers to Sangili Ammaiyar attaining Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, her groom, in Thiruvottriyur. Therefore, it is structured as "selkathi and by grace." Just as Sangili Ammaiyar said, "I belong to one who has been blessed by the Lord," here, the attainment of a beloved is mentioned. In accordance with her statement that she belongs to one who is filled with the grace of Lord Shiva, she is about to meet Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in Thiruvottriyur and attain that state by the grace of the same Lord. She is also about to attain the state of liberation in the future. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman structures it as if her parents and others are saying, "You will attain selkathi and by grace, reside there and perform penance." The Lord alone grants both worldly and ultimate benefits. Therefore, the philosophical meaning of their words is, "Perform penance and attain worldly benefits, and finally, liberation." Therefore, as her parents and relatives had said, by the grace of Lord Shiva, the Lord of Thiruvottriyur, they decided to do as Sangili Ammaiyar, the good woman, had said. With great wealth, they took Sangili Ammaiyar to Thiruvottriyur. Verses 220 and 221: "Entering the temple of Shiva, who wears the crescent moon on his head, they worshipped with their close relatives. With the consent of the elders of the ancient city, they established a Kanni Madam nearby, performed the necessary protective rituals, and arranged abundant wealth. Her father, bowing at her feet, said, 'We will serve you. You reside in this exalted Kanni Madam, desiring to serve the Lord.' As he spoke, tears flowed from his eyes, and he, along with his grieving relatives, bowed and departed to their fortified city." In Thiruvottriyur, where Lord Shiva resides, Sangili Ammaiyar, her parents, and relatives entered the temple and worshipped the Lord. Their village was Gnayiru, and now they had arrived at Thiruvottriyur. Therefore, to reside in the city of Thiruvottriyur, they sought the consent of the elders of that city. They sought the consent of the elders of Thiruvottriyur to establish a Kanni Madam near the Thiruvottriyur temple for Sangili Ammaiyar and her female companions to reside in. This is because they were from another village and were residing in another village. Therefore, they formally sought permission from the elders there. All those who were residing were women, so they also sought permission for security and made arrangements for it. Thus, Sangili Ammaiyar's parents did all these things formally. In those days, Thondai Nadu was divided into many sections such as Kottams, Nadus, Nattams, and Oors. Although these areas were under the imperial rule of Thondai Nadu, some of them also had independent administrative systems. In each village, there were village heads and village councils. These people, who were excellent in education, knowledge, and conduct in that village, were elected and appointed as village heads and council members, and they administered the village.

Those who were elected thus undertook the responsibility of providing village security and ensuring the well-being and security of the village. Such elders were called Nattamai or Oorar in those days. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to them here as Thollai Pathiyor (elders of the ancient city). The practice of electing them through elections also existed. We see this in the Uthiramerur inscription, ancient stone inscriptions, and ancient histories. We see that this practice existed in many parts of Tamil Nadu and Thondai Nadu until very recently. Today, we see that this system has completely disintegrated. Pathiyor refers to the village community that provided village security and the village council. Therefore, they were permanently settling Sangili Ammaiyar and her female companions in Thiruvottriyur from the village of Gnayiru. Therefore, they had to reside permanently from another village. In addition, they had to establish a Kanni Madam in a public place very close to the temple. Therefore, in this worldly context, they were in a position to seek the protection of the villagers. That is why Ammaiyar's parents formally obtained permission from the village elders to establish a Kanni Madam and reside there. They also established their own security in addition to public security. They settled female companions and maidservants in that Kanni Madam. Thus, after establishing the Kanni Madam, they also arranged the wealth necessary for Sangili Ammaiyar to perform her service to the Lord without any shortcomings. The wealth needed for Sangili Ammaiyar's life and the lives of her female companions, maidservants, etc., to be conducted properly, such as food items, clothing, etc., and items for Shiva tapas, these items were arranged as various types of wealth for obtaining them as goods, through barter, and by purchase, and given to Sangili Ammaiyar by her parents. After arranging all this, Sangili Ammaiyar's father came and bowed at Sangili Ammaiyar's feet. Here, can Sangili Ammaiyar's father bow at Sangili Ammaiyar's feet? Scriptures state that a father is one of the five gurus. Can a father, who is one of the gurus, bow at his daughter's feet? This question arises here. Although it is generally stated that way, in a special situation, one can bow. This is because Sangili Ammaiyar had said, "I belong to one who has been blessed by the Lord. From now on, I will go and live by Shiva's grace." Sangili Ammaiyar gives up all her connections to the one who has received the grace of Lord Shiva. Therefore, the relationship of father and daughter had already ceased to exist. From now on, Sangili Ammaiyar stands here as one who has taken a vow to perform Shiva's service, completely abandoning worldly life, saying that she belongs to a devotee who has received Shiva's grace. Therefore, bowing to such a person who has taken a vow is appropriate. That is why her parents, who until now spoke in the singular, "What has happened to her?", now speak in the plural, "to you." Thus, her father came and bowed at Sangili Ammaiyar's feet. Her father took leave of Ammaiyar. "We will perform all the services you need. You reside in this Kanni Madam to perform service suitable for the Lord," he said. Tears flowed uncontrollably from his eyes. "The relationship of father and daughter between us has now ceased. We will now be your servants, and you perform the Lord's service. Therefore, the only connection we now have is that of being your servants. The worldly attachment between us has ceased," he said. With sorrow and unbearable grief combined, tears flowed from his eyes. Unable to part from Sangili Ammaiyar, Ammaiyar's parents, along with their relatives, bowed and departed to their village, Gnayiru. Now, Sangili Ammaiyar performs Shiva tapas in the Kanni Madam in Thiruvottriyur. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 34 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 872, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. Sangili Ammaiyar is performing penance in Thiruvottriyur. Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing. The 222nd verse: "The maidens, who lovingly performed penance, resided there. They entered the temple of the Lord of all beings at all times, worshipped, and without deviating from the path of righteousness, performed the service assigned to them. They then went to a part of the flower pavilion, which was surrounded by screens, and sat there." Now, the 223rd verse: "With the understanding of the service performed in the sacred Kailash mountain in the past, the maiden, who was like a soft creeper of blooming flowers, with her mind fixed on it, prepared garlands of fragrant flowers, which bees frequented, to adorn the Lord of the celestials at appropriate times, and worshipped, and resided there." These two verses describe Sangili Ammaiyar, who resided in the Kanni Madam in Thiruvottriyur, lovingly and constantly meditating and performing penance as a maiden. The actions Sangili Ammaiyar performed during her stay in the Kanni Madam were of two types: those performed in the Kanni Madam and those performed in the temple. When residing in the Kanni Madam, Sangili Ammaiyar meditated on the Lord and performed penance. These included Shiva Puja, meditation, and so on. In the temple, Sangili Ammaiyar performed flower service. In addition, she went to the temple at all times of worship, from Thiruppalli Ezhuchi (waking the Lord) to Thiruppalli Arai (the Lord's chamber), worshipped, and performed flower service in the flower pavilion of the temple. At other times, she resided in the Kanni Madam and performed penance there. Without deviating from the prescribed method appropriate for her lineage, age, nature, and status, she performed the service assigned to her. The Agamas (scriptural texts) state that Shiva's services should be performed according to one's righteous path. Accordingly, she performed the service. The nature of the service Sangili Ammaiyar performed in Thirukailash in the past, those very feelings arose in Ammaiyar's sacred heart, and she performed the same service. In Thirukailash, Sangili Ammaiyar used to pluck flowers in the sacred garden and make garlands and other offerings. The same service, which she was inspired to do by divine grace in her sacred heart, she performed here as well. The continuation of that deep penance is also present here. The temple's pavilion is a flower pavilion. Freshly bloomed flowers are brought there. Devotees who are engaged in making garlands for the Lord perform that service there. Even today, we can see this directly in the flower pavilion in the Meenakshi Ammaiyar shrine in Thirualavai. The Agamas, such as Pushpa Vidhi, state that flowers appropriate for each time should be offered during the puja times, starting from dawn. This is stated in the Shivagamas. Accordingly, Sangili Ammaiyar performed the service. We have contemplated all these methods and Pushpa Vidhis mentioned in the Shivagamas when we read about the service performed by Sivakami Andar in the Thirupuranam of Eribattha Nayanar. We have also contemplated these in the Thirupuranam of Muruga Nayanar. Remembering all those details, we continue to contemplate this Puranam. Thus, Sangili Ammaiyar continued to perform her service. Now, the next verse, the 224th verse: "By the grace of the Lord with the evening hue, Arurar came. It was time for Sangiliyar, who made fragrant garlands, to be united in love. Therefore, by the ancient decree, which does not deviate from the ordained nature, one day, the Lord entered the temple." By the grace of the Lord, who has the hue of the evening, Nambi Arurar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) was born in Thirunavalur. By the grace of the Lord, he has now reached Thiruvottriyur. The time has now come for such a Nambi Arurar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, to be united in love with Sangili Ammaiyar. This is by the ancient decree, which does not deviate from the ordained nature, as ordained by the Lord. The time has come for the marriage of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Sangili Ammaiyar. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's marriage to Sangili Ammaiyar is a love marriage, which is called a natural marriage. It is a spontaneous union, without the efforts of a secret love or a chaste love, which are discussed in Tamil Akapporul (Tamil love poetry) texts. Sekkizhar Peruman mentions it here as "by the ancient decree." The second type of marriage, the ordained marriage, is when the father and mother give the girl in marriage with rituals and ceremonies. Therefore, as the time ordained by the Lord arrived, one day, Nambi Arurar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, departed from his sacred mansion and entered the Lord's temple. The 225th verse: "The Lord of the celestials, who is a Brahmin, ruled Nambi. He worshipped the Lord with the beautiful eyes in the ancient manner, sang praises, and came out. He saw the sacred services performed by the devotees, worshipped them, and proceeded. He entered the sacred flower pavilion, which resembled a lotus pond." The Lord of all gods, Shiva, took the form of an old Brahmin and directly enslaved Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who resided in Thiruvottriyur and worshipped Lord Shiva daily in the prescribed manner, entered the temple as usual on that day, worshipped Lord Shiva in the prescribed manner, sang praises, and came out. He came to the outer circumambulatory path outside the Lord's mansion. There, he saw and worshipped the sacred services performed for the Lord. This implies that even the sacred services performed for the Lord are worthy of being seen and worshipped. Therefore, the sacred services performed for the Lord were taking place in the outer circumambulatory path of the temple. These included sacred garden work, sweeping the sacred courtyard, polishing sacred vessels, lamps, etc., and performing all the cleaning and service work. These are the sacred services of Chariyai (external worship). These sacred services, appropriate for each type of work, take place in various places, from the outer circumambulatory path. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal proceeded, worshipping all these sacred services.

Then, he entered the sacred flower pavilion, which is called Thiruppushpa Mandapam, resembling a lotus pond. Many kinds of flowers were gathered there. It was cool and filled with water everywhere. Therefore, that sacred flower pavilion was like a lotus pond. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal entered that sacred flower pavilion. The 226th verse: "The devotees, whose love was like a thread, strung the five letters (Panchaaksharam) in their hearts, and made garlands of flowers. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worshipped these devotees, who were firm in their inner devotion, and moved to another place. As before, he removed the screen and gave the flowers to be offered to the Lord. He then saw Sangiliyar, who disappeared like lightning, by divine decree." In that sacred flower pavilion, devotees were making garlands of flowers. What kind of devotees were they?
