The devotees were chanting the Sri Panchaaksharam (five-letter mantra) in their hearts with unwavering love, counting the beads, and making garlands of flowers without speaking. This is the rule for making garlands of flowers. In the Thirupuranam of Eripattha Nayanar, we saw that Sivakami Andar plucked flowers in the sacred garden with her mouth covered with a cloth. All these are included in the Pushpa Vidhis (rules for flowers). According to these rules, as stated in the Shivagamas, devotees were making garlands of flowers here. Love is the thread, and the flower strung on it is the Sri Panchaaksharam. Therefore, one should constantly chant and meditate on the Sri Panchaaksharam in the heart. While doing so, one should make garlands of flowers with one's hands. Thus, the devotees were performing the sacred flower service in that sacred flower pavilion. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worshipped those devotees. We saw that even the Lord's sacred services are worthy of being seen and worshipped. Similarly, here, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worshipped the devotees who were making garlands of flowers. As he worshipped and came, there was a private place inside that sacred flower pavilion. It was covered with a screen. It was a private place where no one else could see the work being done inside. It was surrounded by screens. Women would perform the sacred flower work from behind that screen. Once it was completed, they would remove the screen, bring the garlands to be offered to the Lord, give them to the devotees there, and then quickly go back behind the screen. As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal approached that private place, Sangili Ammaiyar took all the garlands she had made, removed the screen, came out, gave the garlands to the devotees to be offered to the Lord, and then quickly went back behind the screen, like a flash of lightning. As she came and went, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal saw Sangili Ammaiyar by divine grace. The next three verses are 227, 228, and 229.

"When the sacred devotee, who had a bull banner, saw her, who was like an unstrung pearl and a tender bud, his mind, which was not guarded, went to her. The arrows of Kama, which had been abandoned by him, came and struck him. He, who was unable to bear it, came out and spoke, 'It is difficult to know what kind of person this is. Inside the screen, in a corner, is she like a lightning creeper, made of gold and jewels, mixed with the light of nectar, and molded with the coolness of the new moon? Who is she who has turned my heart?' As he spoke, those standing nearby replied, 'She is the maiden Sangiliyar. She is a maiden who performs the Lord's service with abundant penance.' He then realized, 'The Lord made me attain this birth by the grace of two women. One is Paravai, and this is the other.' Thus, he was bewildered." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal saw Sangili Ammaiyar by the grace of the Lord. This is one of the Akapporul (love poetry) themes called Kaatchi (sight). We saw this theme of Kaatchi occurring with Paravaiyar in Thiruvallur in the Thaduthaatkonda Puranam. Here too, a similar Kaatchi occurs. This is also a continuation of the sight they had in Thirukailash and then separated. As soon as Sundaramoorthy Swamigal saw Sangili Ammaiyar, his mind went to Sangili Ammaiyar. As he was in this state, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal came out of the temple. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asked about the feelings he had. To this, those standing nearby replied, "She is the maiden Sangiliyar. Sangili Ammaiyar is one who performs the Lord's service with abundant penance." At that time, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal thought in his sacred heart, "The Lord made me attain this birth by the grace of two women. Among those two women, Paravai, whom I have already married, is one, and this Sangiliyar is the other woman, the second woman." Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was bewildered. His question, "Who is she?" is the theme of Aiyam (doubt) in Akapporul. The scriptures state that the Lord granting birth is also His grace, as it helps to experience and exhaust karmas and attain liberation. But here, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's birth and sacred incarnation are not merely for the sake of these two women, Paravaiyar and Sangiliyar. The purpose of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred incarnation is also for the southern land, which performed great penance, to flourish, and for the Thiruthondar Thogai (collection of sacred devotees) to be given. That is why the divine grace made Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind go to those women in Thirukailash. As a result of that, all these great sacred histories occur. We must understand this. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, Sangili Ammaiyar, and Paravai Nachiyar's sacred incarnation is not like the birth of beings like us, which is due to karma. They were born here to make the southern land, which performed great penance, flourish, and to give the blameless Thiruthondar Thogai and Thiruppathigam (sacred hymns) so that Shiva devotion and devotee devotion may prosper. For this, the divine grace of the Lord makes these two Ammaiyars a cause. We must understand this.

We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 35 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 873, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkaama Nayanar Puranam. After seeing Sangili Ammaiyar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal enters the temple, thinking, "I will ask the Lord for Sangili and receive her." The 230th verse: "He entered, thinking, 'I, who live by the decree of being the servant of the Lord with the lightning-like matted hair, am prevented by this Sangili Ammaiyar's penance from attaining the blessings that come from His grace. She binds my life and beautiful flowers together. I will receive her from the Lord with the golden Konrai flowers on His head.'" The Lord, Shiva, has already made Sundaramoorthy Swamigal His servant, separating him from all worldly connections. Therefore, everything that happens to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal happens by Shiva's grace. It is proper to ask Shiva for all these things that Shiva has shown and brought together. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has already stated in his Thevaram, "I have placed my mind only on your sacred feet, without any other attachment," and "I consider you as my wealth and true treasure." These are the states of Shiva's life that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal embraced. Therefore, he went to Shiva, thinking, "I will receive her from Shiva." In reality, it is Lord Shiva who brings about all the experiences and karmic fruits that come to all living beings. But all these beings, unaware of this truth, are deluded by the veil of ego and think that all these things come from their own efforts. But enlightened souls like Sundaramoorthy Swamigal are those who have fully realized the truth that everything comes from Lord Shiva. Therefore, they lived, knowing that Shiva is the giver and He is the Lord. Now, the Lord Himself has said to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "We have given you friendship," and has accepted Sundaramoorthy Swamigal as a friend. We saw this in the Thaduthaatkonda Puranam. Therefore, Sivakavimani Aiyya has commented that this is also appropriate in the context of Paangar Kuttam (meeting of friends), one of the themes in Tamil literature. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal enters the Thiruvottriyur temple. The 231st verse: "He worshipped the luminous Supreme Being, who is the Lord of Thiruvottriyur, who is not known by Brahma on the lotus or by Vishnu, who delved into the sky and earth, who has a sacred head adorned with the growing crescent moon and long anklets, and who, though He owns all the worlds, lovingly resides in Thiruvottriyur, and stood before Him and praised Him." The Lord of Thiruvottriyur is He who is not known by Brahma on the lotus or by Vishnu, who delved into the sky and earth. The Lord of Thiruvottriyur has a sacred head adorned with the growing crescent moon and long anklets. Though He owns all the worlds, He lovingly resides in Thiruvottriyur. This is the unique specialty of Thiruvottriyur. Although the Lord is pervasive throughout the world, He remains hidden there like ghee in milk. In supreme sacred places and Shiva temples, He shines forth like ghee in curd, says the Gnana Sasthiram. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this with the phrase, "though He owns all the worlds, He lovingly resides in Thiruvottriyur, the luminous light." Amarthal means to reside lovingly. "He shines brightly in some places and not in others," says the 12th Sutra of Sivagnana Botham. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who goes before such a Lord of Thiruvottriyur, makes a request to the Lord for Sangili Ammaiyar. That is the next verse. The 232nd verse: "O Lord of Thiruvottriyur, you rejoice in having Uma Ammaiyar on one side, and you lovingly conceal Mother Ganga in your long, sacred matted hair. You, who are so greatly desirous, please give me Sangili, whose face is like the moon, who makes garlands for you and unties the knots of my heart, and relieve my suffering." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says to the Lord, "O Lord of Thiruvottriyur, you rejoice in having Uma Ammaiyar on one side. Not only that, but you have also lovingly concealed Mother Ganga in your long, sacred matted hair. You are so greatly desirous. You, who are so greatly desirous, please give me Sangili, who makes garlands for you and unties the knots of my heart, and relieve my suffering." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was already married to Paravai Nachiyar and living with her. He is asking the Lord for another wife, Sangiliyar, even though he already has Paravaiyar as his wife. If the Lord were to ask, "You are already married to Paravaiyar. Do you want to marry Sangiliyar too?" Sundaramoorthy Swamigal replies to the Lord beforehand, "You have Uma Ammaiyar on your left side, and you have concealed another woman in your matted hair. I also desire the same. If that state is suitable for you, it is also suitable for me." Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asks the Lord in a friendly manner. We can remember here that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is a friend of the Lord, and the Lord Himself desired friendship and gave it to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal showed the path of Sakamargam (path of friendship) to this world. The reason for the Lord concealing Ganga was love. Similarly, here too, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal indicates that love is the reason. When speaking about Sangili Ammaiyar, he says, "She makes garlands for you. At the same time, Sangiliyar is also capable of untying the knots of my heart." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says that Sangiliyar has renounced all her relatives and attachments and has become your property. Therefore, only you can give her to me. You are the only one worthy of giving her. Thus, he makes a request to the Lord, "Therefore, give me Sangiliyar and relieve my suffering." This is what Sundaramoorthy Swamigal requested from the Lord with his sacred words, standing before the Lord. The 233rd verse: "After making many requests to the Lord, knowing that He knows all, he came out. He thought, 'Sangiliyar has stolen all the firmness of my heart, which always thinks of being your servant. I do not know what to do now. O Lord with the cool moon and coral-like matted hair, please bestow your grace upon me,' and he grew weary." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after making many sweet requests to the Lord, knowing that the Lord knows all, came out of the temple. He then thought to the Lord, "O Lord, Sangiliyar has stolen all the firmness of my heart, which always thinks of being your servant. I do not know what to do now. O Lord with the cool moon and coral-like matted hair, please bestow your grace upon me," and he grew weary. Although the Lord knows everything as it is, this verse says that he made requests to the Lord, knowing that He knows all. Making such requests to the Lord, knowing that He knows all, serves to strengthen and mature the love one has for Him. Nambigal's complaint to the Lord is also for the benefit of instructing the people of the world. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says, "I have placed it in you." The verse says, "My heart always thinks of being your servant." We see all these sacred words in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's Thevaram, "I have placed my heart in you," and "I have placed my head, tongue, and heart in you without any deceit." What does it mean to think of being a servant of the Lord? It means to constantly keep the heart in a state of Shiva Yoga. "Even if I forget, I will say 'Namah Shivaya,'" says Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Nambigal practiced Shiva Yoga in such a way that even his external senses were trained in this state of Shiva Yoga. In the Thiruppathigam of Thiruaamathur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal explains the Shiva Yoga he practiced, saying, "I will seek, I will seek, four fingers above the navel." Thirugnanasambandha Peruman says, "I, who cannot forget." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman has sung, "I think, what shall I say I think? I have no eyes but to think of the sacred feet of my Lord." Our Paramacharyas have thus taught us. Therefore, living by constantly remembering that Lord Shiva is our Lord, that we are His eternal servants, and that He is the greatly compassionate one who bestows His grace upon us at every moment, even if we make mistakes, is the only way to attain salvation. Our Paramacharyas have thus shown us. Did Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind, which always thought of Shiva's service, go to something else here? That too was brought about by Shiva's grace, by divine decree. It is to be understood that Nambigal's mind would not go anywhere outside of Shiva. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's decision to receive her from Lord Shiva, and his standing before Lord Shiva and requesting, confirm his state of Shiva Yoga. In this verse, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal requests and calls upon the Lord, "O Lord who wears the moon in your matted hair." The Lord wearing the crescent moon signifies that the Lord is the embodiment of grace, who protects and nurtures any soul that takes refuge in Him and comes to Him in distress. That is the significance of Him wearing the moon in His matted hair. Similarly, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal here takes refuge in the Lord, saying, "I have taken refuge in you. Please protect my life." This is the inner meaning of his request to the Lord. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 36 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 874, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after praying to the Lord in the temple and requesting Sangili Ammaiyar, now comes out of the temple.

The 234th verse:
"Mathi vaazh mudiyar magizh koyil purathu or marungu vandhu iruppa Kathiron melaikkadal kaana maalaikkadalai kandu ayarvaar muthiraa mulaiyaar thammai manam punarkka vendi mularri valai nidhiyaan nanbar thamakku arulum nanbaal ninaindhu ninaindhu azhiya enbadhu thiruppaadal."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is now staying on one side outside the temple where the Lord resides. This is not the usual residence where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stays in Thiruvottriyur. This is a private place where Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is staying next to the temple. It is evening, and the sun is setting in the west. This evening time is a period of sorrow for those separated from their companions. This is beautifully explained in Tamil literature within the various aspects of Akapporul (love poetry). At that time, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal melts in thought, wishing that the Lord would arrange his marriage to Sangili. The Lord is a friend of Kubera (the god of wealth), who possesses the two treasures, Sanghanidhi and Padmanidhi. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays to the Lord, asking Him to help him through the friendship that the Lord has not only with Kubera but also with him.

The verse:
"Umbar uyya ulaguyya ola velai vidamunda thambiranaar vanthondar thambaal eythi Sangiliyai imbar ulagil yaavarukkum eythavonna irundhavaththu kombai unakku tharugindrom konda kavalai oli enna enbadhu thiruppaadal."

As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal makes that request to the Lord in the evening, the Lord, who consumed poison to save the heavens and the earth, appears before Nambi (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal). The Lord appears before Nambi and says, "I give you Sangili, who is of great penance, unattainable by anyone in this world. Therefore, cast away the sorrow you hold in your heart." The Lord thus graciously tells Sundaramoorthy Swamigal.

The idea here is that the Lord consumed poison for the salvation of the Devas (celestial beings) and the people of this world. Similarly, as a result of the impending marriage between Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Sangili Ammaiyar, the Devas and the people of the world are to receive divine grace. This is the implication. The Lord appears directly before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and says this. The greatness of Sangili Ammaiyar is expressed here through the very words of the Lord. If the Lord Himself says that Sangiliyar is one who has performed great penance that no one else can achieve, then the greatness of Sangili Ammaiyar's profound penance is conveyed to us here. Therefore, to be worthy of marrying such a greatly ascetic Sangili Ammaiyar, one must possess even greater penance than Sangili Ammaiyar.

That Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is one who possesses such superior penance is confirmed in the following verse. The 236th verse:
"Andru vennainalluril valiya aandukondu aruli ondrum ariyaa naayenukku urudhi aliththeer uyir kaakka indrum ivalai manam punarkku nindreer ena potri mandral malarchevadi inai keezh vanangi magizhndhaar vanthondar."

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal melts in thought, remembering that the Lord Himself appeared directly and promised to give Sangili to him. He praises the Lord, saying, "On that day in Thiruvannainallur, you forcibly took me into your service; that was the power of your great grace." He continues, "You came forcibly in Thiruvannainallur and took me, a dog who knows nothing, into your service, and gave me assurance to protect my life. Today, you have consented to arrange this marriage for Sangili and have bestowed your grace upon me." Thus, praising the Lord, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal rejoices, bowing at the Lord's lotus feet. Throughout the life story of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, we see the event of divine grace where the Lord Himself came forcibly and took him into service in Thiruvannainallur. When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal denied, "I am not your servant," the Lord forcibly came and argued in the marriage pandal (marriage hall), saying, "This one is my servant." That marriage was a marriage that was to happen under the illusion of worldly circumstances. At that time, the Lord, our Lord, came and took Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who was standing as the groom there, into service, preventing him from getting involved in that marriage and going into worldly life. He won the case in Vennainallur, disappeared, and then appeared on the Rishabha (bull mount), enlightening Sundaramoorthy Swamigal about the truth as it is, and took him into service.

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal rejoiced, considering it the Lord's great grace. The Lord separated Sundaramoorthy Swamigal from worldly illusion and followed him to take him into service. That was the boon given in Thirukailayam (Mount Kailash), and that alone is the assurance for life. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal realized that this marriage was to happen as a continuation of that same boon received in Thirukailayam and the command received there. Therefore, he praises the Lord, saying, "On that day, you gave assurance in Thirukailayam. According to that, first, you came and stopped me in Thiruvannainallur. Now, here, you have promised to give Sangili Ammaiyar to me." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal rejoices, bowing at the Lord's lotus feet. The 237th verse:
"Aandu konda andhananaar avarukku aruli karunaiyinaal neenda kangu yamaththu neengi vaanil nirai madhiyam theendu kanni maadaththu sendru thigazh Sangiliyaaraam thoondusothi vilakkanaiyaar thambaal kanavil thondrinaar."

The Lord, the Brahmin who took Sundaramoorthy Swamigal into service, just as He graced Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, now leaves that place in the night and goes to the Kannimaadam (maiden's chamber) where Sangili Ammaiyar is staying. Here, saying that the Lord appeared to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and then left to go to the Kannimaadam where Sangili Ammaiyar is, is merely a figure of speech. Because for the Lord, going and coming are not applicable in that sense. He is omnipresent, pervading everywhere. All other things are contained within His pervasiveness. Therefore, He is the Punnian (meritorious one) who has no going, coming, or union. So, when it says that the Lord left Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's place and went to the Kannimaadam, it should be understood as the Lord manifesting and then disappearing, or manifesting and then concealing Himself. Thus, going to the Kannimaadam, the Lord appears in the dream of Sangiliyar, who is like a kindled lamp of light. "Thoondusothi vilakku anaiyaar" means a lamp of light that shines brightly, illuminating the minds of the people of this world and inspiring good in them. Another meaning is that it is a lamp that is now to be kindled and shine by the Lord. In the dream of such a Sangili Ammaiyar, the Lord appears. There are many ways in which the Lord manifests and reveals Himself to souls for their grace. Among the three types of souls – Vijnanakalar, Pralayakalar, and Sakalar – the Lord appears to Vijnanakalar in their own nature, as the knowledge within their knowledge. To Pralayakalar, the Lord appears directly before them. To Sakalar, who are ordinary souls, He appears and reveals Himself in indirect ways, such as in the form of a Guru. Additionally, the Lord reveals Himself to the graced ones in dreams and in waking states. We see this in the words of the Thirumurais (sacred hymns). The Thevaram of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman says, "Kanavilum enakku vandhu eythu nirmalan" (The pure one who comes to me even in dreams). The Thiruvaimoor Pathigam of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman contains the meaning, "Unni unni urangugindrenukku thannai vaaimoor thalaivanaama solli" (To me who sleeps thinking and thinking, He revealed Himself as the Lord of Vaimoor). The Thiruvachagam says, "Kanavilum devarkku ariyaay potri nanavilum naayarkku arulinai potri" (Praise to you who are rare even to the Devas in dreams, praise to you who grace even dogs in waking). Therefore, through these sacred words, it is understood that the Lord reveals Himself in dream states and waking states. The Lord also reveals Himself as an ethereal voice, as a pervasive light in the sky. He also comes and graces in many other divine forms. The Lord's manifestation in these various states occurs according to the individual's state and maturity. We should not see differences or hierarchies in these ways the Lord manifests. Thirugnanasambandha Peruman's divine command is, "Aatpaal avarkku arulum vannamum aadhi maanbum ketpaan pugil alavillai kilakka vendaa" (If one attempts to inquire into the manner of His grace to His servants and His primordial greatness, it is boundless and need not be spoken of). Therefore, in these ways of revealing, the Lord appears to Sangili Ammaiyar in a dream.

The 238th and 239th verses are as follows:
"Thotrum pozhudhil Sangiliyar thozhudhu vizhundhu paravasamaay aatra anbu pongi ezhundhu adiyen uyya ezhundharulum peetru en yaan seyvadhu ena periya karunai pozhindhanaiya neetrukkola vedhiyarum ner nindru arulai seygindrar. Saarum thavaththuch Sangili kel saala enpaal anbu udaiyaan meruvaraiyin mempattath thavaththaan vennai nallooril yaarum ariya yaan aazha uriyaan unnai ena irandhaan vaarkonmulaiyaay nee avanai manaththaal anaivaay magizhndhu endraar enbavai thiruppaadalgal."

The Lord appears in Sangili Ammaiyar's dream. When Sangiliyar sees the Lord in her dream, she bows down to the ground, becomes ecstatic, and overflows with immense love. The form in which the Lord appeared in Sangili Ammaiyar's dream was that of a Brahmin adorned with sacred ash, overflowing with compassion. Sekkizhar Peruman highlights that divine form here as "karunai pozhindhanaiya neetrukkola vedhiyar" (a Brahmin adorned with sacred ash, as if compassion itself was overflowing). This very form of a Brahmin adorned with sacred ash is the form the Lord showed to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman in Thirumaraikkadu, the form shown in Thirukailayam, to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in Thiruvannainallur, and to Thiruvathavooradigal. And now, the Lord appears in a dream to Sangili Ammaiyar in the same form. This form of a Brahmin adorned with sacred ash seems to be a familiar and ancient form for the Lord. Bowing to the Lord who appeared in this manner, Sangili Ammaiyar says, "What can I, your servant, do in return for the great blessing of your coming to save me?" The Lord, who appeared as a Brahmin adorned with sacred ash, as if great compassion was overflowing, then graciously speaks to Sangili Ammaiyar. The overflowing sacred ash on the Brahmin form of the Lord is like the immense, unmanifested compassion within the Lord overflowing and unable to be contained. With such a form, the Lord tells Sangili Ammaiyar, "Listen, Sangili, who possesses profound penance that seeks me! He is one who has great love for me, and he is of penance greater than Mount Meru. In Thiruvannainallur, I took him as my servant for all to see. He asked me for you. Therefore, Sangili, you shall marry him with joy in your heart."

The fact that Sangili Ammaiyar is one who possesses penance that seeks the Lord comes from the Lord's own words. Thirukkural says, "Saarbu unarndhu saarbu keda ozhugin" (If one understands the attachment and acts to destroy it). Penance is performed towards Lord Shiva. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to Sangiliyar's penance as "saarum thavam" (seeking penance) to indicate that it is penance that seeks the Lord, eliminating all other forms of penance. The Lord Himself, through His words, calls Sangili Ammaiyar "saarum thavaththu Sangili" (Sangili of seeking penance), indicating that she is full of penance. Similarly, when speaking of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, He says that he is of penance greater than Mount Meru. Therefore, the states and connections of both of them arise from their Shiva-seeking penance. Their marriage occurs due to their Shiva-seeking connection. It is not a marriage that occurs due to worldly connections, like the marriages of other worldly people. To convey that considering it so would be a transgression, the Lord Himself refers to Sangili Ammaiyar as "saarum thavaththu Sangili" and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal as "meruvaraiyin mempattath thavaththaan" (one of penance greater than Mount Meru). We must understand this as it is. Therefore, the Lord graciously tells Sangili Ammaiyar, "You shall marry that Vanthondan (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) according to custom and live happily. Accept him with a joyful heart, and after living happily, you shall come to our world, Thirukailayam." We will continue to contemplate this.

Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 37 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 875, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. The three continuous verses, 240, 241, and 242, are as follows: "When the Primeval Lord stood before her and blessed her, Sangiliyar, whose beauty even Vishnu and Brahma could not comprehend, fell at his cool, lotus-like feet, and then stood up before the Primeval Lord of the Vedas, trembling, and worshipped him, saying, 'O my Lord, I am worthy of your blessing. O Lord of the celestials, I have accepted your divine command. When you graciously give me to Nambi Aruran in a proper ritualistic marriage, I have a request to make to you, who hold Uma, the creeper of the Himalayas, in one part of your body.'"

"Then, before the Lord with matted hair, she, greatly shy, bowed and said, 'O Lord, who wears the shining sacred thread on your chest, touched by the breasts of our Lady, you know that he (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) delights greatly in Thiruvārur. Please bless me accordingly.'" These are the verses. Lord Shiva, the Primeval Lord, tells Sangili Ammaiyar, "Accept Nambi Aruran." As soon as the Primeval Lord blessed her, the beautiful Sangili Ammaiyar bows down at the cool, lotus-like feet of Lord Shiva, whom even Vishnu and Brahma cannot comprehend. She falls down and then stands up before the Primeval Lord of the Vedas, trembling, and worships him.

The word "sennindru" in the verse means "standing straight." This implies the custom of standing straight before the Lord and during Shiva Puja. It is mentioned that she worships, trembling. It is as if she is informing the Lord of something he does not know. Now, Sangili Ammaiyar begins to pray to the Lord. Therefore, she trembled. Even though the Lord knows everything, she trembles when she begins to reply to him. Even though Sangiliyar knows fully well that the Lord knows what is to be desired, she is about to pray to the Lord out of eagerness. Therefore, she trembled. Thus, Sangili Ammaiyar prays to the Lord, trembling. "O my Lord, I am worthy of him whom you have blessed.

O Lord of the celestials, I accept your divine command with reverence. When you graciously give me to Nambi Aruran in a proper ritualistic marriage, I have a request to make to you, who hold Uma, the creeper of the Himalayas, in one part of your body," says Sangili Ammaiyar. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was blessed by you. This marriage was blessed by Lord Shiva in Kailash before. Here, he also appears in a dream and blesses. Among the two types of marriages, Kalavu Manam (secret marriage) and Karpu Manam (chaste marriage), Lord Shiva is about to give Sangili Ammaiyar to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal openly, with the knowledge of the village and relatives. Thus, Sangili Ammaiyar is to be given to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal by the Lord of Thiruvottiyur, who is the special authority here, with the knowledge of the village and relatives.

Now, a formal marriage is about to take place. Therefore, this is a marriage that falls under the category of "vidhi manam" (a marriage ordained by fate or divine will). Sangili Ammaiyar left her parents, village, and relatives and placed herself under the grace of Lord Shiva. Therefore, the right to perform Sangili Ammaiyar's marriage, both generally and specifically, fell to the Lord of Thiruvottiyur. It became the duty of the Lord of Thiruvottiyur. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar prays to the Lord, saying, "When you graciously give me." "You always remain inseparable from Uma, holding her in one part of your body. Similarly, Nambi Aruran, who is to be my husband, should always remain with me, inseparable." This is the request she is about to make. Accordingly, she addresses the Lord as "O Lord, who holds Uma in one part of your body," and makes her request.

Sangili Ammaiyar continues to say to the Lord, "O Lord, Nambi Aruran, who is Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, is one who delights greatly and permanently resides in Thiruvārur. You should know that quality and bless me accordingly." Sangili Ammaiyar, who is like an unfading lamp, makes this request to the Lord. Sekkizhar Peruman mentions "peruga naani" (greatly shy) when she makes this request. Shyness is the first of the four natural qualities of womanhood. It is a natural quality of womanhood to desire that her husband should always remain with her, inseparable, and exclusively hers. But when she expresses this to others, that shyness, which is characteristic of womanhood, comes into play. Moreover, the nature of expressing things indirectly, without being able to state them openly, is a manifestation of that shyness. Therefore, what is Sangili Ammaiyar requesting from the Lord here? Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, after marrying her, should never leave her. This is the desire that Sangili Ammaiyar holds in her heart. She does not express this desire openly to the Lord. Instead, she expresses it indirectly, saying that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is always fond of staying in Thiruvārur, so the Lord should bless her in a way that is appropriate for his nature. Sangili Ammaiyar expresses this request indirectly.

"Delighting greatly in Thiruvārur" is Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's nature. Therefore, if he goes to Thiruvārur after marriage, separation will occur. Moreover, he is the husband of another lady, Paravai Nachiyar, in Thiruvārur. Therefore, Sangili Ammaiyar indirectly conveys to the Lord the condition that he should not leave her after marriage. The Lord, who knows everything, also knows the inner thoughts of Sangili Ammaiyar. Nevertheless, out of eagerness, Sangili Ammaiyar makes this request, and her body trembles. She is like an unfading lamp, an undiminishing, unwaning lamp. The three verses, 243, 244, and 245, are as follows: "The Lord who resides in Thiruvottiyur, having understood the state of Vanthondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) from her words, blessed her, saying, 'O lady with golden bangles, he will make a vow to you that he will not leave you. He will make a vow in a secret manner.' Thus, having blessed her, he returned from the maiden's chamber and went to his friend, who was joyful, and said, 'We have told her your desire to marry her. There is a condition that you must fulfill for her to marry you.' Vanthondar, delighted, bowed and asked, 'What is the duty I must perform?' And he was told, 'Go to her now, this very night, and make a vow that you will not leave her, so that you may marry her.'" These are the verses. The Lord, who resides in Thiruvottiyur, accepted all the words that Sangili Ammaiyar prayed.

The Lord, having accepted Sangiliyar's request, also knew the state of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind. What was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's state of mind or inner desire? It was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's life to go to all the temples where the Lord resided, worship him, and sing his praises. The Lord also knew this. Generally, "aruli" (blessing) refers to the Lord's special awareness that he undertakes by activating his power to operate knowledge, desire, and action. Therefore, the Lord of Thiruvottiyur, having understood Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's state of mind, now blesses Sangili Ammaiyar. He calls Sangili Ammaiyar and says, "O Sangili, adorned with golden bangles, Nambi Aruran will make a vow to you that he will not leave you. He will make a vow in a secret manner between the two of you." The Lord blesses Sangili Ammaiyar in this way.

Having blessed her thus, the Lord returns from Sangiliyar and comes to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The Lord comes to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, his joyful friend, and says, "We have told her your desire to marry her. There is a condition that you must fulfill for her to marry you. If you do that, you can marry her," says the Lord. Nambigal's heart desired to marry Sangili Ammaiyar, but Sekkizhar Peruman clearly states here that it was a pure heart. Sekkizhar Peruman, having researched why Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who had already married Paravai Nachiyar, now desired to marry Sangili Ammaiyar, and to prevent those who read and hear this story from hastily committing sacrilege and suffering, mercifully warns and states in this verse that Nambigal's heart was pure and unblemished.

To reveal that his heart was pure and unblemished, he uses the phrase "thūya manam" (pure heart). He was a friend of the Lord. The Lord, who was such a friend, says to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "If you want to marry her, you must do one thing. If you do that, she is ready to accept you. She gives her consent," says the Lord. Immediately, Vanthondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), delighted, bows and asks the Lord, "What is the action I must perform?" To that, the Lord says, "Before you marry her, you must give her a promise. You must vow before her, this very night, that you will not leave Sangiliyar and will stay with her." The Lord blesses him. Hearing that, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says to the Lord, "To perform this marriage, if this marriage is to take place, I will do whatever action is required for this marriage to take place. But for that, I must receive your divine grace." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays to the Lord. We will continue to contemplate what Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays to the Lord. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 38 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 876, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal gives an assurance to the Lord that he is ready to do whatever is necessary to marry Sangiliyar. The 246th verse says: "If this can be achieved, I will do it. For that, O Lord with the lightning-like matted hair, I need your grace," he said. The Primeval Lord, with a smile, looked at his face and said, "What more do you need for that action?" This is how the verse is structured. The Lord said, "If you want to marry her, you must vow to her that you will always stay here with her and never leave her." To this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal replied,

"I will do whatever action will make this marriage happen, but first, I must receive your divine grace," Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says to the Lord. To that, the Lord, with a faint smile, looks at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's face and asks, "What more do you need to make that vow?" It is not customary to make a vow and then marry. It is not customary to marry by giving a promise. Therefore, the Lord asks Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "What more do you need?" It is Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's duty and responsibility to go to Sangili Ammaiyar, make a vow, and get married. Even so, Lord Shiva asks Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "What more do you need to do?"

The 247th verse says: "Vanthondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), who gave his heart to the maiden with soft, fragrant breasts, knew that his Lord would visit other holy places and worship the Lord in his desired forms. With the thought that this (vow) would be an obstacle to his duty, he prayed to his Lord, expressing his shortcoming." This is how the verse is structured. Vanthondar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who gave his heart to Sangiliyar, asks the Lord. With what intention does he ask? Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's duty is to visit all the holy places where the Lord resides and worship the Lord in the forms in which he resides there. If he makes a vow to Sangili Ammaiyar and promises to stay with her always in Thiruvottiyur, that vow will be an obstacle to his worshipping the Lord in other holy places.

Considering this in his heart, he prays to the Lord, expressing his shortcoming. "Sing in beautiful Tamil about me on earth," was the command given by the Lord to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal when he took him as his own. Considering the Lord's command to him to visit various holy places and sing hymns as his duty, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visits various holy places and praises the Lord's forms. If he makes a promise to Sangili Ammaiyar, as she desired, that he will stay with her always in Thiruvottiyur, it will hinder his primary duty. Considering this in his heart, he prays to the Lord. At this point, Sivakavimani Desikanar, who is commenting, raises another subtle question and answers it.

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has a duty to visit holy places and sing praises to the Lord. So, can't he take Sangili Ammaiyar with him to all the holy places he visits and sing praises? Then, the vow would be fulfilled, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's primary duty of visiting holy places and singing praises to the Lord would also be fulfilled. This question might arise in our minds. If we ask, "Can't he take Sangiliyar with him to all the other holy places and worship?" it is not so. Doing so is an act that people like us, who are bound by worldly life, would do, writes Sivakavimani Desikanar. He further states that even before, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal had married Paravai Nachiyar. But if we look at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's pilgrimages so far, we see that he went alone without taking Paravai Nachiyar with him.

Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's desire for both Sangiliyar and Paravaiyar was only to the extent that it would lead to their liberation in this worldly life, and he did not lead this married life like the worldly lives of other people. We must understand that his life was a Shiva life, and his incarnation and life were for the benefit of this world. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal incarnated here with two divine purposes: "to make the southern region, which performed great penance, flourish, and to give the faultless Thiruthondar Thogai (a collection of hymns about devotees)." The two marriages that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal performed took place only to the extent that they supported the purpose of his incarnation, and they were not like the lives of other worldly people. The Lord's intervention to stop the arranged marriage of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal with the daughter of Puthur Sadangavi Sivachariyar, which was to be like the worldly lives of other people, was also based on this idea. Therefore, we should not consider our great spiritual masters as one of the worldly people. We must understand that their lives are not like ours. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is now going to pray to the Lord.

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is going to pray to the Lord that when he makes a vow to Sangili Ammaiyar that he will never leave her, the Lord should not be inside the temple but should be present under the Magizha tree outside the temple. This is also the inner meaning of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's prayer. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal did not say this or ask for this with the intention of deceiving Sangili Ammaiyar or speaking falsely. Sivakavimani Desikanar writes a subtle commentary, saying that we must keep all this in mind when studying this sacred history. The 248th verse says: "The King of Tamil, bowing at Shankara's feet, says, 'When I come with that maiden to make a vow that I will not leave her, you should leave the temple and reside under the sacred Magizha tree.' Thus he bowed." This is the verse. In this verse, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays to the Lord. He bows at Shankara's feet and prays. Shankara means one who brings happiness. The one who prays thus is Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the King of Tamil. He says to the Lord, "O Lord, when I come to make a vow that I will not leave Sangili, you should leave the temple and reside under the Magizha tree outside the temple." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays. It is only for a very short time that I will come with Sangiliyar and make that vow. Therefore, during that time, the Lord should leave the sanctum sanctorum and reside under the Magizha tree. The Lord is omnipresent and pervades everywhere without any exclusion. He pervades all things, both inside and outside, in a subtle way. He contains all things within his pervasion. If such a great Lord is asked by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to leave the Shiva Lingam and reside under the Magizha tree, it means that even though the Lord is generally omnipresent and pervades everywhere without exclusion, he specially manifests brightly in the Shiva Lingam. Just as milk is present throughout a cow's body but manifests at the udder, the Lord, though present in all things, manifests most specially in the Shiva Lingam, say the Agamas (scriptural texts). Therefore, what Sundaramoorthy Swamigal desires is that the Lord should not manifest brightly in the Shiva Lingam but should remain hidden. He should manifest specially and brightly under the Magizha tree. This is what Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prays for. If we interpret it otherwise, that he should leave the temple and be under the Magizha tree, it would contradict the Lord's divine quality of omnipresence, which is an essential and excellent quality, and it would become an impossible task for the Lord. Therefore, we must interpret it in this way. Some people might criticize this as an intention to deceive Sangiliyar. But that is not the case. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was intent on worshipping the Lord in all the temples. Therefore, he prays in this way, thinking that this vow would hinder that primary purpose. Even if he makes such a vow, we will see that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal will undertake pilgrimages, disregarding the suffering that might come from breaking the vow and worshipping the Lord in the places where he resides. We will see in the coming history that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, even though he was ready to wholeheartedly endure all the sufferings that would come from breaking the vow, considered visiting the Lord's temples as his primary goal and undertook pilgrimages. The 249th and 250th verses say: "When the friend of the Lord prayed thus, the Lord of the celestials agreed, saying, 'Nambi, we will do as you said.' And Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, praising him, said, 'O my Lord, what more can be difficult for me now?' And having received his grace, he came out, with his hands folded in salutation on his head. The Lord with the red matted hair, delighted with his play with his friend, or perhaps contemplating the greatness of Sangiliyar's hereditary devotion, returned in the deep darkness to speak reassuring words to her." These are the two verses. When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the friend of the Lord, prayed thus, the Lord of the celestials agreed. He blessed him, saying, "Nambi, we will do as you said." Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, greatly delighted, praises the Lord, saying, "O my Lord, there is nothing more that is difficult for me to attain." He comes out, with his hands folded in salutation on his head, having received his grace. At that time, Lord Shiva, delighted with his play with his friend, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, or perhaps contemplating the greatness of Sangili Ammaiyar's hereditary devotion, we do not know why, returns to Sangili Ammaiyar in her dream in the deep darkness, when all living beings are asleep. Sekkizhar Peruman has structured this verse as his own statement. Sekkizhar Peruman structures this verse, "thunju irul meelavum anainthar" (returned in the deep darkness), to convey that even when all living beings are asleep, the Lord, without sleeping, is intent on blessing those souls. "Praise be to you, who neither eat nor sleep," is the sacred saying of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. All the actions that the Lord performs for living beings are the five divine functions (Panchakrityam), which are his divine play. All the divine plays that the Lord performs are for the benefit of living beings. The Thirugnanasiddhiyar verse says: "What is the reason for these actions that are said to be performed? If one considers their origin, it is the play of the Primeval Lord. It is also said that he grants lasting intelligence and liberation to souls and, by his grace, removes all dense impurities." Now, the action that the Lord is about to perform is either for the sake of playing a divine game with his friend, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, or to reveal to the world the greatness of Sangili Ammaiyar's devotion. Sekkizhar Peruman raises these two questions here and leaves it to the readers of this book to infer the answer. Sekkizhar Peruman's poetic excellence is evident in such places. Sangili Ammaiyar, in Thirukailai, had performed the service of picking flowers from the Nandavanam (flower garden) and offering them to Uma Ammaiyar, out of devotion. Accordingly, even now, when she incarnated as Sangili Ammaiyar, she continued the same flower service and attained the greatness of serving the Lord alone in this earthly world. Therefore, if the Lord is going to go into Sangili Ammaiyar's dream and tell her the message that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal prayed for, Sekkizhar Peruman implies that the reason for this is the greatness of Sangili Ammaiyar's hereditary devotion, and leaves the answer to that question to be inferred. The hereditary devotion of Sangili Ammaiyar is the nature of her service that she had been performing from Thirukailai. In addition, the fact that Sangili Ammaiyar was born in the Vellalar community, which has the excellence of serving Shiva hereditarily, is also implied here. The 251st and 252nd verses say: "Then, the Lord, having blessed Sangiliyar as before, said, 'O maiden, Nambi Aruran will make a vow to you with love, as you desired. But do not agree to him making that vow before us in the temple. Let him make it under the Magizha tree with fragrant flowers.' And she, with folded hands, said, 'O Lord, whom Vishnu and Brahma cannot comprehend, I have been accepted as your servant by your grace to me.' And tears flowed from her eyes, and she fell at the feet of the Lord with the victorious young bull and rose." Lord Shiva, who had granted Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's request, now appears in Sangili Ammaiyar's dream again. The Lord appears in Sangili Ammaiyar's dream as before and blesses her, saying, "O maiden, Sangili, Nambi Aruran will make a vow to you with love, as you desired. But do not agree to him making that vow before us in the temple. Tell him to make it under the Magizha tree and accept it." The Lord blesses Sangili Ammaiyar. Hearing that, Sangili Ammaiyar, with folded hands, worships him, saying, "O Lord, whom Vishnu and Brahma cannot comprehend, I have truly been accepted as your servant by your grace to me." Tears flow in great streams from her eyes, and she falls at the feet of the Lord and rises. The Lord, by performing this act of grace, has accepted me as one of his devotees. I understand that you have accepted me too. "I have been accepted as the Lord's servant," she says. When Sangili Ammaiyar witnessed this act of grace, being accepted as a servant by the Lord, her heart was filled with overflowing love and tears, as she contemplated the Lord's boundless great compassion and her own boundless insignificance. This is the overflowing tears of love that flow when one contemplates the Lord's great compassion and one's own boundless insignificance, upon witnessing the act of grace of being accepted as a servant by the Lord. With these tears flowing, Sangili Ammaiyar falls at the feet of the Lord and rises. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 39 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 877, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Lord Shiva appears to Sangili Ammaiyar in her dream for the second time and blesses her, telling her to receive the vow under the Magizha tree. The 253rd and 254th verses are: "When the Lord with matted hair, having blessed the maiden, departed, Sangiliyar, amazed, awoke from her sleep and, remembering the grace of the Lord with red matted hair, could not sleep. She woke up the maidens sleeping nearby and told them, who were still sleepy, all that the Lord, who is known by the letters, had graciously told her in her dream. Hearing her words, the maidens, with fear, wonder, and joy, bowed to Sangiliyar." The Lord with matted hair, having blessed Sangiliyar, the maiden, as mentioned before, departed. Due to the wonder that arose from this event, Sangiliyar wakes up from her sleep and sits up. She comes from the state of sleep to the state of wakefulness. These states are called "svapnam" (dream) and "jagrata" (waking). In that night, the Lord appeared twice in her dream and blessed her. Thinking of this abundant grace, Sangiliyar wakes up from her sleep and sits up. After that, she cannot sleep at all. She sits awake. If one has a good dream, it is customary to stay awake that night, apply sacred ash, and meditate on Lord Shiva. Here too, after the events of the dream, Sangiliyar wakes up from her sleep and, remembering the divine grace of Lord Shiva, stays awake without sleeping. With a doubt as to whether the events she saw in her dream were real, she wakes up her female friends who are sleeping nearby. A dream can occur due to many reasons, such as illness, distorted perception, or past memories. It can be a false vision, or it can be a divine event that makes one wonder if it is real. The great divine dream is such that one wonders if it will yield results as it happened, if it is truly real. There is a great difference between the body in a dream and the body in a waking state. Therefore, doubt usually arises as to what happened in such a way. Here, Sangili Ammaiyar sits amazed, thinking that Lord Shiva appeared twice in her dream in the middle of the night and blessed her. She wakes up all the female friends who are sleeping nearby. Female friends are maidens appointed for service and protection. In Tamil Akapporul (love poetry) tradition, they are called "thozhi" (female friend) or "uyirpangi" (soul-mate). One would not tell others, but one would tell a thozhi, which is a tradition in Tamil literature. It is very important to recall the events seen in a dream in the waking state as they were. Therefore, with a doubt that she might forget all that happened in the dream, she wakes up all the sleeping female friends and tells them all that happened. Sangili Ammaiyar does not wait for the female friends to wake up on their own. She immediately wakes them up and informs them of all that she saw in her dream. In addition, it is now dawn. It is the time to gather flowers for Thiruppalli Ezhuchi (waking up the Lord). Therefore, she also wakes up the female friends for that purpose. Moreover, the Lord has blessed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to fulfill the vow that very night. Therefore, before Nambigal comes to the Thiruvottiyur temple, Sangili Ammaiyar wakes up the sleeping female friends for these and many other reasons, tells them all these stories, and asks them to go there. She tells the female friends who have woken up from sleep, "The Lord of Thiruvottiyur, who is known as 'Ezhuthariyum Peruman' (the Lord who knows the letters) because he wrote the letter 'Ezhuthu', appeared twice in my dream and blessed me." Sangili Ammaiyar tells all that happened to her female friends in detail. Hearing that, all those female friends, with fear, wonder, and joy, bow down to Sangili Ammaiyar. We have already contemplated in detail the story of how the Lord of Thiruvottiyur got the name "Ezhuthariyum Peruman." Such a great Lord blessed her, and when she told her female friends, these are all events about the Lord's acts of grace. Therefore, there was fear from hearing them with awe, and it was a great blessing that was rare to obtain, the power of the Lord's grace. The female friends witnessed it directly, so they were also amazed. In addition, since their mistress was about to get married, they were also joyful because they were going to serve their mistress in that special marriage. The 255th verse says: "When it was the pure time for the maiden to gather flowers for Thiruppalli Ezhuchi, and she went to perform her duty, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, having understood that it was the time to make the vow, came to the front of the temple, looking for her arrival." This is the verse. The female friends go to the temple to gather flowers for Thiruppalli Ezhuchi. In Shiva temples, flowers prescribed for the respective puja (worship) for the respective time should be selected and gathered. This is the time for Thiruppalli Ezhuchi, so flowers suitable for that should be selected and gathered. Therefore, those female friends go to the temple to perform their duty. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is present in front of the temple, waiting for Sangili Ammaiyar to arrive. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, realizing that it is time to fulfill his vow, comes there to make the vow as the Lord had blessed him. The Lord had told Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to do it that very night. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who had agreed to it, comes there, thinking that it is the right time to meet Sangiliyar and make the vow before the night passes. If it is night, Sangili Ammaiyar will be in the maiden's chamber, and he cannot go there. If the night passes, the time given by the Lord will pass. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes to the front of the Lord's temple at that time, which is just before the time of Thiruppalli Ezhuchi, which is between night and dawn. He waits there for Sangili Ammaiyar. Nambigal comes and waits there even before Sangili Ammaiyar arrives. The 256th verse says: "He stood there, and when the maiden with beautiful ornaments came, he went to her side and spoke of the grace of his Lord. The maiden with a slender waist, out of shyness, did not speak her consent directly. She moved aside with her companions and entered the temple." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is waiting there. At that time, Sangili Ammaiyar comes there with her female friends. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes to Sangiliyar's side and tells her all about the grace of the Lord to him. Sangili Ammaiyar, out of great shyness, could not directly express her consent to her husband-to-be. She moved aside with her companions and entered the temple. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes as far as necessary to speak to Sangiliyar, who came before him, so that she can hear him. The Lord had said, "Go to her now, this very night, and make the vow." Therefore, the Lord had informed both of them. So, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal tells Sangiliyar that message. Sangili Ammaiyar should express her consent to that. But Sangili Ammaiyar, out of great shyness, could not express it openly to Nambigal, who was to be her husband. She moved aside with her female friends and hid inside the temple. These are all the expressions that occur in a heroine when she sees her hero: inability to speak, inability to express the words that come, great shyness, covering her eyes, covering her face, and standing with a sense of inadequacy. These are all the expressions that occur in a heroine in the presence of her hero in Akapporul (love poetry) traditions. The 257th, 258th, and 259th verses are continuous verses. The 257th verse says: "There, Aruran (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) followed her and said, 'O maiden with beautiful ornaments, I am here to make a vow that I will not leave you. Come before the Lord with the moon-crowned hair.' And the maidens, who had heard Sangiliyar's dream, replied." This is how the verse is structured. Sangili Ammaiyar goes inside the temple with her female friends. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also follows her and says to Sangili Ammaiyar, "O Sangili, adorned with beautiful ornaments, I am here to make a vow that I will not leave you. Therefore, come before the Lord." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says this to Sangili Ammaiyar. But the female friends, who knew the message that the Lord had told Sangili Ammaiyar in her dream, tell Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. In reality, Sangili Ammaiyar should have replied, but due to shyness, she remains there, and on her behalf, the female friends reply to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. That is the 258th verse. "The maidens said to Vanthondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), 'Our Lord (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) has come for this. Is it proper to make a vow before the Lord of the celestials? O creeper-like maiden, where else can I do it?'" This is how the verse is structured. The female friends of Sangili Ammaiyar say to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Our Lord has come for this. Is it proper to make this vow before the Lord of the celestials, the Lord of the gods? It is not fitting for this small act." They call Sundaramoorthy Swamigal "Our Lord" because they know that he is the husband-to-be of their mistress. Therefore, they say, "Our Lord," and convey this idea to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. "This vow or promise is a very small matter. Is it proper to do this small matter in a very high and sacred place, before the Lord? It is not fitting for that," they say. This is because the Lord had told Sangili Ammaiyar in her dream, "Do not accept this vow before the sanctum sanctorum. Accept it under the Magizha tree." Sangili Ammaiyar had also told this to her female friends. Therefore, this was a strategy adopted by the female friends to make Sundaramoorthy Swamigal make the vow under the Magizha tree. The female friends say that it is not necessary to do this small matter before the Lord, who is in a high and sacred place. To that, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asks, "Then where else can I make the vow?" To that, the female friends reply in the verse: "If the beautiful women refuse, then it is better to agree and decide to go under the Magizha tree." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who asked where he should make the vow, is told by the women that it is enough to make the vow under the Magizha tree. Hearing that, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is confused. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is mentally disturbed. He decides that if he refuses to do as they say, it will become a blame, so he must do as they say, and agreeing to it is appropriate. Why was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal disturbed when he heard this? It was because he had prayed to the Lord, "When I make a vow before your sanctum sanctorum, you should be present under the Magizha tree." He realized that this had become a problem for him due to the Lord's grace to them. He would be blamed for trying to deceive the women. But the Lord is present under the Magizha tree as promised. If he makes a vow before him that he will not leave Sangiliyar, it will hinder his duty of visiting temples and worshipping the Lord. He also thinks this. However, he can somehow obtain the grace of the Lord and the permission to undertake pilgrimages. He can obtain consent from the Lord by praying to him. But now, if he refuses to make this vow, he cannot avoid the blame that will come. Therefore, after considering all this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal agrees to this. Therefore, he says, "I will make the vow under the Magizha tree. You go to the Magizha tree." Sangili Ammaiyar and her female friends go to the Magizha tree, and he also goes there. Now, the 260th verse says: "Before Sangiliyar, who had performed unfailing great penance, the Lord of Munaippadi, the protector of the three worlds, circumambulated the ancient Magizha tree three times and stood there, declaring, 'I will not leave this place.'" This is the verse. Before Sangiliyar, who had performed unfailing great penance, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal makes a vow under the sacred Magizha tree. That sacred Magizha tree is an eternal tree, an imperishable, ancient tree that is always there, even though it is old, it does not perish. He circumambulates it three times, worshipping it. Making a vow is a ritual that is part of the vow. After circumambulating three times, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stands before the Magizha tree and raises his hand, declaring with his mouth, "I will not leave this place." He makes this vow, saying, "I will not go away from here." After he finishes, the hesitation in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal causes external hesitation, which leads to hesitation in his actions, preventing him from performing the physical act quickly. Thus, he gives his vow, his promise, to Sangili Ammaiyar. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 40 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 878, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam.

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal makes a vow under the Magizha tree. The 261st verse:
"மேவியசீர் ஆரூரர் மெய்ச்சபதம் வினை முடிப்ப
காவியினேர் கண்ணாரும் கண்டு மிக மனம் கலங்கி
பாவியேன் இது கண்டேன் தம்பிரான் பணியால் என்று
ஆவிசோர்ந்து அழிவார் அங்கு ஒரு மருங்கு மறைந்து அயர்ந்தார்"

As Nambi Arurar thus fulfilled the vow, Sangiliyar, seeing it, was greatly distressed in her heart. Thinking, "Wretched me, I witnessed this by the command of Thampiran (Lord Shiva)," she became faint-hearted and hid herself in a corner, deeply saddened. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's vow was a true vow, a vow made with his physical body. The truth of the vow lay in his feet walking around, his hands raised, and his mouth uttering the oath. While the external organs of his body thus functioned at their own level, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind was confused and bewildered. It then somewhat recovered and became resolute, yet restless, thus fulfilling the vow. Moreover, the vow made by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was a true vow. As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal wished, the Lord appeared and was seated under the Magizha tree. Therefore, it was a true vow made in the presence of the Lord. Making a vow in the presence of the Lord is an unusual act, done under duress. Sangiliyar lamented, "My Lord, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, did this for my sake. How terrible that I became the cause of it!"

Therefore, she went to a corner, distressed, and hid herself. We only mentioned that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was very happy to be residing in Thiruvarur. We did not say that he sought any remedy for it. But the Lord Himself came and said, "He will make a vow not to leave you." And without agreeing to do it in front of our sacred shrine, the Lord Himself came to Sangiliyamman and said, "You shall receive that vow under the Magizha tree." If the Lord had not commanded thus, Sangiliyar's loving heart would not have insisted and requested this so much. This is the truth. Therefore, the Lord commanded Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to perform this act, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal made that vow, and Sangiliyar witnessed it. This is said to be due to the Lord's supreme grace. The truth is that the Lord, out of His immense grace for both of them, commanded and blessed them thus. Just as a physician gives medicine with insistence and treats a disease with force according to its nature, the Lord has shown His grace here. We must understand this. Even though such actions in this great Puranam may appear ordinary to other worldly people, we must keep in mind that these actions are all acts of grace commanded by the Lord.

The 262nd verse:
"திரு நாவலூராளி தம்முடைய செயல் முற்றி
பொறுநாக புனைந்தார் கோயிலின் உள் புகுந்து இறைஞ்சி
அருள் நாளும் தரையிருந்தீர் செய்தவாறு அழகு இது என
பெருநாமம் எடுத்து ஏத்தி பெருமகிழ்ச்சியுடன் போந்தார்"

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the leader of Thirunavalur, having completed his vow, entered the Lord's temple, worshipped the Lord, and said to Him, "O Lord, who always bestows grace upon me, what you did was beautiful and good." He then praised the Lord by uttering His great name (the sacred five-letter mantra, Thiruvaindheluthu) and departed with great joy. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, realizing by inference that the handmaidens' request to make the vow under the Magizha tree was due to the Lord's prompting, spoke to the Lord thus. He praised the Lord's great name, Sri Panchaksharam, and also expressed a hint of reproach to the Lord as he left. What happened now was grace. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was joyful, concluding that grace would continue to protect him in the events that would follow. He was also joyful because, with the fulfillment of the vow, the marriage would now be completed. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal then departed from the temple.

The 263rd verse:
"வார்புனையும் வனமுலையார் வன்தொண்டர் போனதற்பின்
தார்ப்புனையும் மண்டபத்து தம்முடைய பணி செய்து
கார்ப்புனையும் மணிகண்டர் செயல் கருத்தில் கொண்டு இறைஞ்சி
ஏற்புனையும் கன்னிமாடம் புகுந்தார் இருள் புலர"

After Vantondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) left, Sangiliyamman went to the flower-garland-making hall, completed her service, and then, keeping the deeds of the blue-throated Lord in her mind, worshipped Him and entered the maiden's chamber as dawn broke. Here, according to Sangiliyamman's request, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal fulfilled the vow. Even after these events were completed, Sangiliyamman went into the flower-garland-making hall, completed her regular service of making flower garlands, and then proceeded to the maiden's chamber. Her regular service was the flower-garland service. Ordinary worldly people often neglect their Shiva service and devotee service when they experience worldly joys and sorrows. But Sangiliyar's life was disciplined in Shiva devotion. Therefore, whether joy or sorrow came, whatever happened, she continued her regular flower service. If worldly people were to observe and follow Sangiliyar's disciplined Shiva life, it would bring them immense good. Having completed her service, as darkness faded and morning arrived, she entered the maiden's chamber. The darkness here implicitly refers to this worldly life, which came about due to the events in Thirukailayam. The inner meaning is that she entered the maiden's chamber after the karmic effects of that day were completed.

The 264th and 265th verses:
"அன்று இரவே ஆதிபுரி ஒற்றிக் கொண்டார் ஆட்கொண்ட
பொன் திகழ் வன் தொண்டர் முடித்தருள நின்ற புகழ்
திருவொற்றியூர் நிலவு தொண்டற்கு மன்றல் வினை செய்வதற்கு
மனங்கொள்ள உணர்த்துவார் நம்பி ஆரூரனுக்கு நங்கை சங்கிலிதன்னை
இம்பர் ஞாலத்திடை நம் ஏவலின மனவினை செய்து
உம்பர் வாழும் உலகறிய அழிப்பீர் என்று உணர்த்துதலும்
தம்பிரான் திருத்தொண்டர் அருள் தலைமேல் கொண்டு எழுவார் என்பவை"

That very night, the Lord of Thiruvottriyur, the primordial city, in order to fulfill the marriage desired by Vantondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), whom He had taken as His own, instructed the devotees residing in the glorious Thiruvottriyur. He conveyed to them, "Perform the marriage of Nambi Aruran and Nankai Sangili in this world according to Our command, so that the inhabitants of the celestial world may also know of it." Upon this instruction, the Lord's devotees accepted His grace with reverence and proceeded to act. The Lord thus conveyed His intention to the Shiva devotees residing in Thiruvottriyur. He also made it clear that this was His command. He instructed them to perform the rituals according to the rules of chastity and the scriptures. He also instructed them to do it so that the inhabitants of the celestial world would know of it.

The 266th verse:
"மண்ணிறைந்த பெருஞ்செல்வத் திரு ஒற்றியூர் மண்ணும்
எண்ணிறைந்த திருத்தொண்டர் எழில் பதியோருடன் ஈண்டி
உள் நிறைந்த மகிழ்ச்சியுடன் உம்பர் பூமழை பொழிய
கண்ணிறைந்த பெரும் சிறப்பில் கலியாணம் செய்து அழித்தார்"

In Thiruvottriyur, filled with immense earthly wealth, countless devotees residing there, along with other beautiful people, gathered with hearts full of joy. As the celestials showered flowers like rain, they performed the marriage of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Sangiliyamman with great splendor, filling the eyes with delight.

The 267th verse:
"பண்டு நிகழ் பான்மையில் பசுபதி தன் அருளாலே
வண்டமர் பூங்குழலாரை மழம்புணர்ந்த வன்தொண்டர்
புண்டரிக தவள் வனப்பை புறம் கொண்ட தூ நலத்தை
கண்டு கேட்டு உண்டு உயிர்த்து உற்று அமர்ந்திருந்தார் காதலினால்"

According to the events that transpired before, by the grace of Pasupathi (Lord Shiva), Vantondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), who was united with the lady whose hair was adorned with bees, beheld the pure beauty that surpassed the whiteness of a lotus. He saw, heard, enjoyed, breathed, and was deeply immersed in love. The marriage that took place now was a result of the karmic destiny that arose from the events in Thirukailayam. This marriage was successfully completed by the grace of Pasupathi, Lord Shiva, the leader of all beings. This marriage, which occurred through the grace of the Lord, who sent Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, Anindhithai, and Kamalini, protected them from accumulating further karma, and took them as His own, was thus fulfilled. Vantondar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who married Sangiliyamman, who surpassed the beauty of Lakshmi, lived a marital life with great affection. How was Sundaramoorthy Swamigal before, when he married Paravai Nachiyar, as described in the story of Thaduththaatkonda Puranam? It is said, "For many days, he desired the yoga of the supreme." We should apply the same idea here.

The 268th verse:
"யாழின் மொழி எழில் முறுவல் இரு குழை மேல் கடைபிரழலும்
மாழை விழிவன முளையார் மணி அல்குள் துறை படிந்து
வீழும் அவர்க்கு இடை தோன்றி மிகும் புலவி புணர்ச்சிக்கண்
ஊழியாம் ஒரு கணம்தான் அவ்வூழி ஒரு கணமாம்"

The days when Sundaramoorthy Swamigal lived united with Sangiliyamman, whose speech was as sweet as a lyre, whose smile was beautiful, whose sidelong glances played over the makara earrings in her ears, and whose eyes resembled mango blossoms, were such that a single moment felt like an eon. And an eon felt like a single moment. The marital life of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Sangiliyamman here was not consumed by lust, nor did it lead to the accumulation of further karma. Instead, it was contained within the supreme bliss of the Lord's Shivayoga, and only the physical aspect of karma was expended. Sekkizhar Peruman explains this in the next verse.

The 269th verse:
"இந்நிலையில் பேரின்பம் இனிது அமர்வார் இறைவுரையும்
மண்ணு புகழ் ஒற்றியோர் அதனில் மகிழ் சிறப்பினால்
சென்னி மதிப்புனைவார்த்தம் திருப்பாதம் தொழுது இருந்தார்
முன்னிய காலங்கள் பல முறைமையினால் வந்து அகல என்பது"

Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, residing in Thiruvottriyur, remained in supreme bliss. With the joy and glory of that sacred place, Thiruvottriyur, where the Lord resides with enduring fame, he worshipped the sacred feet of the Lord, who wears the crescent moon on His head. Many past periods came and went in due course. The experience of the state described in the Thirundhiyar Sastram, "The joy received is supreme bliss, and there is no illusion," is what we see in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's experience here. Thirukazhittruppadiyar further explains this: "A yogi with a body, when all small pleasures are absorbed into his supreme bliss, nothing begins, nothing ends, nothing is done by him." And the Thiruviruttham of Sivagnana Siddhiyar states: "Even when lying amidst women with fragrant hair, in the midst of fire-like eyes, those who attain divine knowledge, even in their homes, will worship with folded hands." Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal remained, worshipping the Lord of Thiruvottriyur. Many seasons came and went. It is inferred that these must have been at least three seasons. Considering the events that follow, it is reasonable to assume three seasons. It is known from the subsequent events that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal married Sangiliyamman during the rainy season, and he departed from Thiruvottriyur for Thiruvarur during the spring season. This is because Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, while still in Thiruvottriyur, yearned to see the Vasantha festival in Thiruvarur. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal then departed from Thiruvottriyur. Therefore, the period from the rainy season to the spring season is known to be the time Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stayed there. We will continue to reflect on this.

Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 41 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 879, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal lives in Thiruvottiyur with Sangiliyamman. The 270th verse:

"Poṅku tamiḻ potiyamalai piṟantu pūñcantana ttiṉ
Koṅku aṇaintu kuḷircāral iṭaivaḷarnta koḻum teṉṟal
Aṅku aṇaiya tiruvārūr aṇi vīti aḻakar avar
Maṅkaḷa nāḷ vacantam etirkoṇṭu aruḷum vakai niṉaitār"

Tamil language is "pongu Tamil" (flourishing Tamil). It is a language that is always in common use among people, without stagnation in its growth, and without hindrances such as not adhering to grammatical rules. It is a Tamil that grows richer and richer. This is a unique feature of the Tamil language, not found in other languages. The mountain where the Tamil sage Agastya, the abode of such a flourishing Tamil, resides, is the Podigai mountain. It is the mountain of the Tamil sage. From that Podigai mountain, which is full of sandalwood forests, the southern breeze (thendral kaatru) originates. The southern breeze, mingling with the fragrance of sandalwood trees, grows in the cool, watery slopes, and is a rich breeze. The southern breeze, originating from the Podigai mountain, passes through other cold mountainsides, watery groves, and the regions of the Kaveri river, and then reaches Thiruvarur, and finally Thiruvottiyur. The time when the southern breeze comes is the Vasantha Kalam (spring season). During this spring season, in Thiruvarur, the beautiful Veethi Vidanga Peruman (Lord Thyagaraja) celebrates the auspicious Vasantha festival with great splendor. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal remembers that Veethi Vidanga Peruman will hold court during that Vasantha festival. Veethi Vidanga Peruman, the beautiful Lord of the streets of Thiruvarur, is Thyagesa Peruman. During the Vasantha festival in the spring season, on the sixth day, Thyagesa Peruman will hold court with great splendor. Therefore, when the southern breeze comes to Thiruvottiyur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal remembers the Vasantha festival of Thiruvarur. The state of mind of great people is such that all worldly experiences remind them of the Lord, connecting them to the Lord. In this state, the characteristic of Shivayoga is to practice seeing all enjoyable things in the world as related to Shiva. This practice is very beneficial. Here, we understand that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also sees and enjoys all worldly experiences as related to Shiva. The 271st verse:

"Veṇmatiyiṉ koḻuntu aṇinta vīti viṭaṅka perumāṉ
Oṉ mutalār puṭaippaṟanta ōlakkam ataṉ iṭaiyē
Paṇṇamarum moḻi paṟavaiyār pāṭal āṭal taṉai
Kaṇṇuṟa muṉkaṇṭu kēṭṭārpōla karutiṉār"

Veethi Vidanga Peruman (Thyagesa Peruman), adorned with the crescent moon, holds court. In the assembly hall where Thyagesa Peruman holds court during the Vasantha festival, Paravai Nachiyar's singing and dancing take place. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal imagines as if he is seeing and hearing Paravaiyar's dance and song in the assembly hall, surrounded by beautiful women with lovely foreheads. When the southern breeze, which comes before the Lord of Arur in Thiruvarur, came to mind, the sight of Paravaiyar's dance and song in the assembly where he was seated also came to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's mind. To Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, with his focused attention, that scene appeared as if it were actually happening. Now, the 272nd and 273rd verses:

"Pūṅkōyil amarntārai puṟṟiṭam koṇṭu iruntārai
Nīṅkāta kātaliṉāl niṉaintārai niṉaivārai
Pāṅkāka tām muṉpu paṇiyavarum payaṉ uṇarvār
Īṅku nāṉ maṟantēṉ eṉṟu ēcaravāl mika aḻivār
Miṉṉoḷir ceñcaṭaiyāṉai vēta mutal āṉāṉai
Maṉṉa pukaḻ tiruvārūr makiḻntāṉai mika niṉaintu
Paṉṉiya col pattimaiyum aṭimaiyaiyum kaiviṭuvāṉ
Eṉṉum icai tiruppatikam eṭuttu iyaṃpi iṟaṅkiṉār"

Sundaramoorthy Swamigal now thinks of Putridam Kondanathar (Lord of the Anthill), who resides in the flower temple of Thiruvarur, who makes the anthill his abode, and who is the Moolasthana Nathar (presiding deity) in Thiruvarur. The Lord mentioned in the previous verses was Thyagesa Peruman. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who is accustomed to worshipping and praising the Lord who resides in the anthill with unceasing great love, and who experiences the great benefit of Shivanantham (bliss of Shiva) that comes from remembering those devotees who remember Him, is now greatly distressed, thinking, "I have forgotten that Lord of Thiruvarur and am staying here!" In this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman praises Nambiyarurar, saying, "He remembers those who remember Him with unceasing love." This means that the Lord remembers those devotees who remember Him with unceasing great love. If the Lord remembers all beings in general and bestows grace, then this means that the Lord specifically remembers those who remember Him continuously with great love.

The truth stated in the tenth Sutra of Sivagnana Bodham is, "It is the duty of the leader to protect those who seek refuge, and the devotee who seeks refuge, without deceit, and whose karma is destroyed by the Lord Himself, as examined by the wise." The Thirumudukunram Thevaram states, "He who is always present in the hearts of devotees who praise Him without forgetting the tantra." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal remembers the Shivanantha Bhogam (bliss of Shiva) he received by worshipping and praising such a Lord in the past. He grieves, "I have forgotten such a Lord and am staying here!" Sundaramoorthy Swamigal never forgot the Lord of Thiruvarur.
