Therefore, the Lord has performed a divine play as if He had done so. Just as He forcibly took him as His own in Thiruvennainallur that day, so too today, the Lord, who has come with grace, has returned victorious after going as a messenger, says Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, worshipping Him.

The 352nd verse: "Am mozhi vilambum Nambikaiyar tham aruli seivar ni solla nam poi paravai than illam nanni kommai vem mulaiyinalkku un thiram ellam kura kollal vemmai than solli name vendavum maruthal endrar." This verse comes as the Lord's statement. The Lord addresses Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, saying, "Sundara, as you requested, we went to Paravai's house and told her all about you. However, she did not accept it. Even though I spoke sweet words that she would like, and even though I myself pleaded, she refused." The Lord says, "Paravai refused even when I pleaded with her."

The 353rd and 354th verses: "Annalar aruli seiya kettarurar thamum thunnena nadukkamutre thozhudhu neer aruli seidha vannamum adiyal ana paravaiyo maruppal nangal ennalar adimaikku enbadhu indru arivithir endru vanavar uyya vendi marikadal nandri undir than avar purangal veva muvarai thavirthu atkondir nan marai siruvarkkaga kalanai kaindhu nattir yan miga umakku indru anal en seivir podhadhendrar." As soon as Sundaramoorthy Swamigal heard what the Lord had said, he trembled with shock. All the joy he had felt earlier, thinking that the message had been successful, immediately changed, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred body trembled very quickly. He bowed down to the Lord and asked Him, "Oh Lord, did Paravai, your devotee, refuse what you said? You have made us realize today that we are not worthy of being considered your servants." He says, "We are no longer in your thoughts. We have lost the right to be your servants. You have made us realize this today." The reason for Paravai's refusal was that she did not know that the one who came was Lord Shiva Himself. The subtlety here is that the reason for her ignorance is that the Lord did not consider her His devotee. Because He did not consider her a devotee, He did not make her agree. The Lord did not fulfill my wish either. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, including Paravai Ammaiyar, complains to the Lord, "We are not worthy of being considered servants." He says, "Until now, we thought we were your devotees and servants. You have made us realize that this is wrong." This is a statement of anger and complaint made to the leader out of love and right. Therefore, we should not take this meaning literally. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal further complains, "Oh Lord, you consumed the great poison that arose from the churning ocean to save the Devas (celestial beings). When you burned the three cities of the Asuras (demons), you saved only the three devotees who were there and took them as your own. To protect Markandeya, the young boy who was skilled in the four Vedas, you kicked Yama (the god of death) and made Markandeya your servant. If I have become an excess to you today, who are so greatly compassionate, what else can I do?" Sundaramoorthy Swamigal pleads. The Lord consuming poison, the Lord saving only three Shiva devotees when burning the cities of the Asuras, and kicking Yama for Markandeya – these three divine events explain the Lord's overflowing compassion to us and are divine plays that show that we are all His devotees and that it is the Lord's nature to protect us. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal laments, saying, "If you, who have such a nature, abandon me, then your decision is that I am an excess to you." Here, the devotees who were in the three cities were Paramavirathan, Paramayogan, and Paramagunan. They are also called Susilan, Subudhi, and Sudhanma. It is said that the Lord burned the three cities, leaving only these.

The 355th verse: "Avadhe seithir indru en adimai neer vendavittal paviyin thanai andru valiya atkonda patru en novum en azhivum kandir nudangu idai aval pal indru mevudhal seiyir agil vidum uyir endru vizhndhar." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal continues to complain to the Lord, "Oh Lord, today you do not desire my servitude. If you do not desire my servitude today, why did you forcibly take me, a sinner who was not worthy of servitude, as your own that day in Thiruvennainallur, even after I refused? You see my suffering and my sorrow. If you do not make me go to her, whose waist is slender, today, my life will leave me without my effort." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal falls at the Lord's feet.

The 356th verse: "Thambiran adhanai kandu thariyadhu thalarndhu vizhndha Nambiyai arulal nokki nam innum aval pal poi ak kombinai ippodhe kuruguma kurugindrom vemburu thuyar neengu endrar vinaiyellam vilaikkavallar." The Lord, seeing Nambi (Sundarar) who had fallen at His feet due to weakness, unable to bear it, looks at him with grace. This is a look of grace, a divine look that removes distress and saves. Looking with grace, He says to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "We will go to her again and tell that maiden to go to you right now. Remove your suffering." Is He not the Lord who can bring about all actions? Is He not the Lord who can bring about all karmic fruits to living beings? The divine play of Lord Shiva is to make each person experience their own karma and to observe it. He is capable of bringing about the fruits of their actions to each person in a suitable manner. "Vallargal valla vagaiyal thozhil puridhal ellam udane orungisaindhu sellum kalai muttamal seivinaiyum muchai vinaiyum selavu pattole theettum padi potri" (All the actions performed by the capable ones, in their own capable ways, when they all come together at once, without hindrance, the actions done and the actions yet to be done, are all recorded in the scrolls of praise). We see these sacred words in the treatise called "Potri Paguradai." We can learn more about the Lord's ability to bring about actions in the Meykanda Gnana Noolgal (Meykanda philosophical texts). We can learn them as teachings from the Gurunathars (spiritual masters) who teach Siddhanta. Thus, the Lord speaks. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 57 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 895, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. The Lord blessed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, saying, "We will go to Paravai again as a messenger and tell her to go to you right now. Remove your suffering."

The 357th and 358th verses: "Mayangiya nanbar uya vakku enum madhura vaaimai nayankizhar amudham nalka navalur mannarthamum kalakkam neekki um adithozhumbanenai payam keduthu ivvar endro panikkolvadhu endru potru anbar mel karunai kura andavar meendum sella pinbu sendru irainji Nambi pedhuravodu meendar munbu udan podhadharum muraimaiyil sevitthu eka ponpuri sadaiyar madhar punitha maligaiyil sendrar." To save Nambi (Sundarar), his confused friend, the Lord bestowed the sweet, truthful, and joyful nectar of His sacred words. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the great one of Thirunavalur, also said to the Lord, "You have removed my sorrow and the confusion that caused it. Is this not how you should accept me, your servant at your feet, by removing my fear?" He praises Him. The Lord, with overflowing compassion for His devotee Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, goes as a messenger again. After He goes as a messenger, Nambi goes a short distance, bows down, and returns with confusion. The verse says, "Pedhuravodu" (with confusion). "Pedhuravu" means a state of distress where one cannot decide what to do. Earlier, when the Lord went as a messenger, only a few worthy ones accompanied Him, while others stayed behind, as we saw. Now, even those who did not accompany Swamigal earlier are going. Earlier, the Lord went in the guise of a priest. Now, the Lord goes in His true form, with His original sacred body. Therefore, even those who did not accompany Him before are now going with the Lord. Thus, the Lord goes to Paravai Ammaiyar's sacred mansion. It is not just once, but twice that the Lord has graced this mansion, making it a sacred mansion, as praised by Sekkizhar Peruman.

Sekkizhar Peruman, who beautifully described the Lord's visit to Paravai Ammaiyar's mansion in eight verses the previous time, now describes the Lord's visit to Paravai's mansion in a single verse, indicating the urgency to remove Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's suffering.

The 359th, 360th, and 361st verses, which are a continuous sequence of verses: "Madhi nudhal paravaiyar tham maraiyavar pona pinbu mudhir marai muniyai vandhar aruludai mudhalavaragum adhisayam palavum thondra arivutre anji ketten edhir mozhi embiran mun en seiya maruthen enbar kan thuyil eydhar veyya kaiyaravu eydhi ingu indru andarthambirar thozharkkaga archippar ko kolam kondu anaindha avarai yan ulkondilen paviyen endru on sudar vayile parthu ulaiyarodu azhiyum podhil veriyuru kondrai veni vimalarum thamam thanmai arivuru kolathodum alavil pal puthanadhar sirivuru thevar yogar munivargal suzhndhu sella maruvil seer paravaiyar tham maligai pugundhar vandhu." Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing about Paravai Ammaiyar's mental state. After the Lord, who had come in the guise of a Vedic sage, a Siva Acharya, left, divine indications appeared in Paravai Ammaiyar's sacred heart. Many wonders appeared in Paravai Ammaiyar's sacred heart, indicating that the one who had come in the guise of an ancient Vedic sage was indeed the gracious Lord, Lord Shiva Himself. This is because Paravai Ammaiyar recalls His appearance, His conduct, the time of His arrival, the words He spoke, and the light of His sacred form. All these remind Paravai Ammaiyar of the divine indication in her sacred heart. Realizing this, she is afraid and thinks, "I am ruined! I spoke against my Lord. I spoke words of refusal against His sacred words!" She laments. Therefore, she is sleepless and distressed. She is filled with sorrow, thinking, "Today, the Lord of the Devas Himself came here for His friend Nambi, in the guise of a priest. I, a sinner, could not recognize Him as the Lord!" She looks at the bright entrance of her mansion and laments with her companions. At that moment, the pure Lord, with matted locks adorned with fragrant konrai flowers, came to Paravai Ammaiyar's mansion in His true form, so that everyone who saw Him would know that He was Lord Shiva Himself. He was surrounded by countless hosts of Bhootas (divine attendants), many Devas, Yogis, and Munis. Lord Shiva, unlike the first time when He came in the guise of a priest so that no one could recognize Him as the Lord, now comes in His true divine form.

The 362nd verse: "Paridathalaivar munnam palkana nadhar devar nervuru munivar sitthiyakkargal niraidhalale perarulalar eydhappetra maligai than thenpal seer valar kaiyilai velli thirumalai pondradhu andre." Because Lord Shiva Himself had arrived there, Paravai Ammaiyar's mansion appeared like Mount Kailash in the south. Because the Bhoota Ganas (hosts of divine attendants), starting with the leaders of the Bhootas, and various Gana Nathas, Devas, righteous Munis, Siddhas, and Yakshas were present, the mansion that had the blessing of the greatly compassionate Lord Shiva's arrival appeared like the silver sacred mountain of Kailash, which is rich in glory, in the southern direction.

The 363rd verse: "Aiyar angu anaindha podhil akilalokathullarun eydhiye serindhu suzha edhirkonda paravaiyartham meyyuru nadukkathodu mikka azhum magizhchi pongas seiyathaazhinai mun sera viraivinal sendru vizhndhar." When the Lord arrived there, surrounded by all the beings of all the worlds, Paravai Ammaiyar, who went to meet Him, trembled physically, and her heart overflowed with immense joy. She rushed forward to reach His sacred feet and fell at the Lord's feet. Although she trembled because of the offense of having spoken against Him and refused Him when He came in the guise of a priest earlier, she also overflowed with great joy because Lord Shiva, out of great compassion for her, had come again. She fell down and worshipped Him.

The 364th verse: "Ari ayarkku ariyarthamum ayizhaiyarai nokki urimaiyal uran eva meelavum unpal vandhom murugalar kuzhalai innammunbu pol maradhe ninpal pirivura varundhugindran varapperavendum endrar." The Lord, who is unknown even to Vishnu and Brahma, looked at Paravai Ammaiyar and addressed her, saying, "Paravai, by the right of friendship, Sundaran (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) has sent us, and we have come to you again. Oh maiden with fragrant locks, do not refuse as you did before. Sundaran, who is greatly suffering from separation from you, must come here to you. Please give your consent for that," says Lord Shiva to Paravai Ammaiyar.

The 365th and 366th verses: "Perundadankanninarum piran munbu migavum anji varundhiya ullathodu malarkaram kuzhal mel konde arunthiru maraiyuragi anaindhir mun adiyen seidha irundhavappayan am enna eydhiya niro enbar thulivalar kannir vara thozhudhu vinnappam seivar olivalar seiya padham varundha oru iravu maradhazhi varum anbarkkaga angodu engu uzhalviragi ezhivaruvirum anal en seiken isaiyadhendrar." Paravai Ammaiyar, with her large, beautiful eyes, greatly afraid before the Lord and with a distressed heart, with her flower-like hands clasped above her hair, pleads at the Lord's feet, saying, "Oh Lord, is it because of the great penance I performed earlier that you have graced this humble one? Earlier, you came as a rare sacred Brahmin. Now, you have come here again. What is this compassion you have for me?" She asks and pleads. With tears flowing from her eyes, she bows down to the Lord and pleads, "Oh Lord, your radiant sacred feet are suffering, wandering here and there all night for your devotee. If you, who are so rare, come as an easy-to-reach one for your devotee, what can I do but agree to what you say?" Paravai Ammaiyar pleads, "When you, the rare Lord, come as an easy-to-reach one for your devotee, it is proper and fitting for me to agree for the sake of that same devotee."

The 367th verse: "Nangal mangai nin thanmaikku erkum nanmaiye mozhindhai endru mangaiyor pagam vaitha vallalar viraivandu poga thingal van mudhalinarum sendru pin irainji meenda engalai alum Nambi thoodhar meendu ekugindrar." "Oh woman, you have spoken good words, befitting your greatness," says Lord Shiva, praising Paravai Ammaiyar. Is He not the benevolent one who holds a maiden as half of His form? The Lord holds Uma Devi (Goddess Uma) as half of His form. Uma Ammaiyar is the very grace of the Lord, the very compassion of the Lord. Therefore, the Lord is benevolent and compassionate. Such a Lord praises Paravai Ammaiyar thus and takes leave from there. Paravai Ammaiyar also follows the Lord, bows down, and returns to her mansion. The messenger of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who saves us all, returns to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 58 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 896, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam.

The 368th and 369th verses: "Adhiyum melum mal ayan nadarkku aruladhar thoodhinil eki thondarai alum thozhil kande vidhiyil adi padi magizhndhemidai gindrar puthiyil nidum palkananadhar pugazh virar annavar munnum pinnum marungum anaivu eydha minnidayar pal anbarai uykkum viraivodum senniyil nidum gangai thadhumba thiruvarur mannavanar ammaraiyavanar pal vandhutrar." The Bhoota Ganas (hosts of divine attendants) and the heroic Bhootas, seeing the Lord Shiva, who is the Supreme Being beyond the reach of Vishnu and Brahma even when they searched for His feet and crown, going as a messenger and taking His devotee Sundarar as His own, dance and sing with great joy in the sacred street of Thiruvarur where the Lord walked. The reason Vishnu and Brahma could not see Him was that they were not His devotees, and therefore the Lord did not grace them. Here, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was a devotee, so the Lord graced him. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to these Bhoota Ganas as "Puthiyil nidum palkananadhar" (the many Gana Nathas who are immersed in Puthi). "Puthi" means spiritual experience, and here it refers to Shivananubhuti (the experience of Shiva). "Nidudhal" (being immersed) means being constantly absorbed in Shivananubhuti in a yogic state. Therefore, these Bhoota Gana Nathas experienced their inner Shivananubhuti outwardly and rejoiced. As they surrounded Him, coming and going in front, behind, and on the sides in the sacred street of Thiruvarur, the Lord, with the Ganga overflowing on His sacred head, came to Sundarar, the primordial devotee, with the urgency to unite His devotee Sundarar with Paravai Ammaiyar, whose waist is like lightning. The Lord comes to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to convey the news that His mission as a messenger has been successfully completed.

The 370th verse: "Anbarum enpal avi alikkum padi ponar en seidhu meelvar innamum endru idarkura ponpuri munul marbinar sella polividhi munburam nerum kanninai thanum mugilara." The devotee Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, without blinking his eyes, keeps looking at the street from which the Lord will return. He thinks, "The Lord, who went a second time as a messenger to give me back my life, what will He do and return now?" With increasing sorrow, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal stands, unblinking, looking at the sacred street from which the Lord will come.

Now, the 371st, 372nd, and 373rd verses, which are a continuous sequence of three verses: "Annilamai kan manmadhan vazhikku azhivar tham mannu uyir nalkum tham perumanar vandhu eydha mun edhir sendre muvulagum sendradaiyum thazh senniyil vaithu en solluvar endre theliyadhar embiranir en nuyir kavadhidhar seiyum kombanaiyal pal en kodu vandhir kurai enna thamperumanum thazhkuzhal setram thani vitthom Nambi ini poi matra aval thanpal nanugu enna Nandhi piranar vandhu arul seiya nalam eydhum sindhaiyularvam kur kali eydhi thigazhgindrar bandhamum vedum nir arul seiyumbadi seithir endhai piranane en ini enpal idar endrar." As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was thus agonizing, the Lord came and appeared before him. His Lord, who bestows life, came before him. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal went to meet Him, placed the feet of the Lord, which are worshipped by all three worlds, on his head, and, uncertain of what the Lord would say, pleaded with the Lord, "Oh Lord, what deficiency have you brought from Paravai, who causes me sorrow without protecting my life?" To this, our Lord, the great Lord, says to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Sundara, we have appeased Paravai's anger that arose from her sulking. Nambi, now go and join her." When the Lord, whose sacred name is Nandi, thus bestowed His grace, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's heart was filled with intense desire and joy, and he shone with delight. At that moment, he praised the Lord, saying, "Oh my Lord, you have made it possible for me to receive both bondage and liberation. What more sorrow can I have now?"

The 374th verse: "Endru adi vizhum nanbardham anbukku ezhivandhar sendru anai ni achseiyizhai pal endru arul seidhu vendruyar semel vidanga perumal tham pondhigal vayil koyil pugundhar puvi vazha." As soon as the Lord thus spoke, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal fell at the Lord's feet. The Lord, who came easily for the love of His friend Nambi, who fell at His feet, said to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Go and join that maiden." Having thus bestowed His grace, Vidanga Perumal, the Tyagesa Perumal, ascended His majestic bull and entered His sacred temple in Thiruvarur, which has a golden entrance, so that this world may flourish.

Now, the 375th, 376th, 377th, 378th, and 379th verses, which are a continuous sequence of five verses: "Thambiranar pin sendru thazhndhu ezhundhu arulal meelvar embiran vallavar en eydhiya magizhchiyodum avambalar kuzhalar semban maligai vayil nokki Nambi Arurar kadhal nayandhu ezhundhu arul podhu mun thuyil unarndhu suzhndha parijana marungum vaippa min thigazh polampu mari vinnavar pozhindhu vazhtha mandral sei madhura seetha sikaram kondu mandha thendralum edhirkondu eydhum sevaga munbu katta malai than kalavai seru man madha sandhu pongum kolanar pasum karpuram kungumam mudhalayulla salu meykalan kooda sattum punadai varkkam palana piravum endhum parijanam munbu sella ivvagai ivar vandhu eydha eydhiya viruppinodu maivalar nedumkannarum maligai adaiya mannum seivinai alangaraathu sirappu ani palavum seidhu neyvalar vilakku dhuba niraikkuda niraithu pinnum pumali narum pothamam punaimanikkova nattri kamar porchunnam visi kamizh narum sandhu nivi thumalar vidhi suzhndha thogaiyar vazhtha thamum mamani vayil munbu vandhu edhiretru nindrar." The Lord thus bestowed His grace. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal followed the Lord, who had entered His sacred temple, for a short distance, then bowed down, received His gracious leave, and returned. With great joy, thinking, "What is the all-powerful nature of my Lord!", Nambi Arurar, with great love, proceeded towards the entrance of Paravai Ammaiyar's mansion, whose hair is adorned with fragrant flowers. At that moment, all of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's attendants, who had been sleeping until then, now woke up and surrounded Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The Devas showered flowers from the sky and blessed him. The gentle southern breeze, carrying sweet-smelling, cool droplets of water, came to meet Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The attendants went ahead, carrying garlands, cool blended pastes, musk-mixed sandalwood paste, beautiful green camphor, kumkum, and other such items, as well as ornaments that adorn the body, silk garments, and other things. Paravai Ammaiyar, with great desire, also beautifully decorated her entire mansion. She arranged rows of ghee lamps, incense, and full pots. She hung fragrant flower garlands and strings of jewels, sprinkled golden powder, applied fragrant sandalwood paste, and as her companions around her, whose street was filled with pure flowers, blessed her, she herself came to the entrance of her great jewel-adorned mansion and stood there to welcome him. We saw that the attendants who accompanied Sundaramoorthy Swamigal carried garlands, silk garments, and ornaments. It is said in historical accounts that among these attendants were a hunchback and a blind man, and that they carried garlands and betel leaves. It was because they had the good fortune to carry these that, by the grace of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, one was cured of his hunchback and the other regained his eyesight. We see this in the verses of Thiruvandhadhi and in the Puranam of Kootruvar. Thus, when Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrived at the entrance of Paravai Ammaiyar's mansion, Paravai Ammaiyar, who had been waiting until then, saw Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, went to meet him, and welcomed him inside. Sekkizhar Peruman beautifully presents these events, which occurred simultaneously on both sides due to love, as a continuous, unified narrative in these five verses, without any separation. In the fourth verse, we see the poetic beauty of combining the actions of both. Here, we see Sekkizhar Peruman, with his divine poetic skill, beautifully explains the state of overflowing love in both of them after the sulking was resolved.

The 380th verse: "Vandulankuzhalar munbu vanthondar vandhu kooda kandapodhu ullam kadhal vellathin karai kanadhu konda acham kura vananga akkuri silum sengai patri kondu maligaiyul sarnthar." When Vantondar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) came and met Paravai Ammaiyar, whose hair was adorned with fragrant flowers, her heart, seeing him, could not find the shore of the flood of love. With increased modesty and fear, she bowed down. That great one (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) also took her cool, tender, red hands and entered the mansion. Therefore, the four feminine qualities of modesty, etc., are revealed here, which is a significant point.

The 381st verse: "Iruvarum thambiranar tham idai adis seidha thiruvarutkarunai vellath thirathinai potri sindhai maruviya inba vellathu azhundhiya punarchi vayppa oruvarul oruvar me nilaiyil uyir onru anar." Both Nambi (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) and Paravai Ammaiyar praised the power of the overflowing flood of divine grace that Tyagesa Perumal, their Lord, had bestowed upon them, coming between them. In that state of union, after the sulking was resolved, what happened to both of them was the praise of the Lord's grace. This shows that both of them, immersed in the love of servitude to the Lord, lived a life beyond their own control. Thus, immersed in the flood of joy that filled their minds, they became one in spirit, each dwelling in the other. This merging of their spirits is a spiritual union. Thus, Paravai Ammaiyar and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal were united there. In those days, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, whose chest was adorned with garlands of lotus flowers and jewel necklaces, worshipped and praised the Lord of Thiruvarur, who resides in the anthill within the sacred temple where the Vedas originate, and whose matted locks are adorned with the Ganga, without ceasing. He offered sacred verses, which are like garlands, that beautify the world, and lived happily in those days. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 897, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam.

Chapter 59 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 897, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Our Lord Shiva went as a messenger to Paravai Ammaiyar for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and united them. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, residing in Thiruvarur, worships Thyagesa Peruman daily and is composing numerous Thiruppathigams (hymns). At this point, the sacred history of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar begins. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, hearing that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sent Shiva as a messenger to Paravai Ammaiyar, becomes angry with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The Lord resolves this anger and fosters friendship between Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. We are now about to see that history.

Verse 383:
"Nambi Arurar, without any tremor in his heart,
Stood and sent his Lord as a messenger to a woman.
Hearing this extraordinary news, Eyarkonar
Became enraged, astonished, and fearful, and began to speak."

Nambi Arurar, that is, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, without the slightest tremor in his mind or fear, stood and sent his Lord, Shiva, as a messenger to a woman. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar hears this news, which is considered a widespread disgrace in this world. Considering this news to be a disgrace, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar hears it and his mind boils with anger. He is astonished and afraid. If one performs an inappropriate act, the mind trembles. This comes from fearing evil deeds. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar thinks that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal did this without any such mental tremor. That a devotee, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, sent his Lord, Shiva, as a messenger is another great mistake. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar considers this news to be a disgrace. He is distressed and agitated, wondering if such an improper act could be done. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar becomes very angry.

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is surprised that this news, the news of sending the Lord as a messenger, is something unheard of until now. He fears that it is improper even to think or hear of it. He felt fear about sending a messenger to a woman. He felt astonishment that the Lord went as a messenger at the command of a devotee. He felt fear about a devotee commanding the Lord. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, experiencing all these emotions, begins to speak further.

Verse 384:
"To say that a devotee commanding the Lord is good,
And that one who does this is a devotee, what a sin!
Even after hearing this unbearable mistake with my ears,
Why does my life not depart, I, a demon?"

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says: "To say that a devotee commanding the Lord is a very good and appropriate act, and that one who dares to do this is called a devotee—what a sin! I am a demon, and that is why, even after hearing this great, unbearable mistake, this great offense to Shiva, with my ears, my life still does not depart." "Peyanen" (பேயனேன்) means one who has the nature of a demon. The nature of a demon is to appear empty, without true feeling. Here, it is said that remaining alive and bearing a body even after hearing such a great offense to Shiva, which is improper to hear, is the demonic lack of true feeling. They say there are three types of love: superior love, middle love, and inferior love. The ability of superior love is that life departs the moment one hears something improper about that which is dearer than one's own life. Therefore, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, saying that his life has not departed even after hearing this news and that he is still alive, degrades himself by saying, "I am a demon."

Verse 385:
"Driven by love for a woman, at someone's command,
His sacred lotus feet, walking on earth, became sore.
Going and coming through the chariot-laden streets,
He, the unique Lord, toiled as a messenger all night long."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says that due to love for a woman, at someone's command, the Lord's beautiful lotus feet became sore from walking on the earth. Thus, the unique Lord went and came through the chariot-laden streets, toiling as a messenger all night long. The streets of Thiruvarur are chariot-laden streets. The Lord should travel in chariots, Airavatham (Indra's elephant), Rishabam (Nandi), and other vehicles there. The streets of Thiruvarur are not known for the Lord walking on the ground. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar laments that such a Lord was made to walk all night long. "Walking all night long" indicates an extension of time. "Walking through the streets of Thiruvarur" indicates an extension of place. "Walking until his lotus feet became sore" indicates an extension of effort. "Toiling as a messenger, going and coming" indicates an extension of action and a degradation of the action. Thus, this beautiful verse is structured.

Verse 386:
"If the Lord, out of compassion, did not act as a servant,
Even Indra, Vishnu, and Brahma cannot fully comprehend Him.
If our Lord consented, could He be commanded?
My heart does not tremble at this; when will I see him?"

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says: "Even if that Lord, being greatly compassionate, came here because He could not bear the suffering of His devotee, and even if our Lord, whom Indra, Vishnu, and Brahma cannot fully comprehend, consented to this, can He be commanded in such a way? My heart does not tremble at this; when will the day come when I see him who does not tremble at this sinful act?"

Verse 387:
"If I see him who, for the sake of a woman,
Sent the supreme Lord as a messenger at night,
What will befall me? He spoke with intense anger,
His heart swelling as if about to burst."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, with a heart swelling as if about to burst with intense anger, thinks: "If I see him who, for the sake of a woman, sent the supreme Lord Shiva as a messenger at night, what will befall me?" Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is agitated, wondering what will happen if he goes and sees Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who sent the Lord as a messenger, or if Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes here and he happens to see him. This is because, when one witnesses an offense to Shiva, the rule is to kill the perpetrator, or if that is not possible, to die oneself, or to leave that place. Therefore, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar implicitly states that he considers killing to be the decisive action. However, it is not explicitly stated whom he intends to kill here. But we will see in the coming section that Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar decides to kill himself. The upcoming part of the story will be that Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, hearing the news that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is coming to meet him, kills himself before seeing Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Sekkizhar Peruman, the divine Sekkizhar, states this as Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's own words. Therefore, words that arise from pure love become true words and then manifest as reality. This is the rule understood here.

Verses 388 and 389 are two continuous verses:
"Eyarkonar, of boundless fame, thought thus.
Hearing that he received this boon, and accepting his mistake,
He sought a remedy for this from the Lord with matted hair adorned with beautiful Konrai flowers,
And worshipped Him daily. The Lord, seeing this,
Desired that the two great devotees should unite.
He bestowed upon Eyarkonar, the leader, a wasting pain in his body,
A colic, to make him suffer."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, of boundless fame, thinks thus. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal realizes, through divine revelation, that Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is angry with him in this way. The elders say that it is a great blessing for someone to have their thoughts about us enter the minds of great people, and that this is the way for them to be reformed. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal felt proud that even though Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar was angry with him, he had thought about him in his sacred heart, considering it a great boon. And Sundaramoorthy Swamigal accepts that what he did was indeed a mistake. This magnanimity of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal serves as a guide to the world. If people follow this appropriately, they too will benefit. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal accepts that he made a mistake. He also thinks that he needs atonement for that mistake. "Prayaschittam" (பிராயச்சித்தம்) is a Sanskrit word; "theervu" (தீர்வு) or "kazhuvaa" (கழுவா) are its Tamil equivalents. This harm, this error, has occurred. Therefore, what needs to be done now, what can be done, is atonement. This is an offense to Shiva, so unlike other sins, it is a great offense of a unique nature. Therefore, the offense to Shiva must be removed, and the atonement for it must be a very great atonement. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who was taken into service by the Lord, has the rule of asking the Lord for everything he desires. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also knows that the scriptures state that there is no atonement for an offense to Shiva. No atonement has been prescribed for an offense to Shiva other than the grace of Shiva. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal appeals to the Lord with matted hair. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worships and praises the Lord daily, seeking atonement. In "Atmartha Puja" (ஆன்மார்த்த பூஜை), one can appeal to one's Lord for what one desires, and there are two traditions: one should worship without asking for anything and offer the fruit of the worship to Him. These are called "Kamiyam" (காமியம்) and "Nishkamiyam" (நிஷ்காமியம்). The Shivagamas (சிவாகமங்கள்) prescribe that at the end of the "Layanga Puja" (லயாங்க பூஜை) section, within the "Shivapuja" (சிவபூஜை) that includes "Layangam" (லயாங்கம்) and "Bhogangam" (போகாங்கம்), one can appeal for what one desires. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal daily appealed to his Lord, the Lord of Arur. We saw in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman that Thilagavathi Ammaiyar, the elder sister of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, appealed many times for her younger brother, Marulneekiyar, to be rescued from the foreign religion. We can recall that history here. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal daily appeals to the Lord for that atonement. The Lord also consents. The Lord desires that His two long-standing devotees, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, should unite in friendship and be joyful. These two are devotees who have long endured in love. "Enduring in love" means that their love for the Lord is the sole reason for their love to grow more and more. The anger and disagreement of Kalikkamar Nambigal, born into a high lineage, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's acceptance of it, all happened solely due to their deep love for the Lord. We must understand this here. Therefore, both are devotees who have long endured in love, who have stood firm in their devotion for a long time. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is a Shivavediyar (சிவவேதியர்) who serves from generation to generation and also performed service in Thirukailayam (திருக்கயிலாயம்). Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is one who lives a Shiva-centric life, adhering to the lineage that says, "My father, my father's father, and all our lineage, You are our Lord." Therefore, both are long-standing in love. The Lord desires that these two should unite in friendship. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal desires to befriend Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar holds aversion and anger towards Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Therefore, the Lord desires that not only Sundaramoorthy Swamigal but also Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar should have friendship and love for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Thus, the Lord, having consented, bestows upon Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar a colic (சூலை நோய்) that causes his body to waste away. The Lord afflicts Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar with colic, without changing the state of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who admitted his mistake. If one asks "why?", the sacred words of Pillaiyar in Thevaram, "The manner in which He bestows grace upon His servants and His primordial greatness are immeasurable; there is no need to speak of them," state that it is divine grace. Therefore, when, where, and how the Lord will bestow grace and compassion are matters beyond our limited intellect and small efforts. He is beyond all proofs, "Aprameyan" (அப்பிரமேயன்), one who cannot be comprehended by proofs. Therefore, we cannot investigate all that. This is one view. Furthermore, the Lord Himself, being Shiva and Shakti, bestows all experiences upon all beings, causing them to enjoy them. He Himself, through His Shakti, bestows the body, senses, and objects of enjoyment suitable for experiencing pleasures, and also assists in the enjoyment of pleasures. Here, the suffering that arises from separation, sulking, and messengers in the context of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, when viewed by those in the worldly life, may appear as suffering. But in reality, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is a Paramacharya (பரமாச்சாரியார்) who is firmly established in the path of uninterrupted Shivayoga (சிவயோகம்). Therefore, we must firmly bear in mind that these worldly matters of sulking, separation, and messengers do not pertain to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the Paramacharya. The fact that Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar became angry about these matters is simply that he viewed them from the perspective of worldly custom. According to the Thevaram verse, "Only if the Lord with the eye on His forehead shows, will one see," if the Lord reveals, one understands; if not, one understands nothing. If Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar understood and became angry about worldly matters in the way the Lord revealed to him, then the Lord, desiring to bestow an instruction that would reveal and make known this truth to Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, causes the colic to come upon Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. The Lord bestows grace. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar attempts to rip open his stomach with a sword. Seeing this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, also deciding to do the same, tries to rip open his stomach with a sword. By the Lord's grace, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar revives and, changing his action towards Nambigal, becomes his friend. Therefore, it is not the case that all beings who are made to experience pleasures experience this. People may ask questions like, "Could not the Lord have made Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar understand the true state of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in another way, without making him suffer?" If we understand that the Lord's acts of grace are beyond human comprehension, such questions will not arise. Even though Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar became angry due to his love for the Lord, according to destiny, the ways in which the Lord's grace manifests in beings are many and varied. We must bear in mind here the Shivayoga state of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and the fact that he was born into this world to rule and take people into service, by the Lord's command, and that he was revealed by the Lord in this state. We must also understand here the truth that the Shivagama (சிவாகமம்) knowledge texts ultimately conclude: even the Lord's acts of punishment (Nigraham - நிகிரகம்) are grace (Anugraham - அனுகிரகம்). Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman also states in the verse, "He bestowed the colic." Therefore, the Lord's not bestowing is also grace. The Lord's punishing is also grace. The Lord's bestowing grace is also grace. Therefore, this sacred history reveals that the Lord's acts of grace cannot be investigated with human intellect. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 60 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 898, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam.

Verse 390:
"The Lord of Eyars, whose deeds are of faultless greatness,
Suffered the colic bestowed by the Primordial One,
Like a fiery spear piercing him, causing pain upon pain.
Overwhelmed by it, he fell and clasped the golden feet of the Lord."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is one whose deeds are of faultless greatness. He possessed the steadfastness of devotion, having a love for the Lord superior to his own life, and followed that path. The Lord bestowed upon him a colic. That colic caused him pain upon pain, as if a spear heated in fire was piercing him. A sharp spear alone can cause great pain. Moreover, this is a sharp spear heated in fire, so it causes intense burning pain, adding to the agony. Even though the colic itself, due to its nature, is sufficient to cause internal pain and suffering in the body, this colic was bestowed by Shiva, the Primordial One. Therefore, it is very swift, and the intensity of the pain is very great for Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. Therefore, suffering more and more from the colic, and deeply distressed, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar clasps the golden feet of Shiva, the Lord, and praises Him.

Verse 391:
"As the devotee praised the Lord's sacred feet with mind and word,
The Lord came to Eyarkon, our protector, who rules us,
And said, 'The colic that afflicts you by Our grace
Will not cease unless Vanthondan cures it.' Hearing this,
The devotee, Eyarkonar, with love, praised Him."

As the devoted Eyarkonar praised the Lord's sacred feet with his mind and words, the Lord came to Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, our protector, who rules us. Because he was broken in spirit by the illness and his body had fallen, Sekkizhar Peruman here mentions only "mind and word." Such a great one praises the sacred feet of the Lord. The Lord's feet are sacred feet, feet that bestow grace, enabling beings to be reformed. As he thus praised, the Lord bestows grace upon Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, our leader, who came to guide the world by showing the path of devotion and detachment. How does He bestow grace? The Lord says, "This colic that has come upon you and afflicts you by Our grace will not cease by any other means unless Vanthondan (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) cures it." Therefore, if the Lord bestows grace by saying that it will not cease unless Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes and cures it, in the negative, it is to emphasize the certainty. Shiva not only bestowed the colic but also spoke these words of grace. However, just like the colic bestowed by the Lord, these words of Shiva also cause great pain to Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar.

Verse 392:
"My Lord, my father, my father's father, and all my lineage,
You are our Lord, who has lived by relying on Your feet from generation to generation.
This life, superior in this world, is being consumed by the colic.
Only that one, who was taken into service by You, can cure it."

Verse 393 also follows as a continuation of this:
"If he cures it, and it does not cease, then it is good that it afflicts me.
Who knows the greatness of what you, who bear the bull banner, do?
You have done what is good for the devoted Vanthondan.
Then the Lord with matted hair disappeared before him."

These two verses are structured thus. Hearing the Lord say that this colic will only be cured if Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar thinks in his sacred heart. He thinks: "If this disease will only be cured if that Vanthondan comes, then it is better for this disease to afflict me without being cured." Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar thinks and says to the Lord: "My father, and his father, and his father, and all my lineage, You are our Lord, our protector, who has lived by relying on Your sacred feet from generation to generation. It is better for this colic to consume me, who has such a life." Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says: "My father, and his father, and his father," indicating a lineage of servitude from generation to generation. The lineage of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is a lineage devoted to Shiva. To be born into such a lineage, which is devoted to Shiva and adheres to the Shaiva path, and to have the ability and qualification to think in this way, is a testament to the power of love that can lead one to Shiva's feet and salvation. The Siddhanta (சித்தாந்த) doctrine states that being born into such a lineage with devotion to Shiva makes the path to liberation even easier. Therefore, "all my lineage are those who live with continuous reliance on You." If Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has to come and cure my disease, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is one who was forcibly taken into service by the Lord. Therefore, is it proper or fitting for one who was newly and forcibly taken into service by the Lord to come and cure my disease, which belongs to one who has lived with continuous reliance on Your feet from generation to generation? It is not. Thinking this, he believes that it is better for his life to depart without this disease being cured than for him to come and cure it.

Why does Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar think so? Because one must not err in the path of devotion. If Sundaramoorthy Swamigal comes and cures this disease, the sin of being complicit in an offense to Shiva will come, due to the desire for this body and the desire for this bodily disease to be cured. Therefore, he thinks in his sacred heart that it is better for his life to depart without him coming and curing this disease. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, saying all this, laments to Shiva, the Lord who bears the bull banner: "Who can know the greatness of the acts of grace You perform? You have done what is good for the newly devoted Vanthondan." Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar laments, "What is the greatness of the Lord's actions? He makes the small great, and He also makes the greatness of steadfast devotion small. Therefore, who can understand the inner meaning of Your great grace? No one can." As Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says this to Shiva with sorrow, the Lord with long matted hair disappears at that very moment. Shiva disappears without giving any reply or bestowing any grace in response to Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's plea. The Lord disappears, having performed the act of concealment (Tirodhana Shakti - திரோதான சக்தி), so that the next parts of the story can unfold.

Verse 394:
"The Lord, the benevolent one, went to Vanthondan and said,
'Today, by Our command, Eyarkon has been afflicted with colic.
Go and cure that colic.' As He bestowed this grace,
Navalurar (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) worshipped Him with a heart full of joy."

The benevolent Lord says to Vanthondan, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal: "Today, by Our divine command, Eyarkon has been afflicted with colic. Go and cure that colic." The Lord bestows grace upon Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The Lord bestows grace, saying, "Go and cure that colic which arose by Our command, by Our command." This reminds us of a type of medical system where diseases can only be cured by addressing the cause from which they arose. The Lord bestows grace, saying, "Go and cure it." Therefore, the Lord bestows grace, saying, "Go to him yourself, uninvited, and cure that colic." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal thought that what was not a mistake was a mistake. And Sundaramoorthy Swamigal desired to befriend Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and appealed to the Lord. Therefore, the Lord, who sent the colic to Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and decreed that it would be cured by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, and who sent Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to Kalikkamar because Sundaramoorthy Swamigal accepted and desired the friendship of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, is a quality of the Lord that we must deeply understand here. Here we see the manifestation of the Lord's compassion, which bestows grace equally on both sides. Hearing the Lord's words of grace, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal rejoices in his heart. This manifests in physical expressions of joy, such as goosebumps, ecstasy, trembling, shedding tears of joy, and swelling of the body. These expressions appear in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal.

Verse 395:
"As the Lord bestowed His grace and departed, Arurar,
By the command of the Lord of the celestials, quickly went.
With the love that filled his mind, he spoke to Kalikkamar."

As the Lord bestowed His grace and departed, Nambi Arurar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, also quickly goes by the divine command of the Lord of the celestials. The anger that Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar held towards him arose from his steadfast love for Shiva. Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal goes very quickly for many reasons: to gain Eyarkonar's friendship, to cure Eyarkonar's colic, and to fulfill the Lord's command. Moreover, he sends a messenger to announce that he is coming to cure the colic that has afflicted Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 61 - Yeyar Kon Kalikkaama Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Sekkizhar Peruman's Periya Puranam, Part 899, Vampara Vari Vandu Sarukkam, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar Puranam. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar hears the messengers say that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is coming to meet him and cure his colic, by the grace of the Lord.

Verses 396 and 397:
"The colic that came by the Lord's grace, and the sorrow he heard from Him,
Afflicted him again. Hearing that Vanthondan was coming,
He said, 'If the great misfortune of the messenger, who sent my Lord,
Coming here to cure my colic, befalls me, what shall I do?'
Before he comes here and cures it, I will destroy this
Colic, this sinful colic that clings to me, along with this belly.
Saying this, he struck with his sword,
And with his life, the colic also ceased."

The colic that came by the Lord's grace afflicted Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. Above that, the words of grace spoken by the Lord also caused him great pain, like words of sorrow. Furthermore, hearing that Vanthondan, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, is coming here, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar laments, "What shall I do if the great misfortune of Vanthondan coming here to cure my colic befalls me?" Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar says, "Before Vanthondan, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, comes here and cures my colic, I will rip open this colic, which is a sinful colic that clings to me, along with this belly." Saying this, he strikes his belly with the sword he carried. With Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's life, the colic also ceased. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's stand, that he would cure and destroy this colic himself before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal came to cure it, proclaims the valor that shines in devotees. We see this in the sacred words, "Is valor something that can be spoken of?" and "What if the sky trembles?" We recall the many heroic deeds of the devotees in our Thiruthondar Puranam. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar intended to destroy the colic. The colic was in the belly. Therefore, since the colic could not be separated and cured without ripping open the belly, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar ripped open his belly. He was born into the Vellala (வளவர்) military commander lineage and also performed that profession. Therefore, he always carried a sword and was skilled in ripping open enemies. Moreover, he was skilled in easily ripping open and curing this enemy, the sinful colic, as if to demonstrate this. As he ripped open his belly with his sword, his life and the colic both ceased. Sekkizhar Peruman's words, "with his life, the colic also ceased," are an auspicious expression. Although the colic came to conquer Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, it was unable to do so, and the colic itself ceased.

Verses 398 and 399:
"Kalikkamar's queen, of inconceivable greatness and virtue,
When she resolved to depart with her unbearable husband,
Some came and said, 'Nambi is approaching here.'
She said, 'Let no one weep,' and then,
Concealing her husband's act, she said to the servants,
'When Nambi comes, adorn the house well and go to meet him.'
Placing auspicious lamps and full pots at the entrance,
And hanging garlands of clustered flowers, she went to receive him."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's sacred wife was a great lady of inconceivable greatness and virtue. Seeing Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's condition, she decided to end her life with her incomparable husband and depart with him. At that very moment, as she was preparing for this, some people came and announced that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was about to arrive there. It was customary for a wife to end her life with her husband upon his death, as a sign of chastity. We saw in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar that when Pugazhanar departed this world, Madhiniyar also went with him. We also saw that when Kalippagaiyar attained the celestial world, Thilagavathiyar also made the same decision. Kalippagaiyar and Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar were of the Vellala lineage, who served as military commanders for the king. Therefore, we see here that it was a widespread custom in this lineage for wives to end their lives with their husbands upon their death, as a mark of chastity. We also see that chaste women did this willingly. As Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's sacred wife thus decided and was attempting to go with her husband, the news arrived that Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was about to arrive. Immediately, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's sacred wife called all the attendants, relatives, and maidservants and told them that no one should weep here. This implies that they were all weeping. If so, was Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's sacred wife not weeping? One weeps out of sorrow only if one thinks that one has been separated from one's husband. She had decided to go with her husband. Therefore, she said this and further commanded. She commanded the servants to conceal Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's act and to adorn the house well to receive Sundaramoorthy Swamigal when he came to their mansion. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's wife ordered them not to reveal the incident outside, not to weep, and not to reveal the incident through any other words or hints. She ordered them to adorn the house to receive a great person. If the lady, having decided to go with her husband, changed her mind upon hearing this news and chose to receive Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, it is indeed a great and extraordinary act. The state of chastity is a state of virtue, but the state of honoring Shiva's devotees is a Shiva-punyam (சிவபுண்ணியம்). Chastity leads to the benefits of the virtuous world, while Shiva-punyam leads to the benefits of Shiva's world, which is liberation. Therefore, the lady, who had decided to end her life with her husband, chose to forgo that and, remaining alive, eagerly honored and welcomed Shiva's devotee. This is clearly a great and extraordinary act. This lady was the daughter of Manakkanjarar Nayanar. If we recall that sacred history here, on her wedding day, she cut off her hair and gave it as desired by the Mahavratiyar (மகா விரதியார்). This lady, who had attained such maturity at that young age, agreed to give her hair there. There, by the Lord's grace, her hair grew back. Here too, the husband who had died is about to revive by the Lord's grace, due to the lady's action, that is, her welcoming and honoring Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and worshipping Shiva's devotee. If so, this world can attain salvation by observing the great virtue of this lady, who received divine grace. Therefore, according to the lady's command, the servants adorn the house with lamps, pots, garlands, and all auspicious items.

Verses 400 and 401:
"The women of pure heart went forth to greet him and praise him.
Our Lord, Nambi, bestowed upon them a smiling face.
Entering with true humility, he sat on a seat of abundant flowers,
His face blooming. He said, 'I am greatly distressed here,
For I have come to cure Eyarkonar's terrible colic and be with him,
Not to sit alone, though all the customary worship was performed perfectly,
And though Nambi Arurar, who follows the truth of the four Vedas, is here.'"

Those with pure hearts, the servants and relatives of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, because their leader had died, adorned the house without showing the slightest outward sign of their sorrow. They acted obediently according to the command of their mistress, without letting anyone know of this news through their words, actions, or facial expressions. They are those who are about to attain the state of purity, the state of Shiva, by the grace of Nambigal and the Lord. Such relatives and servants went forth to greet Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and praised him. They made Sundaramoorthy Swamigal sit on a seat of flowers in the house, made him joyful, and performed worship according to custom. This worship is the method of honoring Shiva's devotees and Shivacharyas (சிவாச்சாரியர்கள்). According to the Agamic (ஆகம) rules, sixteen types of services, including incense, lamps, water for feet, and water for hands, should be offered. They perform them as they should be performed. As they thus praised him, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who accepts all this and composes Tamil hymns that follow the essence of the four Vedas, says: "I have come here to cure Eyarkonar's very terrible colic and to be with him. I am distressed that I am sitting alone like this." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says, "I desire to cure the colic and be joyful with him. Since that has not yet happened, I am distressed about it. I am distressed every moment without seeing Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar."

Verse 402:
"When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal said this, the servants,
By the command of the lady, said, 'There is no fault here.
He is sleeping inside.' Even though they said there was no fault,
My mind is not clear yet. Therefore, I must see him," he said.

When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal said this, the people working in the house, by the command of Eyarkonar's wife, told Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, "Lord, there is no other fault here. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is sleeping inside." But Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, hearing those words, thinks in his sacred heart. "Even though you say there is no harm to him, my mind is not clear. Therefore, I must see him immediately," he says. Similarly, in the sacred history of Appoodhi Nayanar, we recall the state where Thirunavukkarasu Swamigal, who is Aludai Arasu, hearing Appoodhi Adigal say, "He is not here now," said, "My heart cannot bear these words." We see here that our great Paramacharyas (பரமாச்சாரிய பெருமக்கள்), who are immersed in the pervasive grace of Shiva, have the Lord Himself guiding them from within their hearts. Therefore, their intellect clearly understands the true states.

Verses 403 and 404:
"When Vanthondan spoke again, they showed him.
He saw him lying there, his guts spilling out,
His life departed, blood gushing forth.
He said, 'It is good that this happened. I too will join him.'
With a resolute mind, he grasped the sword.
By the grace of the Lord, he too revived.
'I have become a friend, and I am ruined,' he said,
And quickly rose, grasping the sword in his hand.
Vanthondan fell at his feet in worship."

When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asked again to see Eyarkonar, the servants led Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and showed him. There, he saw Eyarkonar lying dead, his guts spilling out, blood gushing forth. Seeing this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says, "It is good that this happened." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says, "I too will join him, just as he went." Saying this, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, without deviating from what he had intended, grasps the sword he had kept to end his life. At that moment, by the grace of the Lord, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar revived. Seeing Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, he says, "I have become a friend, and I am ruined," and quickly rises, grasping the sword in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's hand. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal fell at Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's feet in worship. As soon as Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar revived, the enmity and anger he previously held towards Sundaramoorthy Swamigal vanished, and he became a friend, grasping the sword. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar firmly grasps the sword in his hand, preventing Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's intended action from succeeding. Immediately, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal falls at Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's feet in worship. This is where the Lord's grace was manifested. Therefore, he falls to the ground and worships Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar.

Verse 405:
"As he fell in worship, Eyarkonar, removing the sword,
Also fell at Nambi's feet.
Seeing this miracle that happened that day, the celestials
Showered golden flowers like rain, as the world praised."

Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, seeing Nambigal fall in worship, removed the sword and also fell at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred feet in worship. Seeing this miracle that happened that day, all the devas (தேவர்கள்) showered flowers like Kalpakam (கற்பகம்) like rain, and the people of the world praised. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar fell at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred feet, having come to his senses. He remembered his former thoughts and that he had erred, and with a melting heart, he fell at Sundaramoorthy Swamigal's sacred feet in worship.

Verses 406 and 407:
"Both rose, and with unbroken friendship,
Their immense joy overflowing, they went to Thiruppunkur,
And worshipped the sacred feet of the Lord. Vanthondan,
Recalling the Lord's grace, sang the hymn beginning 'Andhanalan,'
And after some days, the Lord of Thirumunaippadi,
And the chief of the Eyars, who came with him, rejoiced in Thiruvārūr,
And worshipped the Lord who resided in the cool, beautiful temple,
And remained there with abundant love."

Both, having fallen and worshipped with abundant love, rose and embraced each other. With unbroken friendship, their incomparable joy overflowed. With such joy, they went to Thiruppunkur and worshipped the Lord. Thiruppunkur is a place where Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar performed many services. It is a place where he donated 24 veli (வேலி) of land to the temple and worshipped it as his favorite shrine. Both went to such a favorite shrine of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and praised and worshipped the Lord. At that time, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal composed a Thiruppathigam (திருப்பதிகம்). It is the seventh Thiruppathigam, beginning with "Andhanalan" (அந்தநாளன்), set to the melody of Thakkesi (தக்கேசி). In this Thiruppathigam, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal mentions how the Lord bestowed grace upon all who saw it, and also how the Lord bestowed grace upon Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. The first and last verses of this Thiruppathigam are as follows:

Thiruchitrambalam.
"On that day, when I sought refuge in You,
To protect him, You came, and seeing Your strength,
My Lord, You are mine. If the messengers of Yama (நமன்) torment me,
You will declare, 'This is My servant,' and with that thought,
I have sought refuge at Your sacred feet, O Lord of Thiruppunkur, rich in groves."

"He who rode the mighty elephant, He who burned Kama (காமன்) with His eye,
He whose sacred form is like pure gold, He who is in Thiruppunkur, rich in groves,
He who is the Lord of the celestials, the Lord of Uma (உமை).
Those who are skilled in these ten beautiful Tamil verses,
Spoken with love by Vanthondan of Ur (ஊர்) in his heart,
Will not be touched by evil deeds. Thiruchitrambalam."

The inner meaning of this hymn includes praising Eyarkonar's devotion, praising the grace that bestowed friendship upon him by removing his error, and illustrating the reasons for beings to approach the Lord. Thus, after some days, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar accompanies Sundaramoorthy Swamigal to Thiruvarur. There, both go and worship the Lord residing in the Pungkoil (பூங்கோயில்) of Thiruvarur. The fact that Nambigal went to Thiruppunkur, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar's favorite shrine, and Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar came with Nambigal to Thiruvarur, Nambigal's sacred place, and worshipped, was due to the nature of their love and devotion to Shiva. It is the nature of our great people to interact without attachments related to land, country, home, and bodily relatives, considering only their connection to Shiva.

Verse 408:
"Residing there joyfully, Nambi, by his grace,
Returned to his prosperous city, Thirupperumangalam.
Eyarkonar, performing the services appropriate to him,
Attained the feet of the Lord with the red-eyed bull, with distinction."

Residing there joyfully in Thiruvarur, Nambi (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), having received his leave by his grace, returned to his prosperous city, Thirupperumangalam. During that time, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, performing the services appropriate to him, attained the sacred feet of the Lord, who has a red-eyed and great bull (Rishabam - ரிஷபம்), with distinction. Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, who had unwavering love for Shiva, went and attained the Lord's sacred feet.

Verse 409, the concluding verse in the Puranam of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar:
"Having worshipped the lotus feet of Eyarkonar,
The benevolent one, who became a friend to him who sent his Lord as a messenger in the dead of night,
I shall now speak of the greatness of Thirumoolar,
Who speaks the four truths of inner wisdom in sweet Tamil."

This verse is structured thus. Sekkizhar Peruman says, "Having worshipped the lotus-like sacred feet of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, the benevolent one, who became a friend to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who sent his Lord as a messenger in the dead of night, and with the help of those sacred feet, I shall now begin to speak of the greatness of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar." Thirumoola Devan is the one who composed the sacred text, "Thirumandiram" (திருமந்திரம்), in sweet Tamil, which is bestowed by the Shivagamas. The sacred history of such Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, unlike other histories, is not about being born in this world, serving, and attaining the sacred feet. Instead, it is a great history that states that Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, having attained grace as a Shivayogi (சிவயோகி) in Thirukailayam, came to this earthly world and, through the Siddhi (சித்தி) of "Parakaya Pravesam" (பரகாய பிரவேசம்), entered the body of a cowherd named Moolan (மூலன்), and remained in Shivayoga for a long time under a Bodhi tree (அரசமரம்) in Thiruavaduthurai (திருவாவடுதுறை), and composed Thirumandiram. That is the upcoming Puranam. Thus, the sacred history of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar is concluded. Sekkizhar Peruman says, "I worship the sacred feet of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and, with their help, begin to speak of the sacred history of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar." We too worship the sacred feet of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and conclude this reflection on his sacred history. We will now begin to reflect on the Puranam of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. Praise to the sacred feet of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. Praise to the sacred feet of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Praise to the sacred feet of our Lord Nambiandar Nambigal. Praise to the sacred feet of Sekkizhar Peruman. Praise to the sacred feet of the commentator, Sivakavimani Sambandha Saranalaya Swamigal. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.
